# Changelog

One entry per release, newest first: what changed for the player, and the numbers that moved. Write it under Unreleased as the work happens; `tools/release.sh` stamps it with the version and the date. An entry holds no `## ` heading of its own (the entry would end there): bold lead-ins and nested bullets instead.

## Unreleased

## v0.0.22 (2026-11-18)

- **Your number two fights a man shooting at the stack, whoever's sector he stands in** (the hunt of v0.0.20, T1). A role-player of your corner firing on either of you, whom your #2 sees and you are not on (no shot row of yours on him, your muzzle not on him), is his: taken in before any man of his own, at the expectation his eyes give it, engaged by his own row, and kept when your rifle comes back, as is a man he took in under "Covering!" who is firing by then (`combat/two.js` `fightsUs`, `FIGHTS_US_MS` 1,500; `coverTick` keeps him), and a man he knew already is taken in again when he fires. The corporal on 15 frozen in room 2's doorway: on v0.0.20 your #2 was on nobody for 8 ticks of that man's fire in his sight and you were the casualty; now he puts him down and you are not hit. Over the live lane's 80 lanes of seeds 1 to 8 (the fuzz and honest bots, every sheet, `live:fight,fight`): your casualties 44 → 27, the ticks a man fired in your #2's sight with him on nobody 836 → 416 (the rest: your own muzzle on that man, or his rifle down); the honest bot's 32 lanes the same to the end. The corporal who stows in room 2's doorway and brings his rifle up as your #2 takes room 2's man in: given back with his rounds 0.05 s off and you the casualty on 5 seeds of 8 in the first build, now kept and put down, no casualty on 8 of 8 (the review of the fixes, R2).
- **No gun in your hands inside a room is your rifle down: your #2 says "Covering!" and takes your sector** (T1). Your rifle on its sling (Stow, Sling it), no pistol out and no grenade, charge or tool in the hand, past the threshold of a room you entered (`youDown`); before, he left your man to you. The corporal on 15 who stows in room 2's doorway: "Covering!" 0.3 s later and room 2's man his (v0.0.20: nothing said, and your casualty). A rifle a fumble swung onto its sling is not one you put away: that swing is the drill's own 2 s or so to your Rifle up, and he says nothing over it (`fumbleSwung`, read from the log; the boot recruit's fumbled Off safe on 1000 had "Covering!" said at 8.80 s in the first build, and house-pace's recruit lane from GO read +2.17% against its 2%, then +1.54%, both on v0.0.20's tag; the merged tree reads +2.14%, below).
- **His "Clear!" waits on every man of the room the team has seen** (T1). On the live lane his call for a room holds while a role-player of that room you or he has seen is up and has not given up, gone down or been ruled out, and is never said once the room's leg is cleared or passed (`bundle.js` `twoClearWait`). His "Clear!" with a seen man up: 31 → 0 over the same 80 lanes (the tier one's fuzz on 2 called room 2 clear 38.30 s after GO with its man firing).
- **A casualty, yours or his, takes the stack out of the fight: he starts nothing and says nothing after "I'm hit!"** (T2). From the casualty's tick his slot and his eyes sit out the beat to "Cease fire!", and no call is said on a lane with a casualty (`shoothouse.js` `houseActors`; `bundle.js`'s call beat says nothing and carries on, the casualty's beat halting the drill: the first build halted it there, a choice no act could leave, the clock stopped for good). Before, a fresh Dominate started 150 ms after his strike and said "Clear!" 600 ms after his "I'm hit!"; after your casualty he said "Clear!" in 26 of the 80 lanes, now none.
- **Paint on your number two is said as his** (T3): "Paint on your two, chest. Keep working." or "Your number two took paint, chest. Keep working." (`HOUSE_CHIEF.paintTwo`; his panel's stop, "Paint on your two, chest. His panel took it. Keep working.", `paintStoppedTwo`). v0.0.20 picked from your lines whoever was struck: "You took paint, chest." and "Your panel took it." for his.
- **The hot wash's one thing on his casualty is his** (T4): "The man in your sector shot your number two. Your corner is yours to kill, before he picks one of you." (`casualty:two:yours`, the man yours) or "Your number two went down in his own corner. Through the door together, and call what you see for him." (`casualty:two`); paint on him alone short of a casualty, "Your two took paint. …" (`paint:two`). v0.0.20 said "You took paint and it stopped there." beside the card's "PAINT ON YOU: NONE". The weights are unchanged: only the words follow who was hit.
- **The instructor never calls your paint after you are hit or the lane is over.** A strike's "Paint, chest. Keep working." owed through a take-in was said after the fatal strike's "I'm hit!" (the hunt of v0.0.20: 50 ms after it, the corporal's keys lane on seed 1); it is now owed as the paint's own line and dropped once the casualty has opened or the lane ended, whether said at once or owed (`shoothouse.js paintSays`, `sayOwed`). Paint on your number two is said through the same call, in his words.
- **A role-player your practice frag put down counts DOWN.** The instructor's read rules him out, and the LIVE row and the card now count him as the FRAG row names him: "LIVE: 1 DOWN" beside "FRAG IN ROOM 2: 2A DOWN" (was "LIVE: 0 DOWN", the card "2 ROLE-PLAYERS · 0 DOWN": the hunt of v0.0.20, the sergeant's frag lane on seed 13); a man the read wounds is "1 WOUNDED", never "HIT AND FIGHTING ON" (`T.live.fragOut`, `house-kit.js readsTick`).
- **The card counts your paint where it went.** A round the would-have drew onto the arm in front of your carrier, said "You took paint, left arm.", is a LIMB on the card: "PAINT ON YOU: 1 TORSO, 1 LIMB · WOULD HAVE BEEN: WOUNDED, CHEST AND LEFT ARM" (was "2 TORSO": the hunt of v0.0.20, the sergeant by T on 48213).
- **The card names the room a role-player fired first in.** On the live lane a room whose man's first round came before yours is on FIRED FIRST ON YOU, your par met or not, and the session's grid marks it FIRED FIRST (was left off: the hunt of v0.0.20, the sergeant's keys lane on seed 1, killed in room 2 by a man 0.11 s first).
- **Rounds on the one tick read "FIRST ROUNDS TOGETHER".** His first round and yours in the same 50 ms tick were "YOURS 0.00 S BEFORE HIS" (the hunt of v0.0.20: the sergeant's room 3 by T on 48213).
- **A wound that bleeds you into shock puts you down in the lane.** A wound rated not fatal was judged once, at the strike; the would-have's blood is now judged each tick of the lane, and in shock (70%) or under it the man is down from that tick: your casualty opens with "That wound has bled you into shock. You're down.", then the cease fire, and the card says "FATAL: SHOCK FROM THE BLOOD LOST 198.7 S AFTER THE 1ST STRIKE ON YOU" (was the lane run on and "WOULD HAVE BEEN: WOUNDED, CHEST": the hunt of v0.0.20, the corporal's fuzz lane on seed 31, a chest wound at 239.4 s, shock at 438 s, the lane to 480 s); a role-player sits down at that tick; the pain's floor stays judged at the strike, the adrenaline held while the fight goes on (`would.js wouldTick`, `LATE_WHY`; the one thing "You took a wound and worked on until the blood ran out. …", `casualty:bled`, yours only).
- **A save made at a stop-motion pause carries on byte for byte.** The tick that resumes from a pause took your body's tween start from before the pause, so the original drew a walk the loaded game did not and the two split one tick on (the hunt of v0.0.20); the resumed tick now takes it where you stand (`game.js`, the player layer). Nine house-pin saves move in the ninth digit of your body's energy and water (the resumed tick no longer counts a walk); no tick and no run sheet.
- **T walks the far leg of room 3 instead of looking at it from the near leg.** After "In" in room 3 the line named "Look: the far leg", a look that never covers it; T pressed it 452 times and the room was passed on time at 93.5 s. `cqb-next` now names the far leg's walk (`hotRow`, as `cqb-corner` does): the sergeant on 48213 clears room 3 in 7.7 s (the hunt of v0.0.20, breach-1).
- **Step off a stack you had taken and T puts you back on it.** 1.09 m off room 2's stack, T alternated X and G 1,739 times each and the door never opened; `cqb-stack` now speaks while the leg is 'stacked' and you stand off your point (no charge on the leaf, no task of your #2's): room 2 clear at 8.3 s (breach-2).
- **"In. On him." always does something.** Back on the stack after the take-in in the doorway (new in v0.0.20), the line had no act at 24 stops and room 1 ran out at 90 s; it now takes Carry on, else the room's In: room 1 clear at 9.8 s, as in v0.0.19 (breach-3).
- **The closet's lock is shot from beside it, and a closet nobody looked into is not clear.** "Shoot the lock" fired from 5.53 m across room 2 as if the muzzle were on the lock, and room 2 was called clear over the open closet. Shoot the lock now walks you to the closet's latch side first (0.5 m off the leaf, past the latch jamb clear of the doorway: 0.35 m at room 2's closet), the round resolves at the muzzle's real distance (`MUZZLE_REACH_M` 0.6 m: from 3 m off the lock, 2.4 m, where v0.0.20 resolved every round at 0.05 m), and an inner door opened with nobody of the room at it is 'open', a door fault: "You opened that door and never looked in." The tier one now shoots from 0.94 m of the leaf's middle (breach-4).
- **With one round left on safe, the DI says Off safe.** From rung 2, every shot row struck "one left" and Fire "on safe", the line was silent for 37 stops and room 2 ran out at 104.5 s; now Off safe, Aim, Press, then RELOAD: room 2 clear at 18.4 s (breach-5).
- **T no longer goes round Transition and Rifle up.** On a rifle with no round anywhere T switched 22 and 21 times in 60 s; Rifle up is never named onto a rifle the planner finds no round for, so the P-12 stays the gun that fires: Transition once, then room 3 clear in 2.6 s (breach-6).
- **RELOAD off the floor takes the full magazine.** The ground reach took the nearest, the P-12 magazine just dropped and felt empty, four times running while a full one lay 0.15 m further; it now takes the best believed (rounds, unknown, then empty), as the planner plans it (breach-7).
- **The tower's command carried on locks the slide back before its look.** The queued UNLOAD, SHOW CLEAR on the P-12 halted at "The slide is closed: lock it back to look in." on every press; it now starts with Lock the slide back (breach-8).
- **"He's down." only when someone is.** After a charge's misfire the DI said "He's down. Carry on." with nobody down; now "Carry on." (breach-9).
- **A spent charge reads "charge spent", and a tool left behind is named.** Room 3's Breach read "no charge issued" with the issue badge after your one SC-1 went on room 1; it now reads "charge spent" with a note. Pry it vanished at room 3 once the halligan was let go at room 2; it is now struck "halligan left at room 2", and walked back to it, "Pick up the halligan" makes it live again (breach-10, breach-11).
- **The house's end card never scrolls beside the plan view, a casualty's card included.** Beside the plan the card has its own width, 400 px × --ui (at most 40% of the window), and the side column takes the rest, the run sheet wrapping under the plan; before, the run sheet's long lines took the width and left the card 202 to 627 px, so the live casualty's card scrolled 167 px at 1280 × 720, 212 at 1920 × 1080 and 215 at 1600 × 900 for your number two's, "Back to the title" below the fold. Now 0 at 1024 × 768 to 1920 × 1080 on every lane measured.
- **Every line a card numbers is one a key reaches, and the title's is 0.** The digits reach 1 to 9 and 0, so a card numbers ten lines at most, the tenth labelled 0; on the house's NO-GO card with "Again from room 2", "Export the run" gives up its number (still there, clicked) and "Back to the title" is 0, where it read "11" and typing 1, 1 started the lane again; the Esc menu's "10 Sound" reads 0. "Back to the title" now closes the card: it stayed over the title page.
- **A lane set up ("Again from room 2") is scored as the drill it is, never as the lane.** It is kept but never a sheet's best on the picker (the hall alone, 5.5 s, had become the sergeant's "best 5.5 s" over his full lane's 46.9 s); its card counts the rooms it ran and their men, "2 ROOMS · PAR 56.0 S · 4 OF 4 THREATS" from room 2 where it read "3 ROOMS · PAR 69.0 S · 6 OF 6 THREATS", "THE HALL · PAR 15.0 S" for the hall alone; a room the setup cleared reads "SET UP" and nothing more, with no time, no dummy and no clean room counted; and with nothing to fault the instructor says "That was clean. Now the whole lane, from the top.", never "Clean lane. Again, faster." beside its NO-GO.
- **A stand clicked on the plan view opens its truth card beside the plan, named by its stand.** It read "String 1" with a button 1, Pairs' words, under the plan, and pushed the run sheet out of the side column with no way to scroll to it (129 px hidden at 1280 × 720); it is now "Room 1, 1a" beside the canvas, and the side column scrolls where a narrow window puts it under.
- **The lane timer's paper and the 25 m card's inset are drawn at the size they are shown, so every digit reads.** A 1 px border inside their 200 × --ui box left the canvas 2 px narrower than its pixels at every window size, so two columns were dropped ("9" read as 3, "8" broke, pars and times misread), and at 1024 × 768 (--ui 0.85) a paper drawn at 200 px was shown in 168, 32 columns dropped; the edge is now an outline outside the box and the paper is drawn at --ui × device pixels, shown pixel for pixel (250 of 250 at 1600 × 900, 170 of 170 at 1024 × 768).
- **"Stack and clear" has eight rooms to be drawn from, five of them new: a long room, a narrow room, an offset room, a room with a back room behind it, and a small room** (the house lens; your "game world is handcrafted" of 2026-11-05 holds: the rooms are authored data). X4's other five rooms (PLACES §4.12) are chunks (`src/data/chunks/x4.js` W, D, O, T, Q: 7 × 4, 3.6 × 6.4, 6.5 × 4, 5 × 6.4 with a 1.2 m inner doorway, 3.2 × 3.2 m), each with its stands, four places and its role-player's post. A house drawn from all eight (`&rooms=all`, `createGame({ rooms: 'all' })`) draws three of them lazily (10,000 seeds, never past 5 tries, 0 fallbacks). The back room is cleared by #1's far walk through its doorway, a no-stop funnel. **The picker still draws from the three rooms of layout A and offers no Rooms row** (the scene's `rooms: ['a']`, your question 4's default) until 100 eight-room lanes meet every gauge and you say yes: the eight are URL and tests only.
- **Layouts B and C, the instructors' other two panel settings, are named houses.** `&layout=B` plays the long room, the offset room and the room with its back room (`Wn280pc.Os195lc.Tn165pc`), `&layout=C` the small room, the narrow room and the L (`Qn290pc.Ds205lc.En175pc`), their targets drawn from the seed; a named layout beats a code given with it and never qualifies. The picker's House row lists A and B ("House: A · B · Drawn from the seed", H cycles it); **C is URL only** while its eight funnel faults are open (the keys private in the L's room 3, F4).
- **Every room plan of the house is checked by a derivation from its walls alone.** `combat/derive.js` `derivePlan` gives every field of a plan from its door, its rooms and the area's grids; layout A's three authored plans come out within `DERIVE_TOL` (stack 0.02 m, points 0.25, hot spots 0.30, dead space 0.30 and 0.50, far end 0.80, paths 0.15, sectors 30°; measured 0.000, 0.16, 0.16, 0.16 and 0.40, 0.22, 0.12, 15.8°), and the five new rooms' plans are the deriver's, proposed by `tools/derive-plans.mjs`, committed (`derived@1`) and held fresh by `chunks-fresh`. `&plans=derived` plays a lane on the deriver's plans for layout A too: F1 to F26 met over 80 lanes, the tier one's 43.1 s as on the authored. The review's find: the small room's two points sat inside its own door's funnel (8 of 8 Q cases); a point or far-walk end is now pushed out to 0.6 m, F25 late or out on layout C 19 to 0, F4's game stops 2.50 s in 50 lanes to 0.
- **A role-player's post is never where a target stands.** On every drawn house the cast is drawn fair with each live post's stand read as a threat, the room's other target off the post (the two swap where it was drawn onto it): 518 of 1,000 drawn casts move, their layouts never; the set never draws.
- **What a no-stop zone is, said.** Each zone names its why (`zones.js` `ZONE_WHYS`: door, doorway, leaf, hatch, stairhead, crossing); a leafless gap a layout declares a funnel (1.5 m or narrower, `FUNNEL_GAP_MAX_M`) stops nobody, as a door does.
- **"Again as Tier one" shows where he was: the plan view draws your last run on the same house as a ghost** (D). The end card keeps each run's trace in the page (the last six, gone at a reload; `src/ui/cards/house-ghosts.js`), keyed on the scene, the seed, the house's code with its cast, the setup, the kit, the role-players and the plans, so a ghost is only offered on the lane it was run on. Its two discs ride under you at the same lane time from GO, faint, with a dotted row of its legs under the scrubber and "−5.8 s" in the clock (minus is ahead of the ghost; the sheet said "+5.8 s", your call): on 48213 the private reaches room 2's door 5.8 s behind the tier one, room 3's 13.2 s and the hall 29.8 s. A ghost packs to 6.4 to 14.4 KB (16 at most); the plan's slowest draw in a 4× replay 0.20 ms. Before, the plan view had no second run.
- **The device in your hands is drawn on the stage.** With a G-11 armed, the halligan, the ram or a charge in your hands, or your number two's, the figure held empty fists and the grenade appeared from nowhere on its flight's first frame (kit-10 of the v0.0.18 hunt). Now the device is in the hand in its own colour (the practice G-9P a blue egg), held at the chest, pinned, thrown underhand, the halligan across the body and at the pry, the ram at the belly and swung, the strip upright and placed: the defaults G1, T1, B1, R1 and C1 of `samples/held-devices` until you score it (a one-table change, `templates.js` `HELD_POSES`). A thrown grenade is in the hand until the tick before its flight, then in the air: never both, never neither. The cards are baked behind the brief's splash (about 0.1 s for the full kit); a figure with no device is drawn to the byte as before.
- **The picker names the house and the lane says its letter.** "House: A · B · Drawn from the seed" (H), and "Rooms: The three · All eight" under a drawn house once the scene offers it (R); the run sheet, the end card and the score line say "LANE B" for a named house, a drawn house its code as before; "A" is titled "the house as set".
- **The walls between you and your own grenade count.** A G-11's or a charge's blast on a body with a wall or a closed leaf between is half the free field's peak (`blast.js` `CORNER_SHADOW_K` 0.5), as the instructor's read of a practice grenade already had it: one model, `practice.js` `blastLine`, read by `burst.js` for every person and stand. The boot recruit's own short flash at room 1 (seed 1489397426) puts 14.9 kPa on him round the jamb, not 29.8 and a 2.4 s stun; over twenty of his flash lanes his own G-11s stun him on none (8 bursts at 20 kPa or more and 6 "STACK IN THE BLAST" before). The instructor on the catwalk over the 2.8 m walls takes the free field's.
- **A grenade at your feet or short on you hands back: "Get clear."** Every grenade slipped at your feet stops time with its fuse running ("Grenade! At your feet. Get clear."; v0.0.19 did it for practice grenades only), and so does a failed toss that lands where its burst would stun you ("Grenade short! Get clear."). Get clear takes the way that has you farthest from it when it goes, not the longest run: the boot recruit's G-11 slipped on 48229 puts 15.2 kPa on him, not 321.3; his short toss on 48224 5.2 kPa, not 45.0. Declined, a slip is still the "BANG AT YOUR FEET" fault.
- **Your #2 waits for the bang, and gets clear of one.** He never starts his entry, nor closes up on your heels, into a room with a live grenade in it or by his way in, and a walk begun before it landed stops short of the doorway; the bang lets him go (the tier one's #2 in at 8.55 s against the burst at 8.50, not 6.40). A live grenade on the floor where it would stun him: he runs clear once he has seen it (your slip on 48229: 6.6 kPa on him, not 38.7). Silent: the bang is everyone's cue.
- **The frag is named at the stack, and the DI's shorthand reads for a new player.** "Clear it, frag. The brief: not hostiles only. 13.7 s. Your corner: left." where the brief did not call the room hostiles only (T still presses your row: question 11's default). "You lead. Read the door, then tell your two." (was "Point. Read it, tell your two."), "To your point. Then your sector." (was "Point. Sector."), "His man: the near leg's far corner. Yours: your corner."; the habit's toast "Muzzle kept off SSgt Martinez (low ready): habit." (was "Muzzle past …: automatic.", read as a safety breach).
- **The instructor looks into your chamber before he calls it clear, and your #2's SHOW CLEAR passes behind the splash** (the feel lens). At Stop he walks the catwalk to you, at SHOW CLEAR he calls a gun clear only with his eye within `SHOW_CLEAR_M` 2.5 m of that marine's chest for `SHOW_LOOK_MS` 600 ms, and your #2 walks out under the catwalk where it cannot reach him (deep in room 2 on 37 of 96 live lanes). Before, he said "Clear." from where his walk had got to: 6.45 m on every turner lane, 10.8 m (median) on the live lane; now at most 2.40 m on 148 lanes. Once your guns pass, his clear, the look and the wait for the tower pass as one skipped frame (`house-showclear`, as the hot wash), and "The lane is clear." comes `WASH_AFTER_MS` 800 ms after the last "Clear.": your "Clear." to the hot wash was 5.3 to 16.1 s at 1×, now under 2 s of wall clock in the page.
- **Paint is called when a man's strikes have landed, once.** The instructor holds a strike `PAINT_CALL_MS` 600 ms and calls the pair as one ("Paint, chest, two. Keep working.", "Paint, chest and left arm."), and says nothing where the next round made it a casualty: "Keep working." in the 2.5 s before a cease fire on 46 of 52 casualty lanes before, 0 now.
- **The lane's words end with the lane.** "Weapons clear. Into the booth: hot wash." where the lane was not cleared ("The lane is clear." on 52 of 52 casualty lanes before), no praise for the dummy after the cease fire (6 lanes before), your #2's "Man down!" when you are hit (none before) and none of his "Clear!" or "Up." over you (26 lanes before). **The casualty's lesson names what killed you**: a click inside 10 m with the P-12 holstered, "A click with him that close: the pistol, not the tap-rack."; a hit he fought through; a man on the door before your take-in (over 96 live lanes, 52 casualties: 39 slow, 7 fought through, 4 clicks, 2 unseen). **The umpire rules a man out to him**: "You, out. Sit down where you are." (was "He's out."; question 3's default).
- **"Stack and clear, live" can be played on a drawn house** (tests and `&rooms=all`, `&house=`): the role-players stand at the chunks' posts of rooms 2 and 3 wherever those rooms fall, the forced cards by lane room, the same two men; **the picker keeps it "as set"** until F80's balance band is met (the tier one painted on 3 of 7 drawn lanes). Played from the page, another house on the live lane toasts "This lane is played as set: no other house yet."
- **"Stack and clear" takes three more options from the URL, in one order of precedence.** `&rooms=a|all`, `&layout=A|B|C` and `&plans=derived` are read by `readParams` and ordered by `core/debug.js` `houseChoice`: a named layout beats a code, the rooms and the draw; a code beats the rooms; `&rooms=all` alone means drawn, and with `&draw=set` it is dropped. A parameter that loses is dropped with a toast ("One house a lane: layout B."). A lane that gives none of them holds to the byte. **"Stack and clear, live" is pinned like the turner lane** (`house-live-pin`: four lanes from the fill to the card by tick, run sheet and save), and the role-players' places are read through one seam (`opfor.js` `spotOf`, `rpStandsOf`, held by a source scan). The #2's shim `scenarios/house-two.js` is gone: his code is `combat/two.js`.
- **The picker's House B plays lane B** (the play review of 2026-11-17). Every choice of the picker reaches the lane (`boot.js` `pickedScene`); the first build passed only the par, the loadout, the draw and the qualification, so B lit played lane A. **A named house says its letter everywhere**: "Lane B at 1 G" in the goal, "LANE B" on the card, "This is lane B: …" and "Lane B. Standby." from the tower (`courses.js` `seqHouseNamed`); before, "NEW LAYOUT", "A new layout at 1 G" and "a lane you have not seen" (false on Again); a drawn house's brief now says "a layout the instructors have set".
- **Your number two calls "Clear!" once a room, and before your "Room clear!".** Your "Room clear!" waits, at most `CLEAR_WAIT_MS` 5 s, while his Dominate of the room still owes its call, and a second call of his in a room is never said. The play review's 690 lanes: a second "Clear!" in 12, out of order in 22 of 709 rooms, before; none after.
- **Your #2 goes in on your cue even while he is still stacking up.** Your cue ends his stack-up at his stack point and he goes in after his reaction (the lead's call: the entry waits for his "Ready."). F25 holds on the kit's mixed loadout (keys recruit on 1222, re-stacked after his own Get clear of a slipped G-11): 3.30 s from the cue to his threshold before, 2.25 s after, against 3 s.
- **The casualty's lesson follows the instructor's own rule, and FIRED FIRST names the right room.** "That close, a click means the pistol" only from rung 2, where his stoppage line says "Transition: pistol"; below it "Tap, rack, back on." FIRED FIRST ON YOU names the room whose role-player fired and was never answered (the private by T on 48213, live: "ROOM 1, ROOM 2", room 2 had been left out; on 30 of 50 casualty lanes). **A grenade's cease fire leads the verdict**, said once: "CEASE FIRE: IN THE ROOM AT THE BURST", no longer followed by "NO-GO: A THREAT LEFT STANDING".
- **"Again" keeps the eight rooms and the plans' source; one hot wash after a casualty; the live lane's timed lines are baked up front.** "Again with a new seed" after an eight-room house draws from the eight again (it fell back to the three); `&plans=derived` replays on its own plans, ghost included. The casualty's wash is "Weapons clear. Into the booth: hot wash." (v0.0.21's "The lane is closed." was a second rule never said once both were in the tree, and is gone). The hunt's paint, bled and casualty lines are fixed lines baked at the splash (99, 76 before), so none is heard late; at the first key the bake queues lines behind the sounds in turn, so the range's walk is heard again.
- **The words, and the picker at 1280 × 720.** "Good frag: both men down." (was "Good frag. 1a and 1b down.", the stands' ids); the contact's next row its own sentence ("His man: the armoured target. Move to your point.", was "Yours: Move to your point."); each best on its sheet's own line, so Back sits unscrolled (it was 95 px below the fold with a session's bests).
- **Your #2's admin goes on through SHOW CLEAR's skip** (the integration). His P-12's clear, still running when your guns passed, stood still for the skip's length and a choose opened on its last tick (the keys recruit on 4 live: 133 ms real with one pause, F82; recruit 1148's CLEAR 3.01 s past his macro, F18): `houseActors` runs his admin under a skip once the lane has ended. His pistol clear held from 40.36 s to 44.10 s before, done in time after.

The still-open gates of v0.0.21 stand with their F numbers (`house-drawn` ×2, `house-drawn8` ×2, `house-swings`, `house-ladder` F70, `moment-house` F71, `render-bench` ×2 F73, `house-pace` +2.14% against 2% by chance, the bake deadline of `audio-browser`); no gauge was loosened.

Round suites (v0.0.22's roles, the integration and the fixer; each prints its numbers): stage 0's `house-options` (3), `house-live-pin` (4 cells; two re-recorded in the fixer's step), `syntax` (the #2's one path, the live lane's seam, the ghosts kept out of the sim), `game-upgrade` (two v0.0.21 saves, heavy); A's `plans-derived` (F74), `chunks-fresh`, `zones-kinds`, `house-draw8`, `house-layouts`, `house-cases` (the five rooms' 20 cases and every room's post, F75), `house-shapes` (SHAPES and HOUSE_DRAWS; `X4_CHUNK_PLANS8` `188b8a00e7361e`, set on purpose), `house-fair`, heavy `house-drawn8`, `house-flawless-derived`, `ui-house-drawn`, `tests/flawless-rooms.mjs` (F74 to F77); B's `blast-line` (F78), `slip-g11`, `two-live` (F79), `cqb-frag-words`, `house-words`, `house-shorthand`, `house-twin-words`, `tests/flawless-blast.mjs`; C's `house-showclear`, `ui-showclear` (heavy), `house-live-words`, `house-echo`, `house-end-fixes` (4), `house-cause`, `opfor-spots-drawn`, `house-live-drawn`, `tests/flawless-end.mjs` (F80 to F82); D's `house-ghost`, `house-picker`, `art-held`, `look-held`, `ui-held-device` (heavy), `ui-houseplan`'s ghost case (F83), `tests/flawless-held.mjs` (F84); the integration's `integ-v022` and `house-lane-word`, and `house-done`'s rows to F84 (251 rows: 229 wired, 8 open, 12 user, 2 later, 0 gap); the fixer's `fixer-v022` (13, four heavy) and heavy `ui-fixer-v022`, each failing without its fix (14 of 14 red on the tree with fix.patch's `src/` hunks reversed). ## v0.0.21 (2026-11-16)

- **"Stack and clear" can be drawn from the seed: a house you have not seen.** The shoot house's three authored rooms (the centre, the corner with its closet, the L) are placed by the seed in a drawn order, each north or south of the hall, its door anywhere on the 0.5 m grid from 24 to 31 m for room 1 and 3 to 9 m apart after, inside house 1's fixed walls, hall, apron, catwalk and booth: 36 arrangements, 8,005 placements, 100 different houses in seeds 1 to 100 (the set: one). The hall's last leg is derived from the last door. The code names the house (`Kn240lc.Es195pc.Cn160lc|ftnnant|p303101` on 48213): `&draw=set|drawn`, `&house=<code>` (a layout alone has its cast drawn from the seed; a whole code with an unfair cast is played on its layout with the seed's fair cast, and the page says so), the picker's "House" row (H) and the Tests menu. It reads the user's rule of 2026-11-05, "game world is handcrafted", as an exercise's arrangement of authored panels per lane, never per career (DESIGN.md's log, question 3). **The default stays "as set"** (`HOUSE_DRAW_DEFAULT` 'set'): the sheet's gate, `house-drawn` green on 100 seeds, is not met (below), and the user has not answered question 3.
- **Doors open one way, the latch toward you, and the entry changes with it** (your ask, 2026-11-15: "doors should have a direction they open (pull or push) and it should affect our options for entry"). On a drawn house each lane door is push or pull (50/50), hinged at its downrange end so the stack forms on the latch side facing you as you come (a hinge drawn freely stalled every lane 361 s in the design's probe); Kick is struck at a pull ("opens toward you"); a centre room pushed is a cross with #1 behind the door, pulled a hook, and a corner or L drawn as a pull turns the sectors over (1a your number two's, 1b yours). The brief names them: "The doors: room 1, pull door, hinge left; room 2, push door, hinge right; …". `&doors=fixed` keeps the set's swings.
- **Doors are found closed, open or locked.** Closed 60%, open 25%, locked 15% (push doors only: a locked pull would be a dead end on the standard loadout), never by the loadout or the sheet, so "Again as …" meets the same house. A locked door takes the closet's kick (DC 14; the halligan's −4 where carried). A door found open is open at the start, and its man turns no sooner than GO and his gap (before, he would have turned during the brief). **The lock is found at the door, not from the hall:** Open is struck "locked" and T offers the kick only once the door has been read ("Push door, hinge right. Locked.") or tried (the hand on the handle: "Locked. Kick it."); before that the panel and T offer the push, and the kick's odds are an unlocked door's. The instructor's par books the try (`DOOR_HAND_S`) and the kick. The set's lane doors and the closet are as before. (Your report of a lock known 8 m off, through the play review.)
- **Each room brings its own men, and every drawn lane is fair.** The stands' roles travel with their rooms (the corner's armoured man, the L's hostage, the centre's pair), each at one of four places in its room; the hostage may be in the corner or the L wherever they stand, or nowhere (613 lanes of 1,000), at most one a lane. A cast is drawn again (up to 8 times, then the rooms' own cast as set) until every room holds a threat, at most one hostage and one armoured man, at least one no-shoot, at most one man in your number two's sector a room, every man 0.57 m off every path, point and swing, 1 m from the other, seen from his owner's point, and a hostage 2.5 to 5 m from #1's point in his sector: 1,000 seeds, 0 unfair, 31 on the set cast (F61, F62).
- **A drawn house's id names its layout one to one, so a second house in one session is never played on the first's rooms.** The area id was a 7-digit hash: 27 pairs shared one over seeds 1 to 150,000, and the second of a pair was played on the first's walls, its stands outside its rooms. It is now the layout numbered room by room, 13 hex digits (`melas.wheel.s4~0075b8995b83f`); the layouts held (the last two) are kept by last use, not by the order built. A drawn house loads from a save (`places.live.gen`, rebuilt on load). (The code review.)
- **Every lane of the four bots reaches its card on a hundred houses drawn from the seed, as quick as on the set, and your man is the one in your sector.** The tier one's drawn lane 44.4 s against 43.3 s on the same seeds, the keys private's 73.2 s against 72.9 s, room 1 5.3 to 15.4 s where the set's is always the same; the four dead ends the drawn layouts found (a door found open, two of your men up in one room, a charge's safe point in an earlier door's doorway, a pause on your number two's man with nothing to press) are closed. **Not yet met on drawn houses, and why the default stays "as set":** the sweep's F2, F4, F6, F10, F12 and F65 and `house-swings`' F4 (a corner room last, north of the hall: 0.65 s in 6 of 40 lanes, 0.10 to 0.15 s in its doorway); listed for v0.0.22.
- **Pars on drawn houses allow for the turn and for the second man.** A man standing off the room's sector adds the muzzle's turn to his par (at the turn rate of your Weapon handling: "Par 0.80 + 0.10 (the turn off your sector)"), and a man seen while another of your corner is engaged has his par run from your first man's row. The raider by the honest bot now goes on 18 of 24 drawn houses (13 before), the corporal on 20 (13); the set's pars are unchanged. The rest of the NO-GOs (5 and 4) are not traced: v0.0.22.
- **Your number two no longer floods the router when he cannot step off your path, and no lane ends differently for it.** Each of his up to 18 tries a tick toward a point inside or through a wall made the nav's A* search his whole region of the house, about 12,700 cells, twice; the routing grid's regions are labelled once and such a leg is known to have no way at once, and a tick's synchronous routes are held to `ROUTE_NODES_TICK` 8,000 nodes for the caller that stormed. On 72 fuzz lanes the most nodes in a tick 88,808 → 4,104 (the worst lane 197,919 → 33,134), the busiest tick 10.9 → 2.5 ms; every lane's end the same to the byte (F68).
- **At the tower's Stop, MAKE SAFE puts your P-12 back in its holster, brings the R-7 up and makes it safe, in one press, and Fix names it.** Before, Fix read "already so" over the pistol in your hand and MAKE SAFE left it there, the tower waiting to its move-on; now 4.0 s on the sergeant's lane that ended on his pistol, with no fault. Your number two's Stop is his own, as built.
- **A P-12 left in your hand unchambered at MAKE READY is racked by the instructor at the move-on, as a holstered one is; GO is never given with it cold.** Its own MAKE READY halted after the draw (a fumbled part) took the corporal to GO with it drawn and empty.
- **A slow holster now holsters: "P-12 holstered." is where your pistol is.** A holster roll that went badly without a drop said the words and left the P-12 in your hand (12 of 40 fumbled tries), and a P-12 row's rest run as Carry on did the same; only a drop ("misses the holster: still in your hand.") keeps it out now.
- **The fumble net checks what your log says of your guns against where they are, and its lists are empty.** N15: every word of a place (holstered, in your hand, on its sling, fallen) held to the gun once its step has ended; `KNOWN`, `OPEN` and `UNCOVERED` are all empty, 72 of the sweep's lanes on drawn houses, 0 cases.
- **The sim's busiest tick in the shoot house is halved, and held under 2 ms by a test.** The step panel is built once per moment and shared by every rule of the instinct (it was built two to four times in one evaluation), the body read once per person (it was deep-copied for every roll), a whole second in a pause is due once, and the run sheet is built only when read (`src/core/reads.js`): the tier one's busiest tick on 48213 3.17 → 1.25 ms, the private's 2.18 → 0.96, the kit's lanes 6.7 → 1.83 at most, the live lanes 4.6 → 1.37 at most (each tick the least of three runs, on a machine at a load of 4.5), the four drawn lanes 1.07 to 1.99 ms, and the busiest of the heavy run's 25 lanes 1.99 ms (the tier one on the house drawn from 48213), a hair under the budget: the full suite, at a load of 13, found the kit's breach lane at 2.00 ms, over it. Every lane's end state is the same to the byte (F60).
- **Whose man a target is goes by where he stands.** Each stand's owner is the sector it stands in on its room's plan, read by the team's one helper; the old per-stand label is gone (identical on the house as set; it is what lets a room be drawn on either side of the hall).
- **"Clear the room", your number two and the breach now work where a team meets a room plan with no range session.** An operation is opened on the place (`combat/op.js openOp`, its record `state.cqb`, written only then): your #2 is driven by it (`combat/two.js`), its doors, devices and legs by its rules, a round on its targets by their sensor, and a lock kicked, pried, shot or blown is broken on the door's own record and stays broken when the door is shut again. Off the range, by T, the sergeant clears house 1 in 47.9 s (a drawn house, 49.4 s) with every gauge of the library held, his #2 goes in within 2.7 s of his cue and takes only his own men (F72); the private and the boot recruit clear it in 115.1 s and 114.2 s. Before, an operation put there threw on T's first read. The man-still-up checks, the bracket, the line of fire and nine other reads looked only at the range's targets, so an operation off the range could call "Clear!" with a threat standing; they read the operation's too, and a scan holds them to it. `cqb` is now a reserved, sparse key of the state. Nothing of the shoot house's lane changes: every pin cell, digest and F line as before.
- **The gauges are a library.** The walls, bodies, swings, entry and corners watchers (F1, F2, F26, F25, F7), with two new ones for F45 and F47, run on any scene through `tests/cqb-gauges.mjs`; the shoot house's gauges compose them and print the same numbers to the byte.
- **In stop motion the page now does nothing while you think.** Its reads are keyed on the input you send while time stands still, not on the tick: 0 rebuilds of the step panel in 3 s held (60 before), one a key.
- **No hitch while the sounds bake, and your number two's call-outs are ready before GO.** Every sound, gun part and line renders in chunks of 4,096 samples (the same samples), so a bake's slice ends near its 3 ms: the bake's longest slice 3.3 ms in the page and its longest frame 3.6 ms in node (5.5 to 8.7 ms before, by D's measure; v0.0.18's log 13.6). His call-outs for the lane's targets (45 on the user's seed) are baked at the launch. A line first said after the sounds were baked was never baked at all in v0.0.20 (never heard): it is now.
- **A long session stays light.** The hit card's men are painted through the dressed dolls' cache (24 kept) instead of a map that kept every man for ever, and a scene left is let go by the page (its strip, AVPU, plaque and hit memos too): twelve "Again"s, four of them drawn houses, grow the heap 2.8 MB from the third to the twelfth (24.3 to 27.2 MB), at most 2 layouts held and 2 areas registered, the textures and geometries flat.
- **The shoot house on the bench card, judged.** `?bench=house-kit`, `house-live` and `house-stop` beside `shoothouse`; every card gains the sim's busiest tick, the UI's p95, the programs linked after the warm, the largest upload with its key and when, and a PASS or FAIL against TECH §16.1. On this Mac house-stop passes; house-kit and house-live fail on one clause, a band of 230 to 238 KB sent mid-lane (F73: the bands are to be painted before GO). From Windows: `play.cmd --dev`, then each `?bench=` and Copy results (question 16).
- **"Stack and clear" says what done is: a list you sign.** The shoot house's acceptance list is kept as data (`tests/house-done.mjs`): your verdict, each of your complaints since 2026-11-11 with the test that closes it, every gauge F1 to F73, never a dead end, the ladder, the moments, the saves, the performance, the scored scenes, the hand-off and this round's drawn house. A test resolves every row on every run (the test and its case found, the gauge enforced in its file, the moment's recipe, the sample's file); a renamed test or a gauge taken out of its enforced set fails it. The list says WIRED where the check is there and enforced, and OPEN, with the numbers, for a gauge the last heavy run left red (today F70, F71 and F73); the release announcement quotes the OPEN and the USER rows. 209 rows: WIRED 188, OPEN 6, USER 11, LATER 4, GAP 0.
- **Three GOs in a row on houses you have not seen qualify you.** On the picker, "Qualify as Sergeant" (the sheet after your highest qualified) starts a lane on the drawn house with a seed the page draws and hides until the card, never one you played on that sheet, with the standard kit and your sheet's par or harder; the card says "QUALIFICATION 2 OF 3 · GO" and leads with the next lane, then "QUALIFIED: SERGEANT" and "Qualify as Raider"; the picker marks the sheet QUALIFIED. A NO-GO, a lane outside the rules, one left (the title, Again), or a save loaded, imported or continued ends the attempt. Nothing is locked; kept in this browser only (`olympus.house`). Shown only with the drawn house. (Your question 9 of this round's sheet: three in a row, by default.)
- **The picker chooses the house: as set, or drawn from the seed.** "House: As set · Drawn from the seed" under the kit (H cycles it). On a drawn house the end card names its code in its header, the score line names it after the seed, "Again", "Again as" and "Again from room n" keep it, "Again with a new seed" draws a new one, and "Export the run" carries it (the replay rebuilds the house from the save at GO). The house as set's score line is v0.0.20's to the character.
- **The verdict can be read at another standard, and the ladder is held.** `houseVerdict(state, { standard })` judges the same run by the next par class up. Over 40 seeds a sheet the six sheets' take-in, first round and lane are strictly ordered (the thinnest step the Raider to the tier one, 2.7% of the lane, 73 ms, 79 ms): met (F69). Each sheet a GO at its own standard on 90% and a NO-GO at the next on 80% is **not met on two cells** (F70): the boot recruit's own (25%: two or three of his men's pars missed a lane) and the sergeant judged at operator (30%: his first rounds are inside 0.8 s on three of his four men). Both are v0.0.20's pars, said, not moved.
- **The house has six acceptance moments.** "Step by step, as a private", "Clean, as tier one", "The jam in room 2", "Bang, then go", "Through the hinges" and "A man, not a stand", each played to its card by its bot in node with ROADMAP §2.3's timeline (lived seconds, stops, real minutes at 6 s a stop, decisions a minute, dead air) and its beats saved and shown in the page with one real key pressed. Measured: the private 7.50 real minutes from the brief (52 stops), above the sheet's band (F71 open), the tier one 3.35 (18), dead air 0% in all six.
- **A clean room counts toward a career's rung.** One judge of a clean repetition (a room cleared with no fault called, and on the live lane no paint on the stack) reads the run sheet's "clean rooms" and the career's count: 20 clean rooms practised (rung 2), 60 drilled (rung 3), each teacher only so far (the unit's to 2, the cadre's to 3), a room in an operation counting double, five a sol at most; a test sheet never moves. Nothing calls it in a scene yet: the MOUT day's join will.
- **An old save of the house no longer plans a par on every read.** A save from before v0.0.20's pars gains them once at load (`arms:house-pars`), where v0.0.20 planned four rooms on every read of the timer, the paper and the card (2 to 4 ms a tick in the page). The house's save is held under 200 KB at the card (144.7 KB), with no runtime key in it.
- **The brief reaches you whole.** A line longer than the radio box's two rows is shown a page at a time ("…" where it goes on), each page for its share of the line's time, so a drawn house's doors and your kit's words are read, not cut.
- **The picker fits the window.** Six sheets in two columns and the House choices as chips on their line: Back sits at 665 px of a 720-px window (912 before) and 932 of 1,080 (1,135). The Tests menu's line says "the shoot house … as set or drawn", not "Lane A".
- **Small words put right.** The lane timer's board reads "NEW LAYOUT" on a drawn house (not "LANE A"); the end card keeps the house's code in its own case (in capitals it was a code the game refused); a struck tile's reason reads whole ("stack first", once "stack fi"); X mid-lane says "Go: the clock runs", not "Stand easy"; the hot wash after a casualty says "The lane is closed.", not "clear"; the log no longer says "You flagged …" of a muzzle at high or low ready in the lane, which the instructor holds safe; the DI's "Keep moving", "Next" and "He's on you. Carry on." are said (since v0.0.14 their words were read from an entry the line never named); a `&house=` code the scene does not play, or cannot read, now says so.
- **Off the range, a private walks in as on the lane, and the barracks keep quiet.** An operation is the range to the DI (no "Nothing on fire." mid-clear), its stall clock tells a still marine where to walk, and a "Clear!" alone clears its room; a save anywhere in an operation carries on byte for byte.
- **The test suite's quick minute is the full pool's, and each quick file has a CPU budget.** The wall limit scales by 7 / the workers (140 s at 3); a quick file over 3 s of CPU fails unless `QUICK_SLOW` names it with its own ceiling (35 files frozen: ceilings only go down); the sum is held to `QUICK_CPU_S` 375 × k (the sheet said 200; this Mac's measured quick run is 288 to 446 s, the last at a load of 9); the budgets are judged on a machine at most half loaded; `READS_FREEZE=1` holds the quick suite's reads. Whole-lane cases moved to heavy cases of the same name with "(heavy …)"; house-flawless-quick keeps 6 lanes. The quick suite now: 1,656 passed, 0 failed, 261 left out, 93.6 s at 4 workers (limit 105 s).

The hooks are shut in the game (a golden of 1,824 shaded frames, `1c53d76d17f6d4`, is unchanged). `samples/house-light` (15 views): rooms 1 and 2, the hall and the apron are all lit the same today (0.75 by day); only room 3 is dimmer (0.40). HL-a is today's; HL-b the catwalk's floods, every room and the hall at 0.75, room 3's dim going with it; HL-c warm pendant pools (core 0.75 in room 1, corners 0.30; room 3's corner 0.15), which changes play, not only the look: sight at room 1's north-west corner drops from 41.5 m to 22.6 m. The release run (`npm test`, 2026-11-16) met the same eight open cases and left them out by name (`SKIP`), the rest green: `house-drawn` ×2, `house-swings`' heavy case, `house-ladder`, `moment-house`'s node part, `render-bench`'s house-kit and house-live, `house-pace`; `audio-browser` failed in one run under load and passed whole alone and in the next; `house-tick-budget-more` failed twice in the pool at a load of 7 to 11 from other workflows (F60 at 2.16 and 2.15 ms) and passed alone on the same tree (MET, busiest 1.60 ms, the honest sergeant on 48213), so the last run left it out too. No gauge was loosened.

## v0.0.20 (2026-11-16)

- **Your number two carries a P-12 as standard issue, on every loadout and every sheet, as you do** (your answer, 2026-11-16: "#2 has to have a sidearm"). `loadout.js` `TWO_P12_DEFAULT` is now true: every lane issues him the P-12 in its thigh holster in Condition 3 with its spare in the thigh pouch (1.21 kg, his push and brake a little less: the private's 3.099 → 3.062 m/s² and 5.036 → 4.976 m/s²), the tower waits on it at MAKE READY and SHOW CLEAR, and with his rifle stopped and an armed man facing him within 10 m he draws it instead of "Gun down!". The loadout screen shows "Your #2: P-12, issued as yours" as a line, no longer a control, and the picker reads "Kit: Frag ▸" (v0.0.19: "Your #2: P-12 ⇄ none" and "Kit: Frag, #2 P-12 ▸"). Codes: v0.0.18's read and write back byte for byte; v0.0.19's `…~two:p12` reads with his P-12 and is written without the token; `~two:none` stays for the tests only. A v0.0.19 lane loaded mid-run keeps the kit it was filled with (my call).
- **A fumble making his P-12 ready is tried again, and his stall is never yours.** His rack or its safe fumbled at MAKE READY is planned again from what he believes, as his rifle's halts are (`house-two.js` `twoHalt`, at most `TWO_TRIES` 3); before, nothing tried again and the tower waited to its move-on: the boot recruit from the brief by the keys on 7001, 7300 and 8170 had GO at 127 to 128 s, now 50.05, 48.05 and 49.05 s, within 10 s of the same lanes without his P-12 (40.05, 43.05 and 44.05 s: GO now waits on the instructor's lines, below); on 8248 GO is 95.05 s with his P-12 or without (one 'slow' fault, your own P-12's). At the move-on a want of his is his: the instructor racks his P-12 ("Number two, your pistol had no round up. I've racked it."), his MAKE READY is given up, and no 'slow' fault is charged to you (before, two). The bug was v0.0.19's own on `~two:p12`.
- **On the live lane his P-12 carries marking rounds, as yours does.** v0.0.19 issued his the house's frangible there: one helper now issues both men's, so all four HAD-17s on the lane are marking.
- **A loadout code the page refuses fills the standard with your #2's P-12** (before: the standard without it), and `~two:none` written by a test round-trips (before: read back with his P-12).
- **A marking round now ends the lane only when the live round it stands for would have been fatal** (your answer, 2026-11-16: "Paintball should end if the hit is 'fatal'"). Each strike, on you, your number two or a role-player, is rated on its tick by one helper (`combat/would.js`) through the live path's own (`penetrate.js` `bodyHit`, `traceRound`'s body branch): the calibre's ball (the R-7's 6.5×40 at 788 m/s at 5 m, the P-12's 9×21 at 385 m/s) through the man's armour as worn (the carrier's soft panel, its plates out for training, over 0.85 of the torso, an arm in front of it met first; it lets a 6.5 ball through 98.6% of the time and stops the 9×21 always; the suit's helmet has no rating, so the head is bare) into the game's wound model on a copy of his body, never his own, w at the impact's speed (1.61). Fatal: dead, down for good at once (the head, the torso out, shock, the pain, both legs, never the short knock that follows every round), or out cold from the blood within 120 s at a walk. One body strike is never fatal at any Fitness; two rifle strikes are fatal for 91% of boot recruits, 82% at Fitness 14, 45% at 19 and 25% of tier ones; a rifle round in the head 99 to 100%, a P-12's 88 to 91%. Anything else is a wound: the man carries on, with the sting only (my call: no limp or handicap this round). Lanes ending 'casualty', 34 a sheet, recruit to tier one: 91, 88, 47, 53, 6, 0% → 91, 91, 47, 50, 0, 0% (measured on v0.0.19 with the answers alone; on the integrated tree F46's six lanes a sheet end 5, 5, 5, 4, 0 and 0, every casualty yours): most stack casualties still come on the second strike, but a man the model only wounds fights on (the sergeant on 1999 and the Raider on 1407 and 1851 now clear); the pars and lane times on the lanes that clear both ways as v0.0.19's within a par and 0.3 s.
- **A role-player goes down by the same rule.** v0.0.19 put him down on two torso marks or one in the head (the stands' sensor); now on a strike the model rates fatal: the tier one's 108 strikes on them, 50 fatal, 58 wounds; most go down on the second. A man struck and still fighting is "1 HIT AND FIGHTING ON" on the card, and a round of yours his soft panel stopped is said, once a man: "His panel took that one." (your P-12's torso strikes on them are stopped nine times in ten).
- **The card and the run sheet say what the paint would have done.** The casualty's card leads "CASUALTY · FATAL: THE CHEST, 2ND STRIKE ON YOU, THROUGH THE SOFT PANEL, ROOM 2, BY THE MAN IN THE DEEP CORNER" ("FATAL ON YOUR #2: … ON HIM"; v0.0.19: "CASUALTY · PAINT: 2 TORSO, ROOM 2, …"); a card reads "PAINT ON YOU: 2 TORSO · WOULD HAVE BEEN: WOUNDED, CHEST"; a leg's row "PAINT ON YOU 2 (WOUNDED)"; the hot wash says it in the wound model's words, the verdict first and never a number ("What those rounds would have done: you, down, and staying down: the chest; a gunshot wound in the chest, the round gone through, bleeding hard; …"); and the instructor names the place the would-have gives ("You took paint, left arm. Keep working.") and, for a strike the panel stopped, "Paint, chest. Your panel took it. Keep working."

- **A charge on the door is the only way in until it is fired, and your #2 is never held at its safe point for ever.** Backspace after "Placing." left Push and Kick live over the SC-1, and "Clear the room" @push, @kick and @pry with them; either spent 3 s with the door shut or opened the leaf over the charge, the #2 stayed at his safe point (32.19, 12.45), and T's "Wait." ran every leg out to the lane's end at 363 s, "You left a charge on that door." Now a charge placed or primed on the shut leaf sets the room's rows to it (@charge), the way in has no cycle, and every row that would open the leaf another way (Open, Kick, Pry, Ram, the lock shot, and the drills that breach another way) is struck "a charge is on the door" (`bundle.js` `chargeOnLeaf`, `breach.js` `CHARGE_ON_WHY`); over the #2's own charge your rows wait on his blast. The door beat never waits on a #2 held at his charge safe point, and his hold ends once your leg is another door's. Backspace at "Placing." and the honest bot: clear at 55.5 s (tier one) and 70.7 s (sergeant), no fault; a charge left and the leg stalled on: clear at 131.9 s, was "passed" at 375 s. The hunt of v0.0.18 (kit-6) and its review.
- **The instructor never goes quiet over a man hidden by the jamb.** "Clear the room" chosen at room 1's first contact pause and Backspaced walked #1 back to the stack with 1a still bracketed and out of the frame: the line was "Pair." with no shot row, then none at all until the leg's stall (4.7 to 49.7 s; tier one, sergeant, raider). The bracketed man out of the frame now counts as no aim, as no bracket does: T says "Get in." / "In. On him." and carries on (`cqb.js` cqb-contact). The hunt of v0.0.18 (kit-7).

Kick it." had no act; set to Pry, the DI still said "Kick it again." Now T kicks again by Carry on (the door beat from where he stands) or the room's row that kicks, and once the way is set to another, that way's row is the line's ("Room. Clear it." on @pry). - **#1 tells a pried pull door "Opens out".** The tell keyed the swing on the way in, so the kit's ways (pry, ram, a charge) fell to "Opens in" under "Pull door, hinge right."; it now reads the door's swing for every way but the kick (`bundle.js` `tellWords`). The hunt of v0.0.18 (kit-1).
- **Your own grenade's blast on the stack is on the paper: over 27.6 kPa the fault "STACK IN THE BLAST 30 KPA", from 20 kPa the mark "STACK STUNNED 22 KPA".** The boot recruit's failed toss lands at the door's foot about 1.1 m from him: 29.8 kPa, a 2.4 s stun, and the card said nothing but "ON THE BANG 1.93 S"; between STUN_KPA 20 and BREACH_SAFE_KPA 27.6 a G-11 stuns you U(2, 6) s with nothing said either (seeds 1074 at 26.8 kPa, 1148 at 21.8). The charge's rule now covers the G-11 (one quantity, one fault: `shoothouse.js` `kitMarks`, `HOUSE_CHIEF.stackBlast`), and the stun under it is a neutral mark (my call; the SC-1's 27.1 kPa at its safe point unmarked as before). The throw model is unchanged. The hunt of v0.0.18 (kit-9) and its review.
- **A negligent discharge is felt as the shot it is: the magazine's count goes down one, and the last round's slide or bolt is believed locked back on the empty.** `firearms.js` act()'s discharge branch now runs feel() on the pull, as a function check's BANG does, and leaves only the chamber a question; before, it set the chamber to '?' and nothing else, so the P-12's count stayed one too many for good (truth 6, believed 7), and a last round's slide, locked back, was believed closed. A press check refused on a slide or bolt held back now teaches the hand it is back, and a look in refused on a closed one that it is closed: Fix's next way is the look at the port, where before it named the same refused Press check for ever, the clock still. The hunt of v0.0.18 (pistol-1).
- **UNLOAD, SHOW CLEAR covers the holstered P-12 by what is in it: the lane is never called clear over a loaded pistol, yours or your number two's.** The tower's SHOW CLEAR (`shoothouse.js` houseReady 'shown') now holds while the holstered P-12 has a magazine, a round or its slide back (`pistolLacks` 'pistol:loaded'), or the rifle on its sling still holds anything behind a show already made; with the rifle's own clear done, the panel offers "P-12: SHOW CLEAR" (`sidearm.js` `pistolClearOwed`, the row weapon:pistol-clear) and the instructor names it ("Your pistol too: clear it."), and his nudge says so ("Shooter, your pistol too: show me clear.") where it said "back on your mark". The P-12's own clear stops at a part that halts (a lock back missed, a round seen), never a release and a safe over the round still in it, and is owed again; its parts run with the drawn pistol's carry, so a rifle hanging on its sling no longer refuses every one "P-12 is slung." Your #2's P-12 (`~two:p12`) is judged the same (`house-two.js` `twoLacks`), his halted part planned again from his belief, and whatever his macro still leaves loaded the instructor clears himself ("Number two, that's still loaded. Give it here.") before "Number two: clear.". Before, 12 of 36 lanes of the boot recruit and the private on seed 1 ended with the P-12 1 + 9 in the holster under "The lane is clear.", and 1 of 60 `~two:p12` lanes (v0.0.19: 4) with his loaded; now 0 of 216 (every sheet, setup and bot on seeds 1 and 2) and 0 of 270 (`~two:p12`, seeds 6 to 8, three bots). The hunt of v0.0.18 (pistol-2) and its review.
- **The coach's take at SHOW CLEAR clears every gun you carry and puts a drawn pistol back in its holster; Rifle up under Stop and SHOW CLEAR is the holster and the up alone.** `handling.js` `coachClears` clears the gun in use, the rifle on its sling and the pistol in the holster or the hand (each magazine to a pouch of its family, the P-12's to the thigh's), and runs again only while a gun still holds anything; `sidearm.js` weaponList('rifle-up') adds the rifle's way to ready (tap, rack, RELOAD) only while the lane is live, so off it the row is "Rifle up: holster". Before, with the P-12 drawn at SHOW CLEAR he cleared the pistol alone, Rifle up then racked the R-7, and the sergeant's lane (1489397426) ended "passed" with the R-7 live, 4 in the magazine, on semi. The hunt of v0.0.18 (pistol-3).
- **At SHOW CLEAR with your own guns shown and clear, the instructor tells you to wait for your number two, not to UNLOAD, SHOW CLEAR again.** `range.js` houseCmd's 'two' variant at SHOW CLEAR, as at Stop; before, the honest boot recruit on 1037 cleared the R-7 twice (house-pin's honest/recruit/1037: 9,003 → 8,823 ticks). The hunt of v0.0.18 (pistol-2's lanes).
- **The P-12's feedback says "slide" where the rifle's says "bolt".** `firearms.js` `partOf` in act()'s texts, in said(), in a refused pull's words (`refusalOf`: "Nothing: the slide is back.") and in the weapon card's hover sentence (`steps.js` weaponCardModel); the DI's tier-1 RELOAD on the P-12 says "Slide's back. RELOAD." (`cqb.js` rldP). Before, the P-12's J read "The bolt goes home." under a panel that says "Slide release". The rifle's words are kept to the byte. The hunt of v0.0.18 (pistol-4).
- **Top up works with the P-12 drawn and a rifle magazine in the other hand: it puts that magazine away first.** `plan.js` absOf carries whether a pouch of the held foreign magazine's family has room, and the search may stow or dump it before the reach. Before, Top up was struck with the planner's bare "no plan" (the sergeant on 1489397426 after reach@p1 and Transition); now it runs magOut:pouch, dumpMag, reach, seat in 2.62 s. The hunt of v0.0.18 (pistol-5).
- **Magazines on the floor at LOAD or MAKE READY: the instructor names the pick-up, and the tower's move-on racks a P-12 still owed whatever the rifle lacks.** `range.js` houseCmd's 'floor' variant ("Pick it up. A magazine in.", T the seat, the reach at the feet or RELOAD) where LOAD is not offered; `shoothouse.js` houseMoveOn racks the holstered P-12 whenever its round is owed. Before, "The tower is waiting on you: LOAD." had no act with the magazines at the feet, and the tower went to GO with the R-7 empty and the P-12 unchambered (the fumble net's N11 at rate 0.35). The hunt of v0.0.18 (pistol-6).

You do nothing before the command." and the fault 'ahead' on the KD course's first PRONE, at CEASE FIRE and at test-handling's PRONE, and at the police call, after the show clear, it chambered a round with nothing said. With the tower's command current, Fix is v0.0.17's as before. The hunt of v0.0.18 (planner-1).

- **A move from a body pressed against a wall steps out of it by the nav's own way, never by a waypoint inside the wall's clearance.** `route.js` `routed` dropped the nav's step out of a grid-blocked start, so the leg from the body cut a jamb's corner and the corner hold put its waypoint in a blocked cell: the fuzz tier one's approach to the hall on seed 1185 went (19.363, 7.528) to (19.297, 7.554) and back, 2 legs off the grid (house-flawless-keys F3). - **An armed grenade in the hand now locks the rifle out of every rifle action.** A v0.0.19 save with the rifle at high ready and a G-11 armed in hand 0 offered RELOAD, LOAD and the reaches. RELOAD seated a magazine and chambered a round beside the live grenade (the play review of v0.0.20). Now every row that works the rifle, the magazine or the trigger is struck "hands full: the G-11", and the gun refuses them itself. Sling it stays live. The DI's chip no longer says "RELOAD" or "In" under "It's in your hand."
- **A wound on the live lane is now the debrief's one thing, and a wounded lane is no longer "Clean".** Since your answer of 2026-11-16, a wound short of fatal lets the lane go on. Paint on you or your #2 now weighs 80 a strike: "Paint on the stack. Win the first second: see him first, shoot first." A room with paint on the stack is no longer a clean room. The corporal by T on 1111 read "Clean lane. Again, faster." and "3 clean rooms"; he now reads the paint line and "2 clean rooms".
- **The wound model's sentences are on the end card.** On the live lane they were only in the hot wash, which stands down beside the plan view, so the page said only "WOULD HAVE BEEN: WOUNDED, CHEST". The run sheet now prints them under the TEAM row, for example "What those rounds would have done: you, a gunshot wound in the chest, the round gone through, bleeding hard; …".
- **Each missed par on the run sheet now names its cause.** It reads "BASIC MISSED (SLOW HANDS)" (or seen late, planted first, rifle not up, your gun). The causes were in the hot wash's bars only, so none reached the page.
- **"You saw him late" now needs a late take-in.** It is said only for a take-in over its allowance (1.3 × the sheet's book). A take-in within it, with the par lost all the same, is the hands' cause (the feet's below rung 2, where the row planted). The tier one's 50 ms take-in at a book of 50 was "You saw him late".
- **The lane timer's strip now runs each par on the scene's own clock.** It no longer says HE FIRED FIRST of a man whose par is met. It used to run from the first sighting by anyone, without the instructor's click. The tier one on 105 read "2A HE FIRED FIRST" for 1.25 s on a par met within the click, and "1B HE FIRED FIRST" on the #2's own man (the code review). A par really missed still reads HE FIRED FIRST until a round answers him.
- **The score line now says the shot and the voice you played.** "… · panel C · shot A3 · voice c · …"; they were read from fields nothing wrote.
- **The house's voice now keeps its fixed lines, and bakes every line said.** The PA's lines and the team's calls stay cached, with 160 others (it was 280 in all, the fixed lines dropped first). A line said after the last sound had baked was never baked, and so never heard. Now it is.
- **Breach on 5 at a door's first stop now reads "stack first" where you carry a charge or a tool.** It read "not issued" with the issue badge. "Not issued" stays for the standard and flash loadouts.

- **Three fixes for v0.0.19 behaviour the round's moved dice reached** (found by the integrated tree's gauges, each pinned by the new `merge-fixes`): your #2's failed identification of his own man while you are inside no longer holds his entry 1.2 s (the corporal's room 3 on seeds 1111, 1481 and 2813 was 3.05 to 3.55 s past his cue; now 0 of 1,200 rooms late on the hundred seeds); a failed task's tool goes down at his feet, as the force beat's own end leaves it (he had carried the halligan to the card after a pry that would not give); and a shot row cut because you see a man's hands up makes that your identification of him (the sergeant's give-and-give live lane on 48213 had passed room 2 by the stall at 101.6 s, 1,822 ticks of a cut row).
- **Smaller, in the merge.** The house's sound counts a voiced whisper and shout as the stand-ins' cues did (`src/ui/audio/house.js`); `voices.js` knows "I've" and "panel"; the press model's held lane `LANE_C_HELD` is 1.31 (1.25 after the roles; the hunt's Top up and his P-12 move the lane to 112 choices, C 1.30 against v0.0.13's 1.21; without the entry still strictly under); the live gate steps through a pause at a choice.
- **A lane of "Stack and clear" now ends by 585 s from GO: each room and the hall has a due, and the instructor's "Move on." comes 30 s before it.** With n legs after the current one, a leg is due `LANE_MAX_MS − LANE_KEEP_MS − n × LEG_LAST_MS` from GO, at 450, 495, 540 and 585 s (`LANE_KEEP_MS` 15 s, `LEG_LAST_MS` 45 s, courses.js `laneKeepMs`, `legLastMs`; `shoothouse.js` `stall`, `legDueMs`); at its due the leg is passed with the TIME fault, and steps of its own state buy nothing more. The stall alone had no upper bound: every leg is at least 90 s, and each look or round before the words bought 60 s again, so the fuzz private on seed 1111 ran legs of 148, 153, 227 and 183 s to the lane's 600 s limit (F14). That lane is now passed at 585.0 s; the other 59 fuzz lanes of the probe are unchanged to the hundredth, and no honest lane comes near a due (the slowest pinned lane, the boot recruit's on 1489397426, ends at 308 s). F14 gains a clause, "none past 585 s", printed and enforced. (My call: a lane that simply ended at 600 s as a NO-GO by time would be a loosening, and yours to say.)
- **Your #2 no longer walks back to a stack whose room the stall has passed, and where each of you holds the other his way round comes at 1.5 s, not after your own walk is cut.** `shoothouse.js` `endLeg` halts his walk to the passed leg's stack where he stands (`bundle.js` `twoLegOver`: his Stack up, stack point or heels beat for that door, 'stall'), and he sets off for the next stack from there: the fuzz recruit on 1489397426 had walked back to room 2's stack and stood its ready and squeeze, about 3.4 s, and was 3.41 m off his slot at room 3 (F17; now 0.27 m). A walk of yours held by a body now counts as standing for his rule (`heldStill`): at room 1's stack the fuzz private on 1037 had your Stack up and his heels beat holding each other 0.51 m apart, and his walk was tried only after yours was cut at `PIN_END_MS` 2 s, 1.85 s into his hold; now he steps off your path at 1.5 s and your Stack up carries on. A hold with nothing of his walks left to hold is closed on his record (`gapSettle`); before, it stayed at 1.85 s for the 27.6 s he then stood. F17 on the keys gauge: 1 of 836 stacks over 1.5 m (3.41 m) → 0 (worst 1.31 m); F46 on the bodies: 556 ticks (his longest hold 1.85 s) → 0 (1.50 s).
- **Your #2's "Gun down!" at your "Room clear!" is now closed by his "Gun up!".** With his rifle empty at room 3 he draws his P-12 and takes his man; his Rifle up is still running when you call the room, so he answers "Gun down!". The Rifle up then found the rifle empty and turned into a speed reload, which said "Reloading!" and "Up." and left the "Gun down!" open on all three of F36's seeds. That status call is now the one he owes a close (`house-two.js` `W.down.said`), carried over his transition and never said twice: "Gun down!", then "Gun up!" once the rifle is back. F36: 3 not closed → 0 (30 lanes, 849 calls).

Bang to entry for the recruit by the keys over 16 seeds: 5 of 14 within 3.5 s (mean 5.66 s) with his #2's P-12 and 12 of 13 (3.42 s) without → 14 of 14 either way (2.20 and 2.15 s); the private 16 of 16 both ways. The P-12 itself costs nothing: its 1.21 kg adds 4 ms to his walk to the door, which only re-rolls your notice by a tick.

## v0.0.19 (2026-11-16)

- **Practice frag and concussion grenades are issued, and the instructor scores them as the live ones** (your ask of 2026-11-15: "breaching options and equipment (explosives etc), and grenades (bangs, frags, concussion etc)", finished: v0.0.18 gave the G-11 and the SC-1). The G-9P and G-13P (presets "Frag" and "Concussion", two on the left face) are armed, cooked (the lever let go, "one thousand one, one thousand two": Weapon handling DC 6, from the sergeant up by default) and thrown deep with "Frag out!" or "Concussion out!". A 4 g spotting charge pops and the instructor reads what the live G-9 or G-13 would have done (`src/sim/combat/practice.js`): WEAPONS §12.3's fragments (λ 0.71 at 15 m, 40 at 2 m; the house's panels stop every ball, a closed leaf passes 20 to 24% within 3 m), the blast (room 1's closed pressure 13.7 kPa for a G-13, a body round a jamb at half the free field), each stand down, wounded, injured or stunned. The stands it puts down are turned away by his hand ("Good frag. 1a and 1b down."), the stunned turn late; the spotting charge itself startles nobody (v0.0.18's burst dazed every stand in the room). The read is shown after the burst, never before.
  - **The brief says which rooms are hostiles only, and a frag anywhere else is a fault.** With a practice grenade carried: "Intelligence: room 1, hostiles only. Anywhere else, assume a family." (room 1 on the seeds where 1b is a threat; never room 2's no-shoot nor room 3's hostage). A frag into a room with a no-shoot or the hostage is "FRAGGED" ("You never frag a family."), a concussion that injures one "IN THE BLAST" (room 2's centre: 35.9 kPa on 2b against the eardrum's 34.5), a ball on the stack "IN THE BURST", and in the room at the burst the lane ends: "CEASE FIRE: IN THE ROOM AT THE BURST". The throw seen in the door past the par is "EXPOSED", a grenade four seconds on the floor "UNCOOKED 3.8 S" from the sergeant up. Question 3 of DESIGN.md's log asks whether the brief's word or what the grenade would have hit should judge.

F30 by sheet after it: 10 seeds, private to tier one, 30 reads of each kind, 0 dirty.

Go."; on a push door it is the latch edge, "Latch cut. Go.". The SC-1 stays struck "opens toward you" on a pull door. Placing takes 8.0 s at Demolitions DC 12.   - **Over a gun that cannot fire, the DI names the gun first.** "That one's his. Carry on.", "No shot." with no act, "Wait for him." and "Rifle up." onto a dry rifle no longer lead while your gun is dry: the reload, the bolt, the press check lead; a stoppage with no drill offered names Fix's row.
  - **Your #2 steps aside beside you, never round a wall.** He could pick a point beyond a wall and walk out through a door to it (3.5 m); now only a point a step away.
- **Smaller fixes of the round.** A role-player who gave up with your round in flight was counted DOWN and GAVE UP both; now GAVE UP when his hands came up before his rounds put him down, once, on the card and the room's row. A round already on its way when the hands came up is tallied by where it struck, no no-shoot hit. A practice frag or concussion now reaches the role-players (a G-9P at a man's feet rules the man beside him out 'frag', a G-13P stuns him 2 to 6 s) and they hear bursts set off in the rules layer. Stop with your own rifle safe and only your #2 still on his says Wait, never out and back in every tick (21 dead ends in 2 of 24 honest live lanes). A "Top up" offered with an armed grenade in a hand, which broke the tick when pressed, is no longer offered. `bundle.js` or `combat/breach.js` imported first no longer throws.
- **Stage 0 moved your #2's code and kept the standard lane to the byte.** The #2's drill is `src/sim/scenarios/house-two.js` (re-exported from the house), and the 17 reads of "whose man" in contact.js, bundle.js, cqb.js and the #2 ask `team.js`; 24 pinned lanes from the fill to the card (tick, run sheet and save) are identical to v0.0.18's. The standard lane is v0.0.18's to the byte but for the team's two movers (his side, his stoppage).
