## v0.0.12 (2026-11-11)

- **The Tests menu has "Stack and clear"** (`?play=test-shoothouse&tier=…`, after "Bang"; ROADMAP §4.2's 0.0.7d row, shipped as v0.0.12; COMBAT §25; the build sheet `docs/build/0.0.12.md`), the heart of your idea of 2026-11-07: "a recruit marine would have to do everything step by step while an experience Tier 1 Operator would have "automations through pressing T"". At 1 G in the Wheel's house 1 (your rule of 2026-11-08, "martian marines always train at 1G except for basic weapons training"), in the black combat EVA suit, with a generated marine of your fire team as your #2 on your own sheet and a generated staff sergeant walking the catwalk over your room: a lane of three rooms and the hall, stacked and cleared on his GO. Pick a sheet (Boot recruit, Private focused, Corporal, Sergeant, Raider, Tier one) and a par (the sheet's own, Basic 3.0 s, Advanced 1.5 s, Operator 0.8 s; P cycles it). The recruit does it step by step: Stack on the door, the door's row (Push it open, Pull it open by the door's swing, Kick it, Read the door), X, and the walk in by the movement keys, the world running until his own take-in pauses it. The tier one presses T on "T: Clear the house · 35.8 s" and the bundle runs the stack, the door, the entry, the corners and the far leg, his feet, hands, eyes and voice overlapping, handing the decision back at every man newly seen, the closet's door, a lock, a failed kick, the #2 not on him, the gun and the lane's end. Same rooms, rules and rolls (your rule of 2026-11-07: "no watering down but factor in experience in how smooth and fast we do things"): only the time and what one press covers differ. Turners on their triggers (on GO, the door opening, the hall's west end), a no-shoot with a phone, the armoured silhouette that rings until the head plate falls, a hostage card in room 3's dim far leg (To the head leads), a locked closet, and a dummy round at round 3 to 6 of your first magazine; pars by the timer's tone, not wounds ("he fired first" in red); the instructor's run sheet (each room's time, take-in, first round, par, zones, corners, doorway time, faults) and the hot wash behind the brief splash (90 s, a take-in bar a man) on the end card; "Again as …" every other sheet on the same seed. Scored on three aspects: **the pause**, **the drill**, **the shots** ("OLYMPUS v0.0.12 · test-shoothouse · seed … · pause … · drill … · shots … · ★ … · note: …").
  - **Played at 16 px/m in the page** (headless Chrome at 1600 × 900 and 1024 × 768, seed 48213, after the reviews' fixes): the private by the keys from GO: "T: Stack on the door · 4.7 s" (T), "Push it open." (T, the leaf swinging into room 1 on its left hinge), "X, then in. Corner left. Keep moving.", X and Z with Q held: the amber brackets closed, "CONTACT · RIFLE · TAKEN IN 1.05 S" with the plates to the left of the far left man, clear of the far right one; "Off safe." (T), "Rifle. On him." (T aims), "Fire." (its digit, two rounds in the A zone), his #2 took his own man, "Say it. "Clear!"" (T): room 1 in 13.05 s; the lane carried to the card by the scene's bot (74.5 s, the run sheet, the hot wash). The tier one by T from GO: "Clear the house. Three rooms and the hall.", the pause at the door in one tick with both men bracketed ("Contact · 0.05 s." and "Two."), Pair moving on by its digit, Carry on by T; the lane in 44.0 s, the card. No page errors either way.
  - **On seed 48213, room 1 alone (COMBAT §25.5.1's reference room, #1's own time to his "Clear!") against the sheet's table at 1 G:** the boot recruit by the keys 16.35 s (the table 15.33), take-in 0.95, first round 3.15 s after the sighting (2.98); the private 14.55 s (13.79), 0.80, 2.55 (2.00, his Off safe and the second man's take-in included); the corporal by T 9.28 (8.82), the sergeant 6.47 (6.13), the Raider 5.29 (5.19), the tier one 5.11 s (4.98), take-in 0.05 and first round 0.28 s (0.30). With your #2's sector the room's "Clear!" comes 1.0 to 1.3 s later by T (the corporal 10.35 s, the sergeant 7.75, the Raider 6.35, the tier one 6.30): the room is clear when both sectors are. **The lane:** the boot recruit by the keys 87.7 s, the private 73.5 s, the corporal by T 62.5 s, the sergeant 48.0 s, the Raider 49.3 s, the tier one 44.0 s: the private's lane 1.67 times the tier one's (COMBAT §25.13 asks at least 1.5), every sheet at the card with 6 of 6 threats down and both no-shoots untouched, the #2 firing 3 or 4 rounds on his own men, the keys and T ending every room in the same state. Over 100 seeds the private's lane by the keys averages 75.1 s (70.1 to 80.1, a seeded human start and a line up to 10% long), the sergeant's by T 46.7 s, the tier one's 44.2 s (41.9 to 47.2): 1.70 times; every run reaches the card, and a seed run twice gives one digest. Thirty fuzzed runs (never firing, the dummy never cleared, "Clear!" never called, Backspace and X at random) reach the card in 3.2 to 12.0 game minutes, five a sheet.
- **The stepper and the bundles: one machine for every step, the recruit's and the tier one's** (`src/sim/combat/stepper.js`, `bundle.js`, `drills/enter-and-clear.js`; COMBAT §25.7). v0.0.5's step machinery moved out of shooting.js unchanged (every shipped scene's steps byte for byte: LOAD at Weapon handling 10 still 2,414 ms planned and 2,450 lived), with channels added: a bundle's beats run on the feet, the hands, the eyes and the voice, one at a time to rung 2, the hands and the voice beside the feet from rung 3, the eyes too at rung 4, every boundary at its formula time on the first tick at or after it (a save made mid-bundle carries on byte for byte). Stack on the door and the door's rows are rung 0 singles, so the boot recruit plays the lane; Stack and open, Enter, Dominate rung 2; Clear the room rung 3; Clear the house rung 4. **Below rung 2 nothing but the keys crosses a door's plane into a room not yet cleared**, and T at the open door is X, never the feet (your "then would move in using movement keys (game unpaused with X)"). A bundle never fires, picks, reloads or spends; a halted one is "Carry on: …" first in the panel and on T; Backspace stops it after its running beat.
- **Your #2 is a teammate on the same drill** (a marine of your fire team drawn from the seed, the same man on every sheet, your sheet written on him for the scene): he stacks 0.65 m behind you, whispers "Ready." on every stack (from the integration: before it the single's stack never said it), goes in on your first step through the open door through the same plans and stepper, takes only his slot's stands with his own take-in, identification and drill, and calls his corner; the room is clear on his call and yours. The lane's rules: the funnel (over 1 s stopped in a doorway: "Don't stop in the door!"), the flag (your muzzle across him within 5 m, not at high ready: "Muzzle!"), the corners (a hot spot neither of you covered: "Your corner!"), Relaxed in a room ("Weapon up. You're in their house."), the safety stop (aimed at him or the instructor: "Cease fire! Cease fire!", the card), the inner door left at your back, stalls (60 s: "Move on."; 30 s more: the room passed with 'time'), ten minutes the lane.
- **The movement stances at 1 G, with weight** (V where a scene sets `stances`: the house; BODY §16.1, §16.2). Relaxed walks as built (1.45 m/s at 1 G), Combat 1.23 m/s at low ready, Assault 2.03 m/s shouldered (Melas 0.97 and 1.60); in Combat and Assault the body pushes and brakes at the legs' and the floor's limit with the suit's 25.68 kg (the private 3.12 and 5.07 m/s², a stop 0.24 s from Combat; the tier one 3.66 and 5.88, the grip's limit), so a seam costs what it costs. The take-in reads the stance, the light (room 3's dim fill 0.40: ×1.13) and the hostage (×1.5).
- **The Wheel's sector 4 has house 1** (`src/data/places/wheel.js`, `x4-plans.js`; PLACES §4.15): a 40 × 28 m deck at 1 G of spin (R 500 m, ω 0.1347 rad/s, from the `wheel` preset), X4's layout A built as chunks: 11 rooms and 11 openings, the hall along the spin, room 1 COMBAT §25.5.1's room exactly (5 × 4 m, a 0.9 m door inward, the path in 2.72 m, the start mark 4.00 m from the stack), room 2 a pull door with a locked closet (kick DC 14), room 3 an L, dim at every hour; the catwalk at 3 m and its stairs, the booth. The authored plans hold the stacks, points (0.42 m or more off every wall), corners, hot spots, sectors, the far leg's dead space and its walk (from the integration: up the near leg's east side to where the far leg is seen, the bundle's own beat and the panel's "Move: the far leg"). New goldens on purpose: `WHEEL_S4` 05f066607e544d, `HOUSE1` 04447e988e6958, its grid 1dd12b42226e54, `X4_PLANS` 122fbb741feb40.
- **The spin is one module and moves the hole, not the odds** (`src/sim/places/spin.js`, the one home of ω: a new syntax rule). A body walking along the spin weighs 3.4% more at the Combat walk and 5.6% at the Assault walk (said, not applied to the stride); a frangible round lands 0.15, 0.43, 0.84 and 1.71 cm low at 3, 5, 7 and 10 m fired prograde (high retrograde) and 0.65 cm to the shooter's right at 10 m, within 0.03 cm of an inertial integration. The hot wash says it once: "The Wheel drops a round low along the spin. Not enough to miss by, in a room."
- **The combat EVA suit and the house's issue** (`kit.js` `ces-1`, `house-cqb`): the suit black, 16 kg, its visor down (no flash, no dazzle) and its ears filtering (a training bang at 2 m: 36 points against 60 bare); the plate carrier, two magazine pouches and a dump pouch, the R-7 and three magazines of 10 frangible, at low ready: 25.68 kg. Drawn black with a sealed helmet and a smoked visor, no red and no skin; under the Wheel's roof no cap is drawn (from the integration: a deck is roofed, as a building's floor is).
- **The panel and the line for the drill** (VIEWS §7.4, §10, §11). A bundle's row shows its beats as dots grouped by channel with a bar at each seam; the running bundle its four lanes and a time bar; the instinct line names the act and its planned time ("T: Stack on the door · 4.7 s"); the house's drill and door rows lead the panel (from the integration: the struck Fire and Aim led at GO before); the corners' ticks on the floor, pale until covered; the instructor's lane timer (tenths running, hundredths stopped, the par counting down); a hall's reverb made by code (RT60 0.6 s), the timer's beeps, the harness's low tone, the doors by verb, the PA's "Standby", "GO" and "Stop", the suit's footsteps. `?bench=shoothouse` runs the tier one through the lane for 20 s.
- **Fixed in integrating the round** (each my call): a man in a room you have not looked into is no longer drawn (his card showed through the walls before your take-in); in the house nothing is bracketed until a man is seen (the default was the instructor, "a person: the range stops"), and only a man turned toward you with a clear line is aimed at; the bay's "Downrange! Everything else is people." is quiet in the house, so T at GO does what the line names; a teammate's muzzle habit is checked but never logged as yours ("Muzzle past Pvt Nyberg", yourself, by your #2's rifle); the hot spots are named from the door ("the near right corner", where three read "the far right corner"); the wait row reads "Wait" in the lane; a startle or CEASE FIRE during a bundle stops it after its beat instead of throwing; a beat's name is its key, so a save made mid-bundle passes the id walk; the hot wash's no-shoot bars carry no par; the dim room has its fill colour; the contact setup faces into the room.
- **Fixed after the round's three reviews (the code, the play and the page): T clears the house as a pair, and never waits for ever** (each my call, from the reviewers' 35 findings).
  - **T no longer waits for ever on a #2 already in place.** The hand on the lane's door waits only until he is within 1.5 m of his slot (his walk's closed form, so his "Ready." releases it) and opens; "Your number two's not with you." comes only if he is still away 3 s on, the approach is never cut for him from rung 3, and an inner door's kick never waits on him. Before, the sergeant on seed 48213 was handed back in room 2 with his #2 on his slot, and T then said "Two's not up. Wait." 15 s a press until the lane's ten minutes.
  - **Your #2 takes his own men.** His entry starts on your first step through the open door (before, on your crossing its plane: he waited in the hall while you stood in the doorway at your pause), at his own rung (a private's #2 brings his muzzle onto the door as his #1 does), his eyes sweep his sector, his Dominate runs to its end before his room counts swept, he follows up a man still up (at most twice, To the head after "he's still up"), and he never borrows your aim or your sector's take-in. Your "Clear!" waits on his man and on his sector, at most 5 s and no later than his own call ends (a room is clear when every sector has been looked at), so T no longer calls a room clear with his man up and unfired on. His rounds in the lane on seed 48213: 0 to 2 before, 3 to 4 now on every sheet. A par on his man runs from his sighting, and the hot wash's advice comes from your own corner's men only (before, every sheet missed its par on 1b, 3a and 3b however well it played, and the tier one was told "Too slow off the sighting" after a 0.05 s take-in).
  - **"Carry on" from the aim brings the muzzle up first**, a hands beat that flows with the feet from rung 3: the sergeant's Carry on had walked room 1 with the rifle aimed across his #2 ("Muzzle! Not across your number two.", in the middle of T's bundle).
  - **The doorway fault counts your feet stopped in it**, never a walk through (COMBAT §25.3.2's "standing in a doorway"): the corporal by T had been faulted in every room for 1.30 s spent walking through and 0.05 s still.
  - **The DI's line and T**: a no-shoot hit twice never asks for his head ("Plate. Head." had T aiming at him, eleven no-shoot hits); after a second contact the line remembers every man you identified who is still up, yours first; the corporal inside a room is offered Dominate; the walk in by the keys names each room's own keys and your corner's side ("S and D" into room 2, "corner right" in room 3; it said "Z and Q, corner left" everywhere); a lane door that held a kick "held. Kick it again." (never "hold it", which a lane door has no row for); "The rest of the house. Two rooms and the hall." from room 2; "Out of the door" at rungs 0 and 1 is X on T, as at the open door; "Carry on" names the room's beats and then the rooms ("in, the sector, "Clear!", the mark; then room 2, room 3 and the hall"), never a raw beat key ("2:far"); the hall's two spots read apart ("the far end", "the far end, left").
  - **Before GO and after the lane the instructor's commands lead**: at LOAD, MAKE READY and UNLOAD, SHOW CLEAR the line names the command and T does it, and no drill row is offered (T had walked a cold rifle to room 1's stack at LOAD, and topped up at UNLOAD); a full magazine is no Top up.
  - **A door the bundle asks about from the doorway** ("Door, east wall. Hold it or clear it?", the eyes ahead of the feet from rung 3) offers Hold it there, and neither the line ("No shot. Carry on.") nor a round fired moving on carries on past the question (the tier one had walked past room 2's closet on seed 1703, "That door was never held.").
  - **The bracket at a pause goes to your own corner's man first**, the #2's after (setup contact bracketed 1b, "Aim at the far right target", across his sector); the move keys in stop motion never bracket your #2 or the instructor.
  - **The plates stand clear of the next man**: beside a turner, the plaque, the row's plate and the contact's plate go to the side with no other card under them (in room 1 they covered 1b as he turned). The fault's toast is in words ("Fault: stopped in the door") and waits for a take-in's window to end, as the instructor's word does. At GO, Fire and Aim, struck with nothing bracketed, go to the panel's end, so the stack, the door and the Watches hold the first keys.
  - **The words**: the instructor's brief names the deck's gravity from where you stand ("Full G here: your kit weighs what it weighs on Earth.", 0.38 G under `&env=mars-datum`), and the card's head says "AT 1 G"; the dummy's clearance is said once (two lines on a tick before), the #2's "Clear!" once; the picker's goal names "your number two" (it showed "{two}") and fits the goal box's two lines at 1600 × 900.
  - **The spin moves a man's hole on a turner** as the wind does, never his zone or the odds (it moved only paper's holes before); **a rushed bundle reaches the stack** (its walks are the legs' profile, its hands, eyes and voice rushed: a walk scaled by time alone had stopped 40% short).
- **Every shipped scene plays as v0.0.11 to the digest**, and every save in `tests/saves/` loads; two made by v0.0.11's own code are added (the barracks at 13:00, and test-pairs at a contact's pause with a step under way: the stepper's riskiest case), each saving again byte for byte and carrying on.
- Tests: new `stepper` (6: the built macros through the new engine; a bundle's channels by rung; a contact cutting a move mid-path, its Carry on; Backspace; a save mid-bundle carried on byte for byte; the move's closed form, the private's 4.0 m stack 3.57 s), `bundle` (5), `enter-and-clear` (4: room 1 by the singles and by T, the same end state, against the sheet's table), `instinct-cqb` (4: every act a panel row, never a shot, rung 0 and 1's T at the door X), `body-stances` (4: the stances by gravity, the traction rows, moment 2's walk unchanged), `room` (7), `spin` (4), `kit-suit` (3), `scene-shoothouse` (the reference room every sheet, the lane every sheet, the brief to the card; a hundred seeds, heavy), `shoothouse-again` (the same draws on every sheet, "Again as …", saves at GO, in a bundle, in a window and in the hot wash's splash carried on byte for byte), `shoothouse-nodead` (the fuzz on 30 runs, heavy; the stalls; the safety stop), `ui-house` (the page at 1600 × 900 and 1024 × 768, heavy); `places-validate` with house 1 and its goldens re-set on purpose; `game-domains` routes V's stances and Backspace's drill stop to weapons; `game-upgrade` loads the two v0.0.11 saves; `core-browser` plays "Stack and clear" in the page, `audio-browser` hears the house, `bench-browser` gains `?bench=shoothouse`. Every shipped scene's pins hold (scene-handling, scene-range, scene-pairs, bangs-pin, scene-stress, game-walk, game-upgrade, places-validate, env-matrix). After the reviews: new `house-fixes` (16, each failing without its fix: the T player through the lane on every sheet from the corporal, the #2's wait at the door, the rushed walk, the #2's own rung and his swept room, the spin on a turner and the #2's own aim, the no-shoot's head, the commands before GO, the DI's words, the funnel, the #2's follow-up, Carry on from the aim, the pause's bracket, the call after his, the closet's question from the doorway, the brief's gravity, the panel at GO); `scene-shoothouse`'s reference room is #1's own time to his "Clear!" (§25.5.1 has no #2; the room's call with his sector printed beside it) and its press guard counts the men of your own corner (your #2's are his); `core-browser`'s house pause holds the window's frames to 25 ms and the pause's own frame to 50 (Pairs' shipped pause frame measures 30 to 37 ms on the Mac); `people-roster`'s generation ceiling is scaled by `machineK()` as the other CPU ceilings are. `bench-browser`'s house bench holds the motion to 60 fps (median 16.7 ms, p95 16.8) and a pause's own frame to 50 ms (one at 33.4 ms in 1,199). The quick suite: 963 passed in 24.1 s; the full suite in the foreground on the Mac: 1,109 passed (the house bench's first run failed on its old bound, which held a pause's own frame to 25 ms, and passes with the split bound alone, 10 of 10).

## v0.0.11 (2026-11-08)

- **Every gravity, air, sound, surface, clock and sky number in the game now comes from one environment record, and nothing changes at Mars** (your rule of 2026-11-08: "the engine needs to support any surface on any part of the system by design"; ROADMAP's row "anywhere", steps 1 to 4; TECH §1.4 "Nothing may assume Mars"; PLACES §1.2, §1.3, §3.11, §3.16; CONTRACTS §21). `src/sim/places/env.js` (`envAt`, `gravityAt`, `airAt`, `soundMsAt`, `surfaceAt`, `skyOf`, `localTime`) resolves a place from its body (`BODIES`), its site (`src/data/places/sites.js`), its area and a scene's live override, over the tables in `src/data/places/env.js` (`AIRS`, `SURFACES`, `CLOCKS`, `LIGHT_REGIMES`, `ENV_PRESETS`). G0 9.80665 m/s², R and γ live once, in `src/core/phys.js`, with the one gas law (ρ = P M / R T, c = √(γ R T / M)). Melas reads as before: 0.38 G (3.726527 m/s²); the valley's 65.9 kPa at −10 °C (ρ 0.892 kg/m³, c 321.6 m/s); a building's 66.0 kPa at 20 °C (ρ 0.802, c 339.4).
- **A place with a missing environment is now an error, not a quiet Melas.** An unknown site, area, preset, air or surface throws, and so does a gait, a stance, a fall, a body's environment, a round's trace or an audio voice read without its place's gravity and air (TECH §1.4, rule 2). Before, each module fell back to its own copy of Mars: needs.js `G_MELAS`, `FLOOR_KPA`, `GROUND_KPA`, `ROOM_C`, `OUTDOOR_C`; ballistics.js `G0_MS2`, `MARS_AIR_KG_MOL`; combat/steps.js and ui/audio/steps.js `MARS_G`; look.js `G_MS2`; range-items.js `DUST_G_MS2`; sounds.js `AIR_KPA`, `SOUND_SPEED_MS` and the literal 322s; move.js `WALK_MS`, `RUN_MS`, `STROLL_MS`; motion.js `MARS_GAITS`; range.js `KD_WALK_MS`; and `lonOf`, which returned Melas's longitude for any site. All are gone. The fixer's pass closed the last ones: the body clock's readers (cues.js `stagesOf`, `cuesOf`, `stripOf`, `wordsOf`, `pEffOf`) took Melas's longitude by default and the character sheet 0°; the page's longitude fell back to 0; a ground off its paths was regolith on any world (now its body's ground, `BODIES[body].ground`); a round's trace with no gravity flew at 0 G; and the footsteps played concrete where the surface could not be read.
- **The ballistic table's classes are the environment presets.** WEAPONS §2.1's nine classes (`ENV_CLASSES`) are now a view of `ENV_PRESETS`. Eight are v0.0.10's to the bit, and the battle-sight zero and the dope card are unchanged (200 m 6 down, 300 m 3 down, 500 m 4 up; the 25 m mark −2.7 cm). Europa's gravity is now PLACES §3.11's 0.134 G, 1.3141 m/s² (it was 1.314): its 300 m path is still +31 cm.
- **The gaits follow the place's gravity, and no gravity makes you faster than 1 G.** BODY §16.1's law is clamped at 1 G (the audit's probe had a 3 G sprint at 8.81 m/s). Above 1 G, BODY §14.1's bands slow a marine conditioned to 1 G: heavy ×0.95 from 1.15 G, very heavy ×0.85 with no sprint from 1.5 G, crushing ×0.65 with no run from 2 G, ×0.4 past 2.8 G (walk 1.38 m/s at 1.2 G, 0.58 at 3 G). Under 0.05 G a coasting deck moves at BODY §16.6's push-off speeds (0.3, 0.6, 1.2, 2.0 m/s) and is drawn floating, never stuck at 0 m/s; under 0.3 G the run is drawn as the bound (Europa: 1.97 m/s); Ceres's surface (0.029 G) is in the float band. The scenes' walkers (the coach on the pairs pad, the corpsman's run on the KD, the walk splash between lines, the late card's walk to the rack) read the same gaits at the place's gravity. At Melas nothing moves: 0.75, 1.14, 2.69 and 4.28 m/s, as in v0.0.10.
- **Getting down never takes forever.** Under 0.05 G a stance change is an orientation change, timed as the slowest fall: stand to prone 2,321 ms and kneel to prone 1,729 ms at 0 G, where v0.0.10's formula gave Infinity. At 1 G it is 901 ms; at Melas still 1,033 ms. Only getting down follows gravity this round: standing up and kneeling keep their times everywhere (rising against gravity waits on BODY and COMBAT).
- **A case, the blast's dust, the berm's dust, the steel's sparks and the head plate fall at the place's gravity, and in free fall nothing falls.** On Mars that is still 3.727 m/s² (the case reads it at 1 mm/s², `CASE_G_STEP_MS2`, and rangeLook passes the same `gMs2` to the stage), and every case lands where and when it did: 0.87 s from a standing port, 0.37 s prone, byte for byte. On a 1 G deck a case lands in 0.53 s standing and 0.23 s prone; under 0.05 G it flies 2 m and hangs at its port's height, and the dust drifts and fades instead of dividing 0 by 0. The fixer's pass made the effects end where their fall does: the blast's dust lasts until its highest mote has landed and lain 360 ms (look.js `dustMsOf`: Europa 1,742 ms, where a fixed 1,200 ms ended its prone motes in mid-air; Melas still 1,200), and the head plate lands at √(2h / g) (Europa 1.56 s, where it snapped to the ground at 1.1 s from 0.8 m up) or, in free fall, drifts off its hinge for 1.1 s and is gone, never teleported to the ground.
- **No wind on a deck.** A shot's wind and a shooter's read are 0 m/s wherever the air is not open: on a deck, in a building or in vacuum. The range, out of doors at Melas, blows as before.
- **The world's sounds take the air where you stand.** The water, tap and clippers loops, the flush and the bugle played in a building's 66.0 kPa wherever you were; out of doors they are now 65.9 kPa, 0.007 dB quieter. A step's level is the weight on the foot (+4.8 dB at 3 G) and its cadence follows the speed (4.53 foot-falls a second walking at 1 G, 3.56 at Melas); a floating body makes no footsteps. The klaxon, the range, the bang and the stress sounds hear exactly what they heard.
- **In vacuum a bang is its flash alone.** The chaos's inputs now read the live place's air (supp.js `kOf`): with none, the bang's blast, a muzzle beside you and the din pool nothing (COMBAT §5.1's `VACUUM_K`, built in 0.0.9 and wired by nothing until now), a round 0.4, and a bang deafens no one, its flash still blinding by the angle as before. Before, under `freefall-vacuum` or `europa-surface` the three bangs of `?bench=pairs-bangs` played silent yet deafened the recruit for hours, pinned him at 101 and froze him 2.55 s, and the stress course's horns and blanks put him under fire. At Melas nothing changes: in test-pairs on 48213 the same bang behind the unprotected marine is felt 43 and startles him 1.45 s. The sound conducted through your own body in vacuum (your hands, the rifle, the deck through your boots) is not built yet: in vacuum you hear nothing.
- **The clocks, the lights and the day's jobs read the site's own clock, and at Melas nothing changes.** One helper, `localTime(site, ms)`, gives the time on a site's clock: the sol at Melas, with the timeslip at 24:00, and UTC on a ship. The bay's lamps, the watch and the splash's clock plaque, the log's stamps, every wall clock and the jobs' `local@` and `week@` times all read it; on 2,000 random times they read exactly what v0.0.10 read at Melas. A ship keeps UTC: its 20:00 job falls at 20:00:00 UTC every 24 h, Melas's on the sol's 20:00 every 24 h 39 min 35 s; its watch reads 'HH:MM' over 'Thursday · 22 Mar 2351' with no timeslip, a log line is stamped '22Mar 06:12:33', and its lights are on from 06:00 to 22:00 UTC (PLACES §3.9's `normal` and `shipNight`, drawn with the day and lights-out moods until VIEWS gives a ship its own). No ship is authored yet: the tests prove it on a hull record of their own. `BODIES.mars.dayH` is now the game clock's sol to the millisecond, 24.65979 h (it said 24.6597, 0.36 s a sol short of `SOL_MS`), and sun.js reads Mars's obliquity and radius from `BODIES`.
- **The Plan of the Day and the shower's water are the site's.** A site record names its routine (`routine: 'melas'`, a row of the new `ROUTINES` in `src/data/pod.js`: reveille 05:30, 07:00 on Saturday and Sunday, taps 22:00) and its water (`water`, Melas's 3 minutes). The night's calls, the squad's reveille stand, the bay's lights, the shower and, from the fixer's pass, the DI's own hours (grooming from 05:00 to 21:50, the taps window, the evening and the night) read them; before, they were Melas's hours as constants. The hours are unchanged, and `TIMES` stays a view of them. The DI's words still say "Taps at 22:00" until a site keeps other hours.
- **The sky is the site's, and a ship has none.** `skyOf(state, site, area)` gives the sky class: Mars's open air at Melas, and `none` under a roof or on a ship, so a ship has no sun and no daylight at its windows. A sky with no rows yet (Earth, an airless moon) throws, where the light used to draw Mars's rows over any site. A `&env=` preset changes the gravity and the air, never the sky or the sun (my call): the fixer's pass made the sim say so too, where it had said `none` under a ship preset while the light drew Mars's day (`?play=barracks&env=ship-1g`: the window behind the rack still glows; the range under `ship-1g` is lit as at Melas). A scene under no sky stands on an area or a site that has none.
- **A scene can carry its own environment, and it is saved.** `state.places.live.env` holds a preset and overrides with a rev (`setLiveEnv`); it goes with the player from area to area of its site and stays behind at another. A save made under it carries on byte for byte: the sandbox under `ship-1g` walks at 1.45 m/s and takes 901 ms to go prone. The site's own environment is written as the key's absence, so every save made before it is the same bytes; the upgrade 'places:env' turns a null into that absence. From the fixer's pass an override is resolved before it is written and when a save loads, so a malformed one (a thrust with no value, an unknown air) throws and the state keeps the one it had, never one that breaks every later tick; and a set temperature is honoured (`{ air: 'martian-indoor', airC: 30 }` is 30 °C, c 345.2 m/s; `{ preset: 'ship-1g', airC: 5 }` a 5 °C deck), where the air's own set point used to win in silence. Under a preset the body is where the place says: on open ground under `ship-1g` it is indoors, as the place is, where it settled out of doors in 20 °C air.
- **Every scene is a spec that says where it stands and in what environment, and every shipped scene starts exactly where v0.0.10 started it.** `src/data/scenes.js` gives each test scene a host (`{ site: 'melas' }`) and an env (`{ preset: 'site' }`, the site's own, so nothing new is saved); the career's barracks and the sandbox become `STARTS`. newGame starts from the spec (`specOf`), moves the clock to its hour on the host's own clock (env.js `msAtLocal`), sets the scene's environment live before the fill (anything the fill computes, and the first tick, read the scene's gravity and air), and throws if the fill leaves the host's site. The scenario-to-site table `SCENARIO_SITES` is gone, and so are the five fills' own clock moves and their quiet `?? 0` longitudes. Before and after: the same ms, area, point, facing and bytes on seeds 48213 and 7, checked against v0.0.10's own code.
- **The shoot house has its place: the Wheel, at 1 G.** A new preset, `wheel`, is the 1 G training hall under Base Melas (PLACES §4.15; your rule that Martian marines train at 1 G but for basic weapons training): 1 G of spin (9.807 m/s²; radius 500 m and ω 0.1347 rad/s kept as data), the Martian indoor standard at 66 kPa and 20 °C, sound at 339.4 m/s, no weather, Melas's sol for its clock. `test-shoothouse`, "Stack and clear", stands as a stub spec at house 1 of sector 4 (`melas.wheel.s4`) under it; it is not in the Tests menu and cannot be started until the next round builds its layout (`?play=test-shoothouse` shows the title).
- **`&env=<preset>` runs any scene under any environment, and an unknown name is a toast.** For example `?play=test-handling&env=ship-1g`, or `wheel`, `earth-temperate`, `europa-surface`, `ship-coast` or `freefall-vacuum`. test-handling's 25 m line on seed 48213 under them:
  - Melas (v0.0.10's): 3.727 m/s², sound 321.6 m/s, walk 1.14 m/s, to prone 1,033 ms, a standing case lands in 0.87 s, the round falls 0.19 cm at 25 m;
  - the Wheel: 9.807 m/s², 339.4 m/s, 1.45 m/s, 901 ms, 0.53 s, 0.50 cm;
  - Earth at sea level: 9.807 m/s², 340.3 m/s, 1.45 m/s, 901 ms, 0.53 s, 0.50 cm, and 84.6 cm at 300 m in its denser air;
  - Europa: 1.314 m/s², silent, bounds at 1.97 m/s, 1,535 ms, 1.46 s, 0.07 cm;
  - vacuum and a coasting deck: float at the push-off's 0.6 m/s, 2,321 ms to prone, cases held at the port, no fall; sound 0 in vacuum and 339.4 m/s on the deck.
  No wind blows anywhere but on open air. A name that is not a preset (`?play=barracks&env=vacuum`: vacuum is an air) used to throw in newGame and leave a dark page with `__ready` true; the page now drops it and says "No environment 'vacuum': the site's own instead.", as an unknown `?play` is said.
- **The gun look samples are in the gallery to score (`./play.sh --gallery`, the guns sample): five drawings of the rifle, each on the weapon card and on the figure.** G0 is the R-7 as the game draws it (titled for v0.0.10 by the fixer's pass; it read "as v0.0.9 draws it"). G1 is the standard issue after your first reference (flat dark earth furniture on black metal). G2 is the same rifle in the Corps' black, after your decision of 2026-11-08 ("the Corps red and black", armour black); it replaces the grey-tan guess and has no red accent (my call: the Corps' red is ceremonial, and on the card red already means the fire mark and tracer). V1 is the variety carbine after your second reference (sand, a clear magazine that shows the rounds). M1 is a squad marksman's rifle, a look for the R-7M. Each shows the card in three situations (on the line, at 300 m through the scope, a stoppage with a magazine in the hand) and the figure at 16 px/m in five poses and six facings, plus the rifle alone and the close-up figure. No gun's data changes, and the game draws the R-7 as before until you pick one.
- **The tests run in parallel, and the quick suite is back under a minute.** `tests/run.mjs` runs the files on worker processes (the cores less two), the longest first, the browser files on ports of their own claimed by lock files (8240 to 8249), the frame-time files two at a time once the machine is all but quiet, and the CPU-time ceilings first, alone (TECH §13.7). The quick suite's limit is 60 s again (90 in v0.0.10); in macOS's background band it is scaled by the machine's measured slowness, as the CPU ceilings are. From the fixer's pass: what a worker writes to its stderr pipe prints where it happened, a worker that dies counts the tests it had passed and keeps its file's time for the next run's order, and a filtered run claims a browser port and prints the quiet lanes' header only when a browser file may hold a test it keeps.
- Tests: the parallel runner keeps to half the cores (7 here: the quick suite 19.9 s) and to 3 workers while the play copy answers on :8200 (your question of 2026-11-08 on the dozen node processes in Activity Monitor; `JOBS` sets it). new `env` (5: Melas resolves to v0.0.10's numbers; the classes, the range, the case, the body's environment, the stance times (1,033 and 819 ms) and the gaits (1.14, 2.69, 0.75 m/s) are v0.0.10's; With nothing facing you it is a fixed row on the first page; with a threat up it is paged and marked "! he's up", never struck. Begun 3 s after the last input it sheds 8.00 points more than waiting (6.00 begun inside the 1.5 s grace); a sighting or a bang cuts it at its count's end ("Breathed 2.0 s, cut short."). What it shed is the pool under the same run without it, kept through any input that lands while it runs (the code review: read at its end alone, a blank 2 s in said "shed 1" where it took 4.96 off, a bang "shed 0" where it took 2.80; now 4.96 and 2.80, the gap carried × (1 − pts / 120) through the input's saturating add; the block's `breatheShedPts` while it runs); a Breathe that shed nothing says "cut before it shed" on the card, not "0.0 shed". Out of the mode it is a live step that counts as out of it and still, so a breath in the standby keeps the turn armed. Its running count leads the line ("Breathe · IN · hold · out · hold"), a slow box breath plays on the body's sound, the toast says "Breathed 4.0 s." and J the whole line ("Breathed 4.0 s: the chaos 75 → 59."). ## v0.0.9 (2026-11-08)

- **The Tests menu has "Three bangs"** (`?play=test-pairs&setup=bangs&tier=…`, scored apart as `test-pairs-bangs`; ROADMAP §4.2's 0.0.7c row, its first patch, shipped as v0.0.9; COMBAT §5, §25.18.3; the build sheet `docs/build/0.0.9.md`), your anchor of 2026-11-07: "Tier one operator would have such hardening, that having three flashbangs go off right next to him would barely affect his ability to operate his weapon, where a recruit would likely be so supressed that they would be even likely to fail simple actions like thumbing safety off or reloading." It is Pairs as built, with one change: in the first string's standby the coach throws three training bangs 1.5 m behind your point, 1 s apart (the first 1.5 to 3.0 s into the armed standby, drawn on `pairs:bang`; each spread ±0.5 m across the point by hash), you in utilities with your ears bare (the issue's ear defenders are taken off on this setup only), and the target turns 2 s after the third. His brief says "noise", never "Bang" (that would forewarn you). The picker opens on the Boot recruit, each sheet ending in its hardening word (green, green, steady, hardened, unshakeable); "Again as …" every sheet on the same seed hears the same bangs at the same times and sees the same turners. Scored on one aspect, `bangs`: "OLYMPUS v0.0.9 · test-pairs-bangs · seed … · bangs … · ★ … · note: …". `&setup=bangs:standby` starts in the banged standby.
  - **Played by its keys at 16 px/m** (headless Chrome at 1600 × 900, from the title: Tests, "Three bangs", the picker, Enter; the pairs bot choosing and the keys pressing; on the menu's seed 1214592378): the boot recruit froze 2.40 s in all, his picture closed to pinned, W, X, G and 1 pressed in the startle did nothing (moved 0 m, stop motion still off); at the turn "Contact · 1.25 s." (0.65 calm), Off safe "1.0 s 50% ! shaking", its first try failed ("OFF SAFE ✗"), and his first round came 4.60 s after the turn against 2.60 s calm by the book. "Again as Tier one" from the card, same seed: one tick frozen ("FROZE 0.05 S"), nothing on the picture, his Pair at 95% 95% as calm, first round 0.72 s after the turn against 0.70 s calm. Both ran to the card with no errors. On seed 48213 at 1024 × 768, by the URL: the recruit froze 2.55 s, took in 1.25 s, his first Off safe went through at 50% and his first round came 5.20 s after the turn (2.60 calm); his Off safe fell onto the sling later in the course at felt 66; "Again as Tier one": 0.05 s frozen, first round 0.72 s (0.70 calm); the page's runs are node's to the tick.
- **Three bangs reach the hands through one number, and hardening takes them off it** (new `src/sim/combat/supp.js`, the one home of the chaos, checked as `SUPP_HOME`; COMBAT §5 as designed on 2026-11-07, your words: "suppression should be a roll modifier that picks up on those factors, with "hardening" ... being an offset to it"). Every input (a bang, and built for later scenes: the crack of a round past you, a subsonic, an impact, a muzzle, shots in the room, a kicked door, a blast, being hit, a friend hit close by) adds points to a pool of 0 to 120 that saturates, scaled by readiness (prepared ×0.25, forewarned ×0.6), ear protection (×0.6, never on the crack), vacuum and blanks; it drains at 2 a second after 1.5 s, in closed form, so lived and skipped come out equal. Felt = the pool − your hardening, 0 to 100, in four bands (calm, under fire from 20, suppressed from 45, pinned from 70). It costs −1 per 10 felt on every hands-and-eyes roll (Weapon handling, Marksmanship, and their kind), half on head-and-voice (Awareness, Tactics, Leadership …), none on Fitness, at most −10, as one term in the list; and it slows handling, the aim, the press and a row's splits by ×(1 + felt / 200). Three bangs 1.5 m behind you, ears bare: 60 → 90 → 105 points.
  - **The anchors as built** (`anchors`, 4,000 trials a cell through the real roller; turn to first round from the race model, 20,000 runs): boot recruit (hardening 7) felt 98, pinned, −10, startled 2.45 s in all, take-in 650 → 1,250 ms, Off safe failing 5% → 57% (31% onto the sling), RELOAD failing 27% → 93% (45% dropping the magazine), turn to first round 2.72 → 6.98 s (p95 14.57); private (17) felt 88, −9, 2.21 s, Off safe 5% → 20%, RELOAD 15% → 57%, 2.02 → 3.39 s; corporal (36) felt 69, −7, 1.57 s, 1.24 → 1.89 s; sergeant (45) felt 60, −6, 1.19 s, 1.06 → 1.65 s; Raider (75) felt 30, −3, 0.12 s, 0.74 → 1.01 s; tier one (95) felt 10, −1, one tick (0.05 s), take-in 50 → 50 ms, Off safe 5% → 5%, RELOAD 15% → 15%, 0.60 → 0.61 s. The rates are the same at the range's 'evaluated' as at 'danger'.
  - **In the scene** (`scene-bangs`, the pairs bot, calm compared by the book): on seed 48213 the recruit's felt goes 53 → 83 → 98 at the three bangs, he freezes 2.55 s, takes in 1.25 s (0.65 calm), fails Off safe and fires his first round 4.60 s after the turn (2.60 calm); the tier one peaks at felt 10, freezes 0.05 s and fires at 0.72 s (0.70 calm). Over 40 seeds the first round after the turn against calm by the book: boot recruit 6.66 s (×2.15, p95 10.95), private 4.11 s (×1.62), sergeant 2.12 s (×1.55), Raider 1.31 s (×1.38), tier one 0.94 s (×1.18, his few failed identifications a die that fails calm too; without them within 0.05 s of calm on every seed). The recruit's Off safe failed 37% of its tries (at least once on 38% of seeds) over the whole course: the 98 has drained a little by his tries.
- **Hardening is your experience, your training and your nerve, less what the body takes off** (COMBAT §5.6, SKILLS §6.8; your answer of 2026-11-07: the name Hardening, shown in words): experience round(Warfighting × 0.75 for a soldier), inoculation (the test sheets carry a fixed one: recruit 2, private 6, corporal 10, sergeant 12, Raider 22, tier one 25), the nerve trait ×3, less the toll (exhausted 10, frayed 5, pain 5, shock 10, morale very low 5; at most 25), 0 to 100. The test sheets come out at 7, 17, 36, 45, 75 and 95. C's row reads "Hardening · green · inoculation 2 of 6: as far as boot camp goes", the number and its parts only on the hover ("Hardening 7: experience 5, training 2"); the words run green, settled, steady, hardened, unshakeable. Felt is never a number on the HUD: the roll's hover and J carry the sum ("the chaos 105 − your hardening 7 = felt 98: pinned, −10").
- **The startle takes the keys for 0.05 s (tier one) to 2.45 s (recruit), then the dice decide** (COMBAT §5.7; your answer: the startle takes the keys). A jump of 10 felt or more, unprepared, freezes you for 1.5 to 3.0 s × (1 − hardening / 100)², halved for each bang within 10 s of the one before, at least a tick; every key but Space and Esc is refused (a key that went down in it does nothing on its way up), your feet stop, a command under way halts at its sub-step's end, a stop-motion hold is let go and given back at its end, and a turn seen during it is taken in from its end. Startles within 1 s of each other join one freeze (`STARTLE_JOIN_MS` 1,000, my call), and "FROZE 2.45 S" in amber beside your head says the frozen time in all.
- **A row the chaos holds under 70% is marked "! shaking"** ("1 Off safe · 1.0 s · 50% ! shaking"), never struck (pillar 3: you choose what to try); its hover gives the sum. A failed single step now shows on the weapon card for 3 s with its marks ("Off safe ✗"): Off safe is one sub-step, so the running view had no ticks to show it failing.
- **The bang blinds, dazzles and deafens by physics, not by hardening** (COMBAT §11.4, BODY §17.3): facing the burst (within 60°) blind for 5 s, side on dazzled for 2 s (−3 to sight), back to it nothing; an unprotected ear within 5 m is deafened for 1 to 6 hours of game time, a voice within 2 m still heard. On the bay your back is to the bangs, so you are deafened, not blinded.
- **The bangs are seen and heard**: the coach's throw on a low arc, the body lying on the pad (drawn 3 × 2 px, larger than life), a white flash 1.2 m in radius for 30 ms on the first frame at or after its moment (a flag the fog of awareness never dims), a 4 m pool of light, the smoke growing from 0.3 to 1.5 m over 1.5 s, drifting with the bay's wind and gone by 8 s; the report, its pressure thump and the berm's echo; then the world muffled to 1.5 kHz and −18 dB under a 6 kHz whine, loud for 20 s and falling over 60 s, captioned "[a high whine]". The picture closes by felt's band (corners 6 / 14 / 24% darker for under fire, suppressed and pinned, the edge dithered from suppressed, the colour drained 20% when pinned, easing over 0.6 s), the startle holds it 12% dark after one pale frame, and the world's sound ducks −3 / −6 / −12 dB, cut at 6 / 4 / 2.5 kHz, breath and heart raised. A tier one at felt 10 sees and hears none of the band's. `&flashes=reduced`, the Esc menu's "Reduce flashes" (or the `olympus.flashes` key) turns the white into a 60% grey veil and drops the pale frame.
- **The banged paper and the card say what the chaos did**: the paper reads "BANGS 3 · FROZE 2.40 S · TURN TO PAUSE 1.25 · PAUSE TO FIRST 3.25 · OFF SAFE ✗ · SPLITS 0.70 · 1 A, 1 C" (Off safe's tries ticked, a failed one in red); the end card leads with "Boot recruit · three bangs · the chaos 105 − hardening 7 (green) = felt 98, pinned · froze 2.55 s · Off safe 3 tries, 2 failed · first round 7.10 s after you saw him (calm 2.50 s)" (and "Your calm run on this seed: …" when there is one). Every calm paper is unchanged to the character.
- **The DI after the bangs**: silent while you are startled; then "You flinched. Everybody does. Your lane." for up to 3 s; at the pause, suppressed or pinned, on safe, below rung 2, "Thumb it off." (T does Off safe, never the shot; "Again. Thumb it off." after a failed try, "Rifle up. Then thumb it off." with the rifle on its sling), and pairs-contact's own Off safe stands down so the line never says it twice; in the next standby "Ears ringing. Watch your lane."
- **WEAPONS' flat 'fire' −3 is retired** (`PRESSURE_MODS` is gone; it was never applied, as no caller passed 'fire'): the chaos is the one term, through the one roller. Pressure stays 'evaluated' on the range. `timeK` is exactly 1 with no chaos, so test-handling (100/100/100), test-range and calm test-pairs play to the tick as v0.0.8 (their bots on seeds 48213, 7 and 99; test-pairs' five sheets' papers pinned to v0.0.8's), and a v0.0.8 save's panel, lists, times and plaque digest are v0.0.8's own.
- **COMBAT §25.5.5's two disputed cells are settled in the helpers' favour** (`combat-race`, 4,000 seeded runs a cell on the built helpers): the tier one against an insurgent aimed at the door wins 53%, not 100% (in the room he is clearing the rifle's sight ×1.2 and turn ×0.8 put him at 0.760 s against 0.750 s; the printed 100% left them out), and the sergeant against an aimed UN marine 47%, not 3% (0.535 s against 0.550 s; rounding his aim up to whole ticks gave the 3%). No helper changed. Your overrule of question 6 is read in the race: the UN's line infantry, MSS tactical and enemy special forces wear combat EVA suits (a filtered visor, sound filtering ×0.6, never blinded or deafened), so the bang buys nothing against them (the private by the keys against an aimed UN marine 0 → 0%), and its window belongs to entries against the unsuited (against an aimed insurgent 0 → 100%). On the range in utilities none of it applies; the suits, night vision and power armour are not built yet.
- **The bench `?bench=pairs-bangs`** (the banged standby on seed 48213, 20 s, an "at a bang" row): median 16.7 ms, p95 16.8, the 67 frames at a bang peaking at 16.8 ms, nothing over 25 ms at a bang (the integrator's run in the whole suite: one frame of 1,199 at 33 ms away from the bangs; the face's own run had none over 16.8; Apple M5 Pro).
- **Saves**: a combat block on your actor (the pool, its time and rate, the startle, the bangs, blindness, dazzle and deafness), the test sheets' inoculation and the session's bangs are plain saved data, added with no upgrade: a v0.0.8 save loads as it stands (no block reads calm). A save made a tick into a startle, in its middle, after it, at the banged pause and mid-pool loads and carries on byte for byte, in node and in the page.
- **The places golden 'places: PAIRS' is re-set on purpose** for `PAIRS.bang` (the bangs' authored placement): 1e36cce2a670a5 → 0957dd557b1ff6. No other golden moved.
- **To the head is in the panel whenever the coach or the DI says he is still up, even when you stand back in the 10 m box** (your report, 2026-11-07: "am i supposed to aim for the head ? How do I do that ?"). On the armoured string a shooter 0.6 m or more behind the 10 m point (10.6 to 11.4 m from the turner) lost both head rows: they reached only 10 m and were compared on the rounded range. Meanwhile the coach said "Still up! That rang: he is wearing a plate." and the DI said "Head." over a panel of Pair, Fire and Hold. A row's reach is now judged by the line: your own lane's turner counts at your line's 7 or 10 m anywhere in that line's box. Other targets use their measured range, no longer rounded, so a neighbour's turner at 7.30 m is now out of the Hammer where rounding had let it in. The odds stay at the true range: the private's To the head at 11.3 m is 0.55 s, 95%.
- **A rifle's head shot reaches 25 m and the failure drill 15 m (both were 10).** Hammer and Triple stay at 7 m, as close-range drills.
  - To the head at 10 / 15 / 25 m: boot recruit 70 / 55 / 35%, private 95 / 85 / 65%, corporal 95 / 95 / 90%, sergeant and above 95%.
  - The failure drill's head round at 15 m: recruit 45%, private 80%, the rest 95%.
- **After "He's still up" To the head is never missing: it is offered first, or struck with its reason and the step that lifts it, and the DI names both keys.** The reasons: "aim first", "off safe first", "empty, RELOAD first", "a stoppage", "chamber unknown", "chamber empty", or "N m, past 25". The remedy is brought up beside the struck row, on the first page: Off safe, RELOAD, IMMEDIATE ACTION, Press check, or MAKE READY (else Bolt release, else Charge). The DI's line was "Head." and now reads, for example, "Still standing. To the head: 1." or "To the head (1) is struck: a stoppage. IMMEDIATE ACTION: 2.", and T does the remedy. Before, a rifle that couldn't fire dropped every shot row. To the head also keeps its key on every page: pressing 0 and then 1 still fires it, where page 2's 1 was Finger off.
- **The DI no longer calls for a row the panel doesn't have, and T always does the step it names.** A fuzz shooter over the whole course found 169 lines with no matching row before this fix:
  - "Empty chamber. Charge it.", "MAKE READY" (the bolt locked back on a loaded magazine) and "Press check" (the bolt locked) now name Bolt release or Charge.
  - "Reach for one" with the rifle slung is now "Rifle up first".
  - With no Top up in the panel, "Top up with the short one" names seat it, stow the other one, magazine out to a pouch, or the reach (chest pouch 3, or at your feet).
  - With nothing in the pouches, "Empty. Reload." names seat it, the empty out, or the reach.
  - Dry with no rounds anywhere, the DI says "Dry, and nothing to load" and names no row.
  - "Challenge" no longer comes under "To the head" once you have fired on him.
- **A thumb that slips on the selector leaves the lever where it was** (your words of 2026-11-07, "likely to fail simple actions like thumbing safety off"; WEAPONS §5.4; the three reviews of v0.0.9): a biting 'miss' on Off safe costs its slow time (×1.6) and halts the chain, says "Your thumb slipped off the lever: still on safe.", and the rifle stays on safe; before, it went to semi anyway, so the paper's "OFF SAFE ✗" and the card's "1 failed" stood beside "Off safe: semi." and a red SEMI. Now every failed try leaves it on safe (a 'miss' 26%, the rifle onto its sling 31%: the boot recruit pinned fails 57% a try, as the anchors had it), and the DI's "Again. Thumb it off." means it. In the scene (the pairs bot, 8 seeds): 14 tries, 6 failed (43%), at least once on 5 of 8 seeds (63%; 38% before). Calm test-pairs and test-handling move by nothing (their pinned runs equal to the tick).
- **The startle is seen as your marine's**: he ducks a pixel and trembles a pixel side to side while it lasts, the FROZE stamp counts up beside his head from its first frame (it showed only once the freeze was over), a key pressed in it pulses the stamp, and the DI's line goes on the bang's own tick (it stood up to a second into the startle: "Let it come."). Startles joined within 1 s show one FROZE, after the last bang, with the whole freeze (it read "FROZE 1.90 S", then "FROZE 2.40 S"), and the DI's "You flinched." waits for the series too.
- **The DI's "You flinched. Everybody does." is for a real flinch**: never for a freeze under 0.3 s (the tier one's one tick, the Raider's 0.15 s: it told the tier one he flinched like a first-timer), "the first time" only for a green or settled hardening ("Flinched. Your lane." for the steadier).
- **A bang stops what runs at the end of its sub-step**: a Pair, a Hammer or To the head is cut there (its rest offered again from the panel), a command halts with its rest as "Carry on", in live motion too, so no round leaves the rifle while the keys are taken (a Pair's two rounds went 0.10 and 0.50 s into a 1.70 s startle). And a take-in held by a startle reports the sighting to the pause, the startle named in its sum: "Contact · 1.40 s. Taken in in 1.40 s: 0.54 (Warfighting 15) × 1 (your sector) + 0.85 (startled)" (it said the planned 0.55 s).
- **One quantity, one number on the bangs card**: its first round is now from the sighting, the paper's TURN TO PAUSE + PAUSE TO FIRST (7.10 s on 48213, where the card said 7.20 s "after the turn" beside a paper summing to 7.10), against the book's same sum (2.50 s). The card's line gives the chaos less the hardening for every sheet ("the chaos 105 − hardening 95 (unshakeable) = felt 10, calm"); from the second sheet on the same seed the session's list gives each sheet's bangs line, the one just played marked and not said twice above; "Again as …" leads with the hardening ("unshakeable · seed 48213"); the calm line only for your own calm run on the seed. The tier one's banged paper says "OFF SAFE IN THE AIM" (from rung 2 the row's aim takes the safe off), so it has the step the recruit fails.
- **"End the scene: to its card" in the Esc menu of a test scene** (every "Again as …" cost the whole five-string course for about 15 s of scored play: 231 s for the recruit on 31337), and **"Reduce flashes: on | off"** there too (only the URL had it). The picker labels and leads with the hardening on "Three bangs" ("Hardening: green", its number and parts on the hover: "Hardening 7: experience 5, training 2").
- **The bang's smoke no longer hides the shooter or the coach, nor lies across the line**: over a standing figure it is a checker's half, so the figure shows through; carried off the pad by the wind it thins from 1 m of drift and is gone by 2.5 m. The thrown body has an ink outline in flight (5 × 4 px) so the throw reads. The smoke has a draw of its own, written at most once a tick of game time and never while the clock stands: the uploads with the clock held went from 453 KB a frame to nothing (the coach's arm still has no throwing pose: it needs a frame on the figures' atlas, left for the figures' round).
- **The weapon card names every step** ("MAKE READY ◦◦✗◦", not "macro:ready ◦◦✗◦", after a load, an "Again" or T's step), and the radio box keeps the coach's standing command once, as his own speech beside you ("One target. Standby." stood twice, as the TOWER's and his, from v0.0.8).
- **The DI and the pairs bot are never stuck between strings**: an empty magazine seated with the bolt home now has the DI's "That magazine is empty. Swap it." (Top up, else the magazine out); a count not known in the lull has "Not sure what is left in it. Top up." (the lull waited on a Top up nobody named, and faulted the shooter slow). The bot follows them, reaches for the short magazine dropped at its feet, carries on a halted UNLOAD, SHOW CLEAR and brings the rifle up when it fell onto its sling in the clear; the review's seeds (31337, 1, 10, 17) reach the card with the mode entered 8 times where it was 125 (seed 31337: two faults and "no reload when dry" before). Only the dice's own faults remain (a negligent discharge on the recruit's thumb under the bangs, on about a quarter of the seeds: the ladder's, since v0.0.5).
- **The muzzle habit's flat +2 "under fire" is retired** (`FIRE_DC`; one cause, one entry: the chaos reaches the habit through the roller's felt term). No caller passed 'fire', so nothing moves.

## v0.0.8 (2026-11-07)

- **The Tests menu has "Pairs"** (`?play=test-pairs&tier=recruit|private|sgt|raider|tierone`, `cpl` by the URL only; ROADMAP §4.2's 0.0.7b row, shipped as v0.0.8; COMBAT §25.10.3; the build sheet `docs/build/0.0.8.md`), the first piece of your close-quarters idea of 2026-11-07 ("a recruit marine would have to do everything step by step while an experience Tier 1 Operator would have "automations"… sub .1s for tier 1 operator vs maybe 0.8+ for recruit between seeing enemy and game pausing… double and triple tap (chest, mozambique), combat reloads etc as special actions when relevant"). The 25 m bay of v0.0.5 gains a turning target 1 m in front of each frame and a 7 m and a 10 m line painted on a new pad; the R-7 comes with magazines of 10, 10 and 2 in chest pouches 1 to 3, and a generated coach runs the bay with a shot timer. Five strings: one turns at 7 m while you wait out of stop motion at low ready; two together at 7 m; three at 10 m, one a no-shoot; the armoured silhouette at 10 m; two at 10 m on the two-round magazine, which runs dry; then a lull. A sheet picker opens first (Boot recruit, Private, Sergeant, Raider, Tier one; Private focused) and the end card offers "Again as …" each other sheet on the same seed: the same targets turn at the same gaps, since each string's turners, faces and gaps are drawn from the seed alone (`pairs:pick`, `pairs:gap`), never the sheet. Scored on three aspects: "OLYMPUS v0.0.8 · test-pairs · seed … · turn … · shots … · gun … · ★ … · note: …". Scenes never write a career.
  - **The end card** reads "Private · 5 strings · take-in 0.55 to 1.25 s · 14 A, 1 ring, 1 head down, 4 miss · no-shoot untouched · RELOAD 2.55 s · topped up" (the pairs bot, seed 48213), and the paper after each string is the coach's shot timer: "TURN TO PAUSE 0.55 · PAUSE TO FIRST 1.50 · SPLITS 0.40 · 2 A".
  - **Played by its keys at 16 px/m** (headless Chrome, 1600 × 900 and 1024 × 768, the pairs bot choosing and the keys pressing: G, X, Z Q S D, D for the brackets, the digits): the boot recruit in 127 s of real time (98 keys) and, from its end card's "Again as Tier one" (key 7), the tier one on the same seed in 68 s (76 keys); the private at 1024 × 768 in 102 s. Turn to pause by string: the recruit 0.65 · 1.50 · 0.80 · 0.65 · 0.75 s (sector, alert with two, sector with three, sector, sector with two), the private 0.55 · 1.25 · 0.65 · 0.55 · 0.60, the tier one 0.05 · 0.15 · 0.05 · 0.05 · 0.05; pause to first round 1.85 s, 1.50 and 0.55. Z, X and 1 pressed in a window moved nothing (0.000 m) and left it running. Frames 16.7 ms median, 16.8 ms p95 through string 1 and at the end; `?bench=range-line` 16.7 / 16.8 ms, `range-far` 16.7 / 16.7 ms.
  - **The scene never dead-ends** (your lesson of 2026-11-07): a string not engaged ends at 20 s lived with a fault, and the coach moves the line on; entering stop motion in the standby holds the turn ("Out of it. Wait for the turn.") until you are out again, with the next drawn gap. One magazine a string is waived on this course, written in (`oneMag: false`): the dry RELOAD inside string 4 is the point.
- **A target that turns is taken in at the marine's own speed, then time stops** (new `src/sim/combat/react.js`, the one helper of the take-in; `contact.js`, the detector, the window and the pause; COMBAT §25.4). ticks(max(floor, core(Warfighting) × the expectation × the notice roll × who × how many × stance × light × body × behind) + 200 ms a suppression band), the core 30 + 700 × (1 − WF/100)² ms. The private takes 0.55 s in his sector (a target within 20° of his muzzle) and 1.05 s alert; the boot recruit 0.65 and 1.30; the sergeant 0.25 and 0.50; the Raider 0.10 and 0.20; the tier one 0.05 and 0.15 (0.25 surprised: your question 12's floor). The world runs at 1:1 through the window, every action refused and every key but Space and Esc; the untrained feet carry the walk for the body's go (0.235 s for the private, 0.228 m) and stop (your question 11's default); then the pause on the exact tick, stop motion with the bracket on the target nearest the muzzle. A step running at the window's end finishes its sub-step first (a command's rest queued as "Carry on"), and no step's end holds time inside a window. Several at once are one window (two ×1.15, three ×1.24). On seed 48213 the recruit's take-in is the tier one's ×13 in his sector (650 against 50 ms) and ×10 alert with two (1,500 against 150).
- **The take-in is visible, not lag**: hollow amber brackets open at three times the target on the sighting tick and close to it over the marine's own take-in (one colour whatever the target, so they never say what it is); at the pause the stamp "0.55 S" on the target (amber from 0.50 s) for 2.5 s, the plaque's lead "CONTACT · RIFLE · TAKEN IN 0.55 S" with a strip ticked every 0.1 s, and "Contact · 0.55 s." leading the instinct line; the factors on the plaque's hover and in J ("Taken in in 0.55 s: 0.54 (Warfighting 15) × 1 (your sector); Awareness 6 + 14 = 20 against 7, noticed.").
- **What the marine saw is said, and only that**: at the pause an Awareness check against 6 + range / 5 m for each target names a rifle, a pistol, hands empty, a phone, or "can't see his hands", never a weapon that is not there (the recruit names 78.5% at 7 m over 2,000 rolls, against the check's 80%). Hold him and Challenge ("Show me your hands!", 1.2 s, +4 to look again) lead the panel for an identified no-shoot.
- **The double and triple taps are rows chosen while time stands still** (new `src/sim/arms/specials.js`; COMBAT §25.6; your question 9's default: the real-time Fire-again tap is retired, Fire during a fire step is 'busy'): Pair (Marksmanship 8, to 25 m), Hammer (12, to 7 m), Triple (18, to 7 m), Failure drill (12, to 10 m: two to the chest, the assessment, one to the head) and To the head (after "He's still up"), each priced and with every round's odds before you press ("Pair · 0.40 + 0.52 s · 95% 95%"), each round its own roll, part, zone and hole at its own formula time. The private's Pair 0.79 s on a 0.40 s split, the corporal's Hammer 0.57 on 0.21, the tier one's Pair 0.52 on 0.26, Hammer 0.38 on 0.11, Triple 0.49, Failure drill 0.51, To the head 0.25. Below rung 2 a row is struck "off safe first" (the Off safe single brought up beside it) and "aim first"; from rung 2 the row's aim takes the safe off; a row above your Marksmanship is struck with its gate ("Hammer 12 · Triple 18 · Failure drill 12"), so the recruit sees what training buys.
- **The turners are man-targets and the armoured one is steel**: the A zone at a margin of 4 or better, C on a pass, the head on a pass, a miss into the berm; a paper threat goes down at two torso hits or one head hit and turns away 0.1 s later. The armoured silhouette's 10 mm plate rings about 91% of torso rounds at 7 m (787 m/s, 6.95 mm of penetration: 90.5% ring, 9.5% through, never down) and its head plate falls at a margin of 4 or better (the recruit 50%, the private 80%, the rest 95%): "He's still up. Head." and To the head first.

When the coach names the two-round magazine, Top up reaches for its pouch ("Top up: the two-round magazine, chest pouch 3"). RELOAD 3.27 / 2.53 / 2.13 / 1.89 / 1.45 / 1.15 s at Weapon handling 4 / 10 / 15 / 19 / 30 / 42; test-handling's panel says "Top up" where the tactical RELOAD read "RELOAD", its course to the tick.

- **The review round's fixes** (the fixer's, after the code, runtime and design reviews of the round):
  - **A key pressed by reflex as the target turns no longer takes the pause away**: for 0.35 s of real time from when the page first shows the pause (`PAUSE_GUARD_MS`, as the cards' 400 ms guard) no key acts but Space and Esc, and a key that went down during the take-in does nothing when it comes up. Before, X (the stop-motion key) pressed 150 to 250 ms after the brackets appeared landed after the tier one's 0.05 s and the Raider's 0.10 s pause and put them back into real time (the world ran 500 ms on; string 1 lost to "time" in the runtime review's runs), and digits mashed in the window chose rows the player never saw.
  - **The picture no longer tells the target's kind before the marine knows it**: every paper face is one khaki board (was olive for a threat, pale for a no-shoot, from the turn), and what it holds is painted only once this turn's take-in has identified it (`pairs.js seenFace`; PEOPLE §7.6, COMBAT §25.4.6); "can't see his hands" draws empty hands' place blank until a Challenge answers. The steel stays steel.
  - **"He's still up" only where it is**: after a torso round rang on a plate, or two torso hits and still standing (`specials.js stillUp`, `STILL_UP_TORSO` 2). One round in a paper man was "still up" (the coach 47 times in 20 private runs, the DI, To the head first in the panel): the recruit went to the head on paper and ended with 8 head hits to the tier one's 2. Now one hit leaves Pair first and the DI says "Again. Chest."; at the steel's ring the DI's line is "He's still up. Head." (was "You are on him. Now press.").
  - **The lull has something to top up with**: at the last string's end the coach hands over a fresh magazine of ten into a pouch with room (`COURSES.pairs.lullMag` 10; the issue 22 rounds, 32 once the lull is reached), and Top up is offered with the magazine believed empty and a round up (`spoutTopUp`, this course only). Before, every sheet reached the lull on seed 48213 with nothing fuller and "topped up" was met by "nothing fuller".
  - **The reloads are counted by their purpose as believed at their start**: "dry" on a bolt believed locked on an empty, "lull" once the lull is open (any reload there, RELOAD too), "topUp" inside a string, "between" otherwise. The card's "topped up" is only a lull's (it credited a Top up before string 2), and its RELOAD is string 4's dry one. The coach's "Topped up in the lull" only after one.
  - **The turn waits for a shooter who is ready and on his point**: no turn is armed while the coach's readiness lacks anything (a magazine, a round up, the safe, low ready, out of the mode, the point); the line's box stays both lines' from "Back to the ten" until you have stood on the 10 m point. A shooter the move-on left on the 7 m point was stopped "off the firing point" for a point he never left (2 of 20 recruit runs).
  - **The DI and the coach name what is lacking** (`pairs.js pairsWant`): "No magazine in. Reach for one.", "Not sure what is up the spout. Press check.", "Back on your point first.", "Rifle up." after a fumble slung it; the coach's nudge "Shooter, a magazine in." (was "on safe, low ready, out of it" to a shooter who was), and his own words for a slow line and no rounds ("I will not wait for you.", no tower on this bay). The panel's wait reads "Wait for the turn" in a standby, "Wait for the coach" otherwise.

  - **The tower's command stays in the radio box until you have done it, marked NOW** (your report from the 300 m line, 2026-11-07: "tower sometimes doesn't say anything but we're expected to do it ?"; your 200 m screenshot showed only the coach's wind call). Now the command you still have to answer (handling.js standingCmd: not the brief, the coach's reading of the groups or the end; none once your own readiness meets it) is read from the game itself, so it survives a loaded save, the end of a splash or a cleared box. It has a brass tint and a NOW tag. The box stays in the order things were said, newest at the bottom: the command takes the place of its own spoken line, the coach's lines after it go under it, and the tower's earlier commands (a done LOAD, an overtaken RELOAD) leave the box while it stands. Commence fire.", "Five rounds slow, five minutes. Commence fire.", "Sitting, five rounds. LOAD."; it was a bare "Commence fire."). The voices after it go last. RADIO grows from 720 × 52 to 720 × 60, three rows (the splash's outcome strip stays 52). In the 200 m string, the move-on counted whole seconds from a command given between two, so it reached 60 s just before the string's own sixty seconds ran out, and anyone who used the whole minute got a 'slow' fault (seed 99, played by the keys). And the relay's lag was counted against you. Now (range.js kdWaitOnYou) a second counts only when all of these hold: the command is yours, you are not ready for it, the relay is, no timed string is being fired, no splash or activity is under way, and no casualty holds the line. A shooter who leaves a command undone still hears "Point eleven, …" at 30 s and gets the move-on and its fault at 60 s: nothing is relaxed. The 25 m course (test-handling) is unchanged tick for tick.
