  - **The 25 m bay's numbered stakes stand on their own lane's row, 2 m behind the point, and your own is marked with a brass frame and a caret, as on the KD lines** (your report on the KD, 2026-11-07: "did call me line 5 when I'm line 10 here"; the tower verifier found the same on the bay). Each lane's number was on a board 1.3 m tall, 1.6 m behind its point and 0.9 m north of its row. On screen the number sat nearer the next lane's mat than its own: lying on lane 5, the "5" was 60 px above your mat and the "6" only 36 px below it (32 CSS px a metre). Each stake is now the KD's 0.6 m stake on its own row. Your own is marked in test-handling and at test-range's zero alike (rangeLook's new `own`; the view's range stats count its pixels as `own`). The stakes no longer block walking, as on the KD. test-handling's play is otherwise the same: its digests and the save/load run in the page are unchanged.
  - **On the KD course, a shooter the tower has moved on without is told what is still owed, in the training's order, and T does the first of it: "You are behind the line: sitting, then LOAD, then MAKE READY."** (the player verifier's finding of 0.0.8, seed 2614078355). Before, a shooter who idled out of the mode at the 200 m's "Sitting, five rounds. LOAD." got the move-on and its 'slow' fault, which is right. But then, standing with no magazine at MAKE READY, the line said only "The tower said MAKE READY.", which T could not do. A second 'slow' fault followed, and the rapid string went down not fired. Now at LOAD, MAKE READY and commence fire on a KD string, the DI's range-cmd line (instinct/range.js `owedOf`) names what each earlier command needed and you have not done: the string's position (prone, or sitting at the 200 m), a magazine in if you have rounds left, a round chambered. Then it names the command itself, and T sits, loads or makes ready. Under fire it speaks only before your first round since the command, so a reload or a stoppage drill partway through a string is never called owed. Nothing is done for you and nothing is forgiven: the fault stands, and the tower waits no longer. On that seed, a shooter pressing T was firing 9.9 s after MAKE READY, with no second fault. test-handling's lines are unchanged.
- Tests: the range fixes' new kd-tower, ui-zeroing, wind-signs and ui-far suites, render-far, view-range, instinct-range and core-browser extended, every one failing on v0.0.7 where it pins a report; the merged tree's places goldens re-set on purpose, GROUND_AREA to 1682873bc5491e, KD_RANGE to 1bf6880e66d850, RANGE_PIECES to 143eb1aef84906 (test-pairs' pad and turners with the fixes' flags and stakes). new react (§4's take-in table and COMBAT §25.4.3's two tables from the code), contact (the window, the pause on its tick, the act gate, the feet, a live step and a command at the window's end, no hold inside a window: settled to the build sheet's §2.2 and §8 item 9 at integration), react-save (saves at seen + 1, + 5 and + 9 ticks pause on the same tick, byte for byte), ident (2,000 identifications a sheet), sheets, specials (every row's time ±0.01 s, the odds against 4,000 rolls, the rounds at their formula times), reloads, steel, scene-pairs (the pairs bot to the card on five sheets and three seeds; the body floor on all 25 strings), pairs-nodead (4 quick and 30 heavy fuzz runs to the card, the longest 10.6 game minutes), pairs-again, instinct-pairs, ui-contact, ui-pairs; browser (test-pairs in the page as the private and the tier one: saves mid-take-in, at a contact's pause, between a row's rounds, in a Top up and in the dry RELOAD started again end on the straight run's digest, which is node's); game-upgrade loads two v0.0.7 saves made by v0.0.7's own code (`v0.0.7-range-confirm`, `v0.0.7-handling-hot`); syntax's REACT_HOME rule (no take-in or reaction delay defined outside react.js); steps (the tap retired), places-validate (the pairs ground; the goldens above re-set on purpose), ui-steps, ui-audio, view-range and core-browser (test-pairs by the page: the picker, the brackets, the pause on the sim's tick, the Pair, the paper, "Again as Tier one", the score line) updated. The whole suite with the heavy tests: 798 passed, none failed (1,246 s). The quick suite: 713 passed in 174 s. The review round's: specials (a magazine out strikes the Pair "one left"; still up after a ring or two torso hits, not one), scene-pairs (the reloads by purpose, the lull's fresh ten, the steel's RING and HEAD DOWN; the turners drawn from belief), pairs-nodead (the recruit's fumbles on seeds 1, 5 and 6 put right to the card; a shooter left on the 7 m point never stopped), instinct-pairs ("Again. Chest.", the steel's line, "Not him", pairs-live-out, pairs-ready's magazine and the coach's nudge), ui-pairs (the recruit's first page at the pause), core-browser (heavy: X 150 ms after the brackets as the tier one and the Raider, and a digit down in the window, leave the pause standing).

## v0.0.7 (2026-11-07)

- **The Tests menu has "The line at 300 m"** (`?play=test-range`, ROADMAP §4.2's 0.0.7 row; the build sheet `docs/build/0.0.7.md`): the KD range outside the East gate, zero at 25 m, confirm at 300 m with the pits marking every shot, a ten-round string sitting at 200 m with a magazine change inside it, the coach's dummy round, the relay firing beside you, the brass policed, the rounds turned in, "No brass, no ammo", the end card ("Clear at 10:27 · zero in 2 groups · 300 m 16 of 25 · 200 m 40 of 45 · no faults" on seed 48213) and the score card on ROADMAP §2.2's three aspects: "OLYMPUS v… · test-range · seed … · far window … · shot at distance … · range safety … · ★ … · note: …". Played by its keys at 16 px/m, 1600 × 900 (headless Chrome, the KD bot choosing and the keys pressing: 61 keys, G, the digits, T): 27.6 game minutes in 4 min 54 s, the walks and the pits behind their splashes. The setups `&setup=confirm`, `s200` and `far` enter it part-way.
  - **The zero**: three-round groups on a zeroing card whose rings are 1 cm apart (the 25 m card's 2.5 cm left 35% of rifles unzeroed in four groups); the coach reads every group since the first as one, each hole moved by the clicks dialled since, and calls it ("Group high left. 6 down, 14 right."); the group card shows the holes, the coach's centre, an arrow to the mark and the call; the turrets move on the weapon card as you dial (Z S ↑ ↓ elevation, Shift with Q D windage, a click a key); within 1 cm the coach says "Zeroed. Leave it." and writes it down, else the next group, at most four. 500 rifles drawn N(0, 10) clicks off: all zeroed within four pooled groups (87.6% reading each group alone); seed 48213 zeroes in 2.
  - **The confirm at 300 m**: four power, the dope for 300 (3 down), the coach's wind call ("Wind from the right, about 3 metres a second. Hold half a mil right."), five rounds slow fire. Each shot is marked by the pits in front of you: the face goes down into the pits over 2 s, the puller rolls Awareness to find the hole, the spotter goes in, the face comes up with the score disk on its pole for 4 s or the red flag waved. After two marks the coach calls the correction.
  - **The pits are relay 2's, behind a 20-minute splash**, their confirm rolled shot by shot; then the 200 m line sitting (+3), LOAD, MAKE READY, ten rounds rapid in 60 s with the change from the first magazine of five to the second inside the string, no fault (the one written exception to one magazine a string; the panel no longer marks that RELOAD "! one magazine a string"), the faces marked together after CEASE FIRE.
  - **The coach's dummy** rides in the confirm's magazine or one of the 200 m's, round 2 to 4: the click, the reflex card ("Tap, rack · 1.5 s"), the coach's word after.
  - **"No brass, no ammo"**: you police your brass at each line (kneeling at the point, the cases kept on 1 m tiles, 4 s for four), hand the unused rounds back to the coach, and declare; brass policed is compared with rounds fired, rounds held with none, and a false declaration is a fault the coach puts right. T does each of them now (the DI's line carries its act), as do their rows on the panel.
  - **Never a dead end**: 44 fuzz runs of random legal steps all reach the card (the longest 44.7 game minutes); v0.0.6's dry check and 60 s move-on hold on every command, and the move-on polices, takes the rounds and declares for you, a fault each.
- **The round flies Melas's thin, cold air** (new `src/sim/arms/ballistics.js`, WEAPONS §2): a G7 point mass in all nine environment classes, the flight tables built once a calibre, load and barrel (a lookup 0.09 µs). The R-7 at 300 m on the valley floor (65.9 kPa, −10 °C, 0.38 g): 657 m/s, 1,684 J, 0.42 s, 3.7 cm of drift per m/s of crosswind; MCRN air 667 m/s, Earth 607, Europa 790; every cell of WEAPONS §2.4's 100 to 600 m table within 1 m/s and 1 cm; supersonic to 1,295 m (WEAPONS 1,250).
- **Every rifle has its own zero, dialled in clicks of 0.1 mrad**: drawn N(0, 10) clicks off at issue (at most 25), never shown; the battle-sight zero is 400 m on the valley floor; the dope card reads 200 m 6 down, 300 m 3 down, 500 m 4 up; the 25 m mark sits 2.7 cm under the aim box. The weapon card shows the turrets from the zero you believe ("▼3"), 4×, the line's dope ticked when on it or "▼3 TO GO", and the hold on a small reticle; a v0.0.6 rifle reads as zeroed at 1× and saves byte for byte.
- **The wind blows down the range in gusts the flags show** (new `src/sim/arms/wind.js`): a closed form of the seed, the sol, the time and the place (the day's mean 2 to 6 m/s at 10:00, a drift, a veer, three gusts travelling with it), so a lived and a skipped string feel one wind and nothing saves. Read the flags (1.6 s) and you get the steady wind times (1 ± up to 1 − windCorr): mean error 52% at Marksmanship 6, 43% at 10, 26% at 18; the coach's call 33% at 10. A hold is where the point of aim moves: a wind from the left is held left.
- **On a KD face the hole lands where the round went**: the d20 against its DC sets the spread as tradecraft's does; the zero, the path, the wind and the hold move it in centimetres; the ring is scored where it lands. The plaque gives the chance of a five or a V on the strike you believe ("FIVE 73% · 300 M"; it was the chance of a scoring hit, "PAPER 94%", which sat at 94 to 96% held or not, on the dope or off it: the design review's), with a plate of its terms in glyphs (range, the wind as read or as the coach called it in his whole metres a second, "3 M/S >" where it showed "2.6", the hold, the turrets off your zero), the call's age in the hover ("the coach's call 40 s ago"), and a strip of the rings' odds; the log says the roll and your own terms ("Marksmanship 33 against 20 · away: the wind as called, 4 m/s from the right, held 0.5 mil right."), never where it went nor the true wind, which it gave seconds before the pits' disk ("a V, dead centre: wind 1.6 m/s from the right (the coach called 2.0)": the reviews'); the pits and the coach tell you the shot. Marksmanship 10 prone at 300 m at 4× in still air: 4.50 points a shot; a 4 m/s crosswind uncorrected 3.34; held for your own read 4.13, for the coach's call 4.34. The 25 m card of test-handling keeps v0.0.5's hole by margin, byte for byte.
- **The far window opens on your target at 300 m** (new `src/render/far.js`): the stage again at 16 px/m in a round window at the frame's edge along the bearing (radius 198 CSS px at 1600 × 900, 126 at 1024 × 768), labelled "TARGET 8 · 300 M", the SO-4's reticle at 4× drawn offset by the hold; on aim, on bracket, or with E held on a KD line; it dissolves in over 0.2 s once its ground is built, and slides along the edge round every HUD rectangle (432 placements at four window sizes, none meeting one). Beyond perception's 54 m it sees by an open-air reach of its own (600 m by day, ×4 at 4×). The faces are drawn twice life size: the carrier, the holes, the spotter, the disk, the flag. With the window open during the 300 m string the page ran at 16.7 ms a frame (p95 16.8); +16 draw calls.
- **The KD ground stands in full beside the 25 m bay** (PLACES §4.12): the 200, 300 and 500 m lines of twenty numbered points, the faces on carriers at 1,600 m, the pits behind a 0.5 m parapet, the 8 m impact berm in four steps, wind sleeves every 100 m on both sides blowing as `flagOf` says, the shack and the ammunition canopy, the rope round the lanes (in runs of at most 30 m, so the stage draws every run in reach; one 537 m piece went undrawn wherever its middle was out of reach); the carriers' frames stand under their faces as drawn. The watch names "The 300 m line" and the others.
- **The relay is alive beside you**: five of your squad, generated, on the lanes beside yours, each firing its own strings through the same shot model (its own handling rolls on a stream of its own, kept out of your log), reading its own wind, dialling its own dope from the zero it believes, taking the second magazine in the 200 m string and clearing its stoppages; relay 2 marks in the pits. 900 relay shots over 12 seeds: none on a cold line, none forward of the line, all on safe after CEASE FIRE. On seed 48213 the five score 54 to 64 of 75 (v0.0.7's first build had them dial the dope from an unzeroed rifle and counted the zero card: 4 to 77 of 105). On the KD course you take in the firing line 12 m either way (the relay drew as figures remembered from the walk, standing, before), and they are drawn prone, sitting and aiming as they are: a rifle sheet painted for a look with none was never asked for again, and the relay stood.
- **Range safety in full** (WEAPONS §6.4, §6.6; tradecraft's `safety()`): v0.0.5's rules on the current line, plus the cold line (off safe after a 6 s grace), forward of the line while hot (the stop), the muzzle over a neighbour (a warning), and the tower's CEASE FIRE at once on any negligent discharge. An ND now lands somewhere real (new `src/sim/arms/penetrate.js`, tradecraft's armour.js adapted): the berm, the ground, the shooter's own foot (10.6% of NDs while bringing the rifle up or down), a neighbour (ND-4), a death (ND-5). Of 1,000 rounds under the berm's crest none got past it; the pits' pullers stand 2.5 m down under the parapet's shield (a round 1 m over the trench had hit a puller's legs).
- **A round that finds a person wounds them, as tradecraft's did** (new `src/sim/body/wounds.js`, `blood.js`; BODY §17): wounds by part, bleeding, pain, the knock, shock, unconsciousness and death, with BODY's changes (a limb wound not in an artery bleeds at most 8 %/min; the player is knocked down too; cold blood bleeds ×1.25 under 35 °C, ×1.5 under 33). An untreated thigh wound at 7.2 %/min collapses at 6 min 15 s and kills at 7 min 38 s, lived, stepped or skipped to the same second. A wound slows the walker and costs the rolls (a broken arm −6 on Weapon handling); the DI says it first ("Bleeding hard from the left thigh: a tourniquet, now."), C lists it in words, the picture dims as a casualty fades. Blood on the ground: a spatter where a round strikes, drips under the bleeding, a pool under someone lying still.

The tower runs this line." is no longer the idle line on the KD (the reviews').

I'm okay with no time skip for actions taking up to 10s"). `LIVE_MAX_MS` 45 s to 10 s, 10 s itself still lived. Washing your hands, a drink at a tap or the fountain, and a bar from the machine (30 s each) took 30 s of your time; each is now a fast-forward of its lived ticks behind the brief splash, about 0.65 s (600 ticks at 40 a frame, then the 0.4 s strip), the body and the clock landing on the same second as before; so is the KD's brass call when it runs over 10 s. The price says ", done HH:MM" from a minute (`DONE_FROM_MS` 60 s, my call: it was tied to the 45 s line, and on 30 s it rounds nothing), so R's plate still reads "R WASH HANDS · 30 S".

## v0.0.6 (2026-11-07)

- **R says what it does before you press it, and does only that** (your ask, 2026-11-06: "the shave+wash+water when pressing R at a basin without the tootip telling us what is going to happen is no good. First of all, implement ways of combining actions and quick action needs to be the most obvious"; DESIGN.md's log, "the user on interactions"; the build sheet `docs/build/0.0.6.md`). In v0.0.4 and v0.0.5, R at a basin did teeth, a shave and the face together as "the basin · 7 min", its chip hidden on urgent lines.
  - **R's plate is on every line, urgent ones included**: the instinct line's right end carries R's keycap and its words in the game's own pixels, the 3 × 5 capitals on the plaques' dark enamel: "R WASH HANDS · 30 S", "R SHAVE · 4 MIN, DONE 20:06", "R OPEN THE DOOR", "R AGAIN: WAIT 15 MIN", and on the klaxon's line "R GET UP" (v0.0.5 hid R there, SKILLS §14.1's old exception). Amber when the action would make you late; "HOLD ▸" in brass at its right when R held offers more than the plate and R's wait (a door and the wait carry none; in review it was three brass dots on the corner, which read as decoration). Where the spot's own action is struck, the plate says why before R is held, in the struck rows' grey before the keycap: "USE THE HEAD: NOTHING TO GIVE · R WAIT 15 MIN", "HAIRCUT: THE BARBER OPENS AT 10:00". Drawn at round(2 × --ui) CSS pixels a pixel, at most three quarters of INSTINCT, the chip shortened before it. On an urgent line the DI's order comes first: the plate drops ", done …" and ", the count …" (the late shows in amber anyway) and gives the line its room, so "PT kit is not the uniform of the day. Utilities." is never cut (in review it read "PT kit is not th…" at 1600 × 900).
  - **At a sink R is one single action, the most urgent** (your ask: "R should wash hands if hygiene is low enough, drink if we're thristy and shave if none of the above (use a priority thing and R is the most urgent)"): wash hands when they are dirty (dirt 40, the hands' band 2), else drink when thirsty (a 1% deficit), else shave where stubble shows (12 h), else buzz a shaved head whose cut is due, else nothing is due and R is the wait, the plate saying "SHAVE: CLEAN-SHAVEN" before it and R held offering the rest (my call, overruling the build's own "else wash hands": a plate that always shows a chore stops being read; say if you want the hand-wash back). All sixteen cases of the grid (hands × thirst × stubble × a shaved head due) give that order.
  - **Every spot's quick action is a priority kept as data** (`ROWS`, `PRIORITY` in `src/data/activities.js`): a toilet stall the stall if the bowel could go, else the head over 50 mL in the bladder, else the wait (it had offered six minutes in a stall for nothing); a urinal the head over 50 mL (under it the row is struck "nothing to give" and R is the wait); your rack in a drill dressing into utilities, else stand fast; by night sleep until reveille, by day a 45-minute nap; the barber's pole a haircut in your own cut, struck "fresh from the barber" while your cut is at its first step (under 3 sols for a man's), as the shave is struck "clean-shaven", and the clippers likewise "freshly buzzed".
  - **", done HH:MM" shows only from 45 s**: a lived action ends in front of you ("wash hands · 30 s"), a skipped one says when it lands.
- **The basin's bundle is six single actions, and every spot offers single actions only** (your ask: "not pre-combined actions like you do now that make lists hard to read"): wash hands 30 s, brush teeth 2 min, shave 4 min, buzz your head 10 min (a shaved head only), wash face 1 min, drink 30 s. A woman's sink has no shave row. Each has its own log lines ("Soap, a scrub, a rinse: 30 s.", "A clean scrape: 4 min. An inspection would find nothing."). Every fixture was reviewed for bundles: the basin and the sleep's turn-in were the only ones. v0.0.5's basin record and the turn-in stay in the table, never offered, so a v0.0.5 save made inside them finishes as it would have.
- **R held opens a pixel-art menu of every single action at hand, with checkboxes and drop-downs, and several checked run one after another behind one splash** (your ask: "long pressing R should open the menu with all the possible interractions as single actions, with the ability to select several actions ..., implement pixel art menu drop downs or checkboxes for multiselections"). It replaces v0.0.4's choices card, whose lines were bundles.
  - **The card**: one canvas of the game's pixels on dark glass at round(3 × --ui) CSS pixels a pixel: the fixture's title plate ("THE SINK"), up to ten rows in the order one does them in ("1 ☐ WASH HANDS · 30 S"), the cursor on R's quick action, a struck row greyed with its reason and never done ("SHAVE (CLEAN-SHAVEN)"; also "buzzed today", "nothing to give", "already in PT kit", "reveille comes first", "the barber opens at 10:00", "taken"), the foot's total and end from TIME's plan ("TOTAL 7 MIN · DONE 20:09"), amber with the late's words when late, the keys line and DO IT. A choice is one drop-down on its row, not a row each: sleep until reveille, 1, 2, 4 or 8 h; a nap of 20, 45 or 90 min; PT kit or utilities; your cut. The sink's six rows measure 561 × 408 CSS px at 1280 × 720 and 935 × 680 at 1920 × 1080.
  - **Its keys are its own, by physical code**: 1 to 0 check a row and put the cursor on it (one key a row: R held, 2 3 4, Enter is the errand; in review it took a digit and Space a row), ↑ ↓ and Z S move, ← → and Q D change a row's choice, Space checks the cursor's row, Enter does the checked rows (else the cursor's, a ghost tick in its box so the total matches something you see), Esc or R closes; the keys line reads "1-0 CHECK · Z S ↑↓ · ENTER DO · ESC · Q D CHOICE"; the mouse hovers, checks, opens a drop-down and picks, presses DO IT, or closes by a click outside. Nothing reaches the world while it is up, and the game holds still (every card pauses), so its total holds.
  - **One row is sent as the single action, two or more as one chain** (new `src/sim/time/chain.js`), run in the menu's order, each step judged by what the steps before leave (a stall then the head is refused "nothing to give", the stall having emptied the bladder): brush teeth, shave and wash face checked at 20:02 are one 4.07 s splash for 7.01 minutes, three log lines and three ✓ in its strip, one toast for the errand ("Brush teeth, shave, wash face · 7 min."), the stubble 14.5 h to 0.02. The menu's wait row and its door row do what they say wherever R's quick action is something else (in review both went nowhere: the wait at a sink, the lounge's door beside its vending machine). The steps change on whole seconds at the same millisecond lived, fast-forwarded or skipped, the bodies within 0.0015 kcal of each other. A chain is checked late as a whole, the late card first; "fit it in" keeps the leading whole steps.
  - **Backspace stops a chain after the running step**: the ticker reads "Backspace stop after this step", then "stops after this step", and the shown clock decelerates to the step's end over 0.35 s. At 2.5 minutes, in the shave, the chain ended at +6 minutes, the strip "✓ … 2 min. | ✓ … 4 min. | ✗ wash face: dropped."; an interrupt cuts the running step and drops the rest. A save made mid-chain carries on byte for byte, in node (10 skipped, 91 fast-forwarded) and in the page.
- **The head no longer washes your hands, and your hands keep their own dirt** (your ask: "using the head shouldn't wash our hands either afterwards"). A ledger of 0 to 100 beside grime builds at grime's hourly rate (indoors and idle "could do with a wash" after 16.7 h, dirty after 33.3; walking outdoors 7.4 and 14.8 h; asleep 0.5 an hour) and by events: the head +40, a stall +60, a bar +10, each rifle handling step +1.5, kneeling or lying prone on regolith +15. A sink or the shower washes it to 0. The bands are clean, used (20), dirty (40: R at a sink washes them first) and grimy (70), and the words name the cause: "Dirty hands, since the head.", "Gun oil and carbon on your hands.", "Regolith ground into your palms.". For two minutes after the head or a stall the DI says "The basins. Wash your hands.", and at a sink "Wash your hands." with T to do it; the stubble line's T now shaves. Through test-handling on seed 48213 the hands reach 88, "Black with gun oil and carbon.". The doll marks dirty hands by band in the cause's colour. The day before a career soils and washes them as a marine would, so New career starts at 12 and the barracks at 19:30 at 0.3.
- **You sleep in what you wear.** v0.0.5's sleep began with 4.4 minutes of changing into PT kit, a bundle; dressing is now its own row, first at your rack, and R held chains "dress (PT kit), sleep until reveille" behind one splash (on seed 48213's first night it lands at the 02:57 klaxon, in PT kit, lying, "R GET UP" on the line). R's sleep always begins lying, so from utilities too the page lies you down in sight first (4.8 s from a lower berth). A sleep or a nap that would run past reveille is now refused by the game itself, not only left out of R's offers.
- **Every marine wears one of CAREER §2's regulation cuts, and a man whose hair is thinning shaves his head** (your ask, 2026-11-06: "this haircut is not military acceptable ... a soldier with half balding hair would 100% go fully shaved head for service"; DESIGN.md's log, "the user on hair"). v0.0.4's horseshoe 'bald', its 'crew' and a man's 'short' are gone from the data and the art. Men wear high and tight (sides 0 mm, top 13 mm at the cut), low regulation (1.5/19), medium regulation (3/32) or a shaved head (0.5), weighted 30/35/25/10; women a regulation bun, short (25/50) or a braid pinned up, weighted 55/30/15 (my calls). Thinning is drawn by hash against THIN_P by age (after Norwood 1975: 0.10 at 18, 0.16 at 25, 0.30 at 30, 0.50 at 40), so no dice stream moves: seed 48213's company has 22 of 101 men thinning, all shaved; over 60 seeds, 902 of 6,119 men (14.7%), the shaved head 24.5% of men, no other cut on a thinning man and no horseshoe anywhere. Men and women share no cut, so a squad has sixteen (skin, cut) pairs for men where v0.0.5 had twelve, and a squad that cannot be told apart is 3 in 720 (0.42%, was 1.31%). A v0.0.5 save maps its cuts on load (crew to low regulation, a man's short to medium regulation, bald to shaved).
- **Hair grows on every head and is groomed like the beard: drawn at four steps, said in words, kept in regulation by the barber or the clippers** (your ask: "we should have to groom our hair the same way we will have to groom our beards"). The hours since the cut grow every second, lived or skipped (one 8-hour step equals 28,800 one-second steps), at 0.40 mm an Earth day (Loussouarn et al. 2005; 0.411 mm a sol). A man's cut steps from fresh to growing out at 3 sols, due at 7 and out of regulation at 10; women's short is due at 21 sols, bun and braid at 42 (my calls). A new join arrives fresh: New career's player at 12:39 is 31 hours from the cut, "Fresh from the barber.". The figure, the doll and the sleeper draw each cut at its step: a man's sides go from skin to a shadow, a dark and the hair's colour, and his top stands a pixel taller out of regulation; a woman's short cut grows a row a step, a bun or a braid works stray pixels loose; under the cover a man's sides show below it. The squad keeps its cuts with a barber every 7, 8 or 9 sols, out of your sight, never out of regulation (at most 90% of due over 30 sols), and a squadmate's cut never changes in view. The DI says "Your cut is due Wednesday. The PX has a barber. Use him." as a cut comes due and "That hair would fail an inspection. The barber, today." once it is over, by day only, never in a drill, on the range, busy or lying down. PERSONNEL's Haircut row reads "High and tight · fresh from the barber", "· growing out", "Haircut due Wednesday.", "Out of regulation.", never a number.
- **The PX has a barber's pole, and R at it is a haircut**: a red, white and blue spiral in a glass tube on a steel stand by the PX's north door, 2.5 m east of the door and 4.4 m out from the wall, where the camera sees it over the PX's 4 m roof (on the wall it hid behind the roof; my call). R there: "haircut (high and tight) · 12 min, done 14:12", seated inside behind a splash, the cut chosen from the drop-down (a thinning man is offered the shaved head only); the barber works 10:00 to 20:00, and at 09:00 the row is struck "the barber opens at 10:00", the DI then giving his hours ("The barber's at the PX, 10:00 to 20:00.") and never "Haircut. Now, while you are here." for a T that would be refused. A shaved head is buzzed at a sink instead ("buzz your head · 10 min", the clippers humming).
- **PERSONNEL has a Hands row and a Haircut row**, and "Head wet." now reads for the shaved cut (v0.0.4's bald is gone); the sheet's and the inventory's dolls wear the cut as it has grown.
- **The menu clicks and the clippers hum, all made by code**: a 12 ms tick for a move, a latch's click for a check (centroid 4.1 kHz) and a lower one for an uncheck (3.7 kHz), two rising notes on DO IT (660 then 880 Hz), a falling blip on close, all on the UI bus so a splash never muffles them; the clippers loop while a head is buzzed, a 120 Hz motor's sawtooth through a 2 kHz band (centroid 2.3 kHz); the tap now also runs while the hands are washed. All my calls, unscored (VIEWS §11.5's set to come).

The "…" on R's plate had shown almost everywhere, since every menu ends in R's wait: it now counts the rows other than the wait (my call).
- **test-handling never dead-ends: a shooter with no rounds left sits the string out, and the course runs on to its end card and score card** (your report, 2026-11-07: "I shoot two prone, two kneeling and when I have to shoot standing, I don't have any mags left"; DESIGN.md's log). Your save showed why: four fired in the prone string, the magazine changed by the single steps, six more, so at "Standing. RELOAD." the three magazines lay empty on the firing point and the tower waited for ever for a loaded rifle. Now, once the tower has waited 3 s of lived time on a command that wants rounds, still or not (with nothing anywhere no reload can be under way), and no stoppage in the rifle, the coach counts what you have (the chamber, the magazine in, your hand, your pouches, the ground at your feet); none, and he says "Nothing left to load. You sit this one out, on safe." before any move-on, and a dry shooter is never faulted 'slow' (in review, your save played as a puzzled player would, reach at your feet, look, drop, moved on with a 'slow' fault before the coach said a word); the DI's reload line says "Nothing left to load. Tell your coach." instead of "Empty one out, full one in"; every string left is sat out and goes down as not fired ("That string goes down as not fired."), and the end card says so. Your own save, loaded in this build and played on by the keys, ends "Clear at 09:02 · 70 of 75 · 7 V · 1 string not fired · 2 faults", and its score card opens.
- **The tower no longer waits for ever on anything**: a command not answered in 60 s of lived time (the nudge at 30 s, as before) is passed over with a fault in the coach's words ("The tower will not wait for you. Keep up."), and at UNLOAD, SHOW CLEAR a rifle never shown is taken, cleared and shown by the coach himself, once (in review a shooter busy with steps after his "Clear." drew a 'slow' fault and "Clear." every second, 342 of them, and the line never closed). Time standing still in stop motion never counts, so choosing is never hurried. Pressing nothing at all from the brief reaches the end card at +9.4 minutes with nine faults; a shooter pressing random legal keys reached it on all 30 runs tried (20 from the brief, 5 from the hot line, 5 from your save), in 9.0 minutes of game time at most, dropping, dumping, picking up and slinging as the dice fell.
- **One magazine a string**: a magazine changed while a string is fired, before the tower's RELOAD, is a fault on the card each time, the coach saying "One magazine a string. You reload on the tower's word." the first time, and the step that would do it is marked in amber on its row, "! one magazine a string", never struck (your report: the change that emptied your pouches; REMEDIAL ACTION, which strips the rifle's own magazine and seats it again, is never marked). In v0.0.5 a change by the single steps drew nothing, and a RELOAD macro drew "Wait for the tower." (now one act, one fault). **The coach says so before the change when one is still up the spout**: a magazine check reading "0 in the magazine" with a live round chambered, or the magazine about to come out so, in a hot string, draws "You've one left up the spout. Fire it." once a string (your run began there). **A string fired with more than its rounds is remarked on, never praised**: your ten in the five-round prone string now draws "Ten rounds in a five-round string. Five a string, on the tower's word." where v0.0.5 said "All ten on the paper. Same again.", and the end card says "1 string over-fired".
- **Reach for a magazine is a step of its own, and in the hand you learn what it holds** (your ask, 2026-11-07: "putting magazine in should be a "Reach for magazine" (or equivalent) and when we have the mag we reached for, we see if it has ammo in and which kind before we decide to either put it away again, drop it or put it in"). The panel offers "Reach: chest pouch 1 (5 ball · front of 2)", "Reach: chest pouch 2 (5, kind not seen)", "Reach: the dump pouch" and "Reach: at your feet (empty · front of 3)", each saying what it would bring as you believe it ("a few by its weight" from the hand's feel): 0.69 s at Weapon handling 10 from a pouch, 1.0 s to pick one up (+0.1 dust off regolith). In the support hand its weight tells at once, none, a few (1 to 7 of 30), about half, nearly full or full, and a weight unlike the count you believed makes that count unknown and stops a drill ("From chest pouch 1. It feels empty. You thought 5 rounds."). Look at it (0.6 s, routine for the trained) reads its witness holes and its top round: "5 rounds, tracer on top: a red tip.". Then Magazine in (0.92 s), "Put back: pouch 1" (into a chest pouch with room, behind the one there, so reaching again brings the next; "Put it back: chest pouch 1" had wrapped its digit onto a line of its own at 1600 × 900), Dump it, or Drop it. The reach and the seat are v0.0.5's magazine in (1.4 s of base, DC 9) split in two, the reach rolling nothing, so LOAD (2.41 s), MAKE READY and the RELOADs (2.53, 2.99, 3.22 s) live the ticks they lived: the range bot's course on seeds 48213 and 7 is unchanged to the tick, the point and the coach's word. REMEDIAL ACTION keeps its 6.05 s.
- **LOAD and the RELOADs choose the magazine by rule**: the kind the course fires (ball) first, then a kind not known, then another, the fullest believed, never one believed empty (playing the mixed setup by the keys, "RELOAD, keep the empty" had reached for the very empty it had just dumped: fixed and pinned); a magazine already in your hand is seated with no reach. A drill's own reach says nothing unless its weight surprised it.

- **Doors swing in 0.4 s, a weather door in 0.6 s, both ways** (your report, 2026-11-07: "doors open and close much too slow"; DESIGN.md's log, "the user on doors"). In v0.0.5 an interior door took 1.0 s and the outside and vestibule doors 1.2 s, and a door you opened blocked you for all of it. The way is still freed once the leaf is fully open, so walking through a door you open now waits 0.4 s; closing, a leaf blocks at once and its picture swings shut over the same time. The numbers are each opening's `openS` and `closeS` (`OPEN_S` in `src/sim/places/echo.js`), and `DOOR_S` 0.4 s in `doors.js` for a leaf whose opening names none (the test rooms'). Freeing the way at about two thirds open, as DESIGN.md's log first had it, was not built: 0.4 s is already well under a second (my call). Played by the keys from New career: the bay's door, Head A's and the east and main stairs' doors each read fully open 400 ms of game time after R, and from R to 0.6 m past the door line, 1.15 m away, took 1.55 to 1.60 s.
- **The close-quarters design is written** (your ask, 2026-11-07: "a recruit marine would have to do everything step by step while an experience Tier 1 Operator would have "automations through pressing T""): COMBAT §25 and its pointers in the other sections, drill rungs earned by training, one take-in for you and every NPC with the world running while it lasts, special shots and reloads as rows, and the scenes `test-pairs` and `test-shoothouse`. Nothing in the game changes yet; five questions for you, each with its default, are in COMBAT §24, items 9 to 13.
- Tests: **636 in 120 suites at the release**, the last check's full run with the heavy ones in the foreground (machine factor 1.00) after the stairs and the doors: 634 passed and 2 failed in 925.6 s, both answered below; then the quick suite's 560 all pass in 31.4 s against its 60 s limit. The round as it went: 609 in 118 suites, all passing on the merged tree (891.1 s with the heavy ones, run in the foreground); the quick suite's 535 all pass in 25.9 s (machine factor 1.00). An earlier full run in macOS's background band (1,351.7 s, factor 3.3) failed places-walkers' walkFloors on one tick of 4.7 ms against its 4 ms ceiling, which machine().k does not scale (0.55 to 0.74 ms at worst in the foreground, three runs): the band's, not this round's. Extended: game-upgrade (two v0.0.5 saves made by v0.0.5's own code, inside the basin bundle at 19:32 and the sleep's turn-in at 21:57, each finishing as v0.0.5 did, cuts mapped), game-domains (every 0.0.6 fact registered and emitted; the gunless digests re-pinned for the hair and the hands), time-activities, time-review, instinct (the scripted morning: the head, "Wash your hands." and T, then the shave by T), time-determinism (the two-hour sleep 120 min, was 124.4 with the turn-in; the 8-hour sleep now from 21:30 until reveille), people-roster (thinning by age against THIN_P), places-validate (the pole's spot reachable), people-poses (a squadmate's step changes only out of sight), body-skip, figures-art, figures-frames, render-worker, ui-instinct (R's plate on the urgent line; the "…" without R's wait), ui-splash (a chain's strip, Backspace's 0.350 s deceleration), ui-sheet (Hands and Haircut at 0.5, 3, 7 and 10 sols), ui-audio, ui-scores, core-input (a card's own keys never reach the world) and core-browser (heavy: "R says what it does" after the urinal, by keys and by mouse, the card inside the window at four sizes; test-night's R held moved to the menu). view-splash's every-vignette test begins the sink's by the shave and the head's at 20:00 (it had begun v0.0.5's basin, now never begun, and a head with nothing to give), and now holds all seven (it let one pass). Kept green, their numbers read: moment-shower, moment-night (mustered 02:32:41, the dawdle back at 02:43:10.091 as in v0.0.5), scene-handling and the range bot, game-walk (quick and heavy), time-placement, view-squad, time-parity and the three benches. game-determinism's GOLDEN_SANDBOX re-set as above. The arms round after it (your 2026-11-07 report): the whole suite with the heavy ones 620 passed and 5 failed in 908.3 s in the foreground (machine factor 1.00); four were this round's own pins of the old magazine in (audio-browser's seat click, core-browser's three ticks of LOAD, view-range's magIn step, range-nodead's coach line), re-pinned and passing on rerun, and view-squad's dressing splash missed once and passed on rerun (the barracks, untouched); then the quick suite's 549 all pass in 29.4 s (factor 1.00). Updated for the new rules: time-actions (the sink's sixteenth case the wait; the barber's pole struck while fresh), time-activities, ui-kit and ui-menu (a digit checks its row), ui-instinct (HOLD ▸, the struck note, the urgent short plate), game-domains, moment-basin's routine (a stall taken from R held's menu). The stairs and the doors after the review (your 2026-11-07 reports): `places-stairs` rewritten. A walker tries every way onto each of the six stairs from 38 starting points round each, and reaches the other floor only from a flight's foot (the floor changing at z 14.55 to 14.60) or a well's landing; this fails on v0.0.5's rules. Also checked: the drawn rails, the rail across the foot and the landing's rails match what the sim blocks, for every facing, both directions and every open side. On screen the opening shows 0.94 m clear past the handrail's picture. The words sit at the far ends, upright, clear of the closed end's picture and a pixel off the guard. The arrow's head points at the word, and on a flight it comes in at the open side. A bot from the hall climbs each flight by its foot and goes down each well. Walks past each landing, walking and running, never go down (fails without the landing's rails). A walker settled near a flight's top lands outside its lane (fails with the old settle). At 23:00 the marks lie in a night light's pool. The last check (after the stairs and the doors): moment-basin failed on a chain of 420.6 s, the faster doors bringing the walk to the sink 0.4 s past a whole second, so the first step's end went to the next whole second (`ceilSec` in chain.js); its check now holds the chain from 420.0 s up to but not including 421.0 s, ending on a whole second (it had rounded to 420), and passes; view-squad's dressing splash was missed once and passed on rerun (39.9 s), as before. Played by the keys from New career at 12:39 with no page errors: the urinal "R USE THE HEAD · 1 MIN, DONE 12:41", the hands 51.8 after it; the sink's plate "R WASH HANDS · 30 S | HOLD ▸", R washing the hands alone; R held's six rows, the shave struck "clean-shaven", rows 1 and 2 checked by digit, "TOTAL 3 MIN · DONE 12:44", Enter one splash from 12:41 to 12:44, one toast "Wash hands, brush teeth · 3 min."; and test-handling on seed 48213 to its score card, "Clear at 09:01 · 48 of 70 · 3 V · no faults", frames 16.7 / 16.8 ms median and p95, 1 of 6,159 over 25 ms. view-squad's "the dressing splash was not seen", a flake seen four times in full runs (and the one that stopped this release's first attempt), was a race: T could land before the recorder's first frame, which then already showed the splash; the recorder now runs and draws a frame before T is pressed (three runs in a row, 137 to 138 frames each).

## v0.0.5 (2026-11-07)

- **The 25 m line of Base Melas's KD range is built, and "Show clear" plays on it: you, an R-7 and three magazines of five, alone on your lane, a generated coach at your shoulder and a generated range officer in the tower** (ROADMAP §4.2's 0.0.6 row, `test-handling`, cut as v0.0.5; the build sheet `docs/build/0.0.5.md`; PLACES §4.12, WEAPONS §5 to §7). Until now G only held time for a digit and there was no rifle in the game. **The place is authored** (your rule, 2026-11-05: "game world is handcrafted"), part of `melas@1` and the same in every career (`src/data/places/range.js`): a bay of six lanes 3 m apart (z 283.5 to 298.5) in X3's north strip outside the East gate, firing east, the firing line on the 500 m line's x 1,100, the cards at 1,125 m on frames 1.0 × 1.2 m (the card's centre 0.6 m up), a berm at 1,128 to 1,134 in four 0.75 m steps, a fence along the north and a range rope on the south and west, a red flag on a pole at each end of the line, and the 9 m tower 20 m behind it, its deck at 6.0 m under a half shade so the range officer stays in sight. The ground grows over X3's strip (x 1,040 to 1,680, z 260 to 440), so nothing placed now moves when the 200, 300 and 500 m lines come at 0.0.7; the four gates stay shut, and a career cannot reach the range yet. **What happens there is procedural** (the same rule: "events … are all procedural"): your lane (2 to 5), the coach's set stoppage, the tower's gaps (1.5 to 4 s once the line is ready), the words by hash, each on its named stream. **The range officer and the coach are generated** (your rule, 2026-11-05: "All people generated"), drawn for their posts on `stream('staff', seed, 'melas', post)` (a SSgt, GySgt or Sgt in the tower, a Sgt or Cpl coach), in the range staff's dress (utilities, a red brassard; the officer's headset), the roster and you unchanged by them. The coach stands at your left shoulder, 0.9 m back and 1.4 m north of your firing point, facing the line, drawn behind you so he covers nothing of you standing, kneeling or prone (the sheet's 0.6 m beside you put his back over your legs and rifle at 16 px/m; then 1.4 m behind your right shoulder put his head on your feet for the whole prone string: the design review's).
  - **The tower gives the Corps' commands when it sees the line ready**: the brief ("Range is open. Familiarisation fire, two-five metres: three strings of five, prone, kneeling, standing. The line is cold."), prone, "With a magazine of five rounds, LOAD.", MAKE READY, commence fire (the flags hoisted over 0.6 s), CEASE FIRE, "Kneeling. RELOAD.", the second string, "Standing. RELOAD.", the third, UNLOAD, SHOW CLEAR, "The line is clear." The officer reads only what he can see (a stance, a magazine in, hands still, the rifle on safe); a command not done after 30 s of lived time is said again with your lane ("Lane four, LOAD."). The coach speaks at the brief, after each string ("Four in the five. Breathe out, then press."), after the set stoppage is cleared ("A double feed. I seated that one proud. Good drill."), at your show clear ("Clear.") and at each fault, and never does your work.
  - **Seed 48213, played by the range bot to the end**: lane 5, a double feed seated in the third magazine's third round; 92.2 s lived in 34 decisions (4.9 minutes at 6 s a decision), 14 shots for 48 of 70 and 3 V (21, 13 and 14), the double looked at and cleared by REMEDIAL ACTION, no fault. Seed 7: lane 2, a stovepipe, cleared by IMMEDIATE ACTION, 97.2 s in 35 decisions, 58 of 75 and 5 V. (95.2 and 100.2 s before the reviews: the thumb's work no longer resets the held aim.) The scene ends on "The line is clear." (the mode left, the rifle slung front, 6 s of play) or on the line stopped; Again, Score and Copy my scores as the other moments have them.
- **G brings the rifle up and stops time; time stands still while you choose, and each step plays for its own time** (WEAPONS §7.1, §15; TIME §4.3; your words in tradecraft, 2026-11-03: "time stops while we get the weapon ready … each step to ready a weapon has to be acted on by the player and will have time move forward as we do"). A step lives the ticks its formula takes, ceil(Σ ms / 50), and the clock stands at its end exactly: at Weapon handling 10, LOAD 2,450 ms, MAKE READY 3,150, UNLOAD 5,050 (its seven sub-steps rounded to whole ms make 5,001, one tick past the sheet's 5,000), the aim 900, the hold to +4 1,350, Fire 400. 600 ticks of choosing move nothing, the clock and the state's digest alike. **The Corps' commands are macros of rolled sub-steps** (WEAPONS §5.3): LOAD 2.41 s at Weapon handling 10, MAKE READY 3.10, UNLOAD 5.00, UNLOAD SHOW CLEAR 7.90 (the coach's look 1.0 s), RELOAD 2.53 (dropping the empty), 2.99 (keeping it in the dump pouch) and 3.22 (tactical), IMMEDIATE ACTION 1.57, REMEDIAL ACTION 6.05 (its eight steps: lock back 0.69, strip the magazine 0.98, rack it clear three times at 0.52, the magazine in 1.61, charge 0.92, a look at the port 0.30), CEASE FIRE 0.40; a recruit at 3 takes LOAD in 3.66 s and a veteran at 30 in 1.18. Each sub-step rolls Weapon handling at the range's pressure, routine for the trained and marked so (SKILLS §5.1); a failure that is not routine halts the macro and offers its rest first ("Carry on: …"); a routine natural 1 costs 1.6 times the time and halts nothing. Over 400 rushed UNLOADs at Weapon handling 4, 51.5% halted; at 10 rested none of SHOW CLEAR's nine rolls halts; exhausted, cold and in the dark at 10, 72% do. **A step that answers the tower's command runs on as a wait** until the next command (15 s at most), cut at once by any step you choose, and a command ends a wait or a hold at once; CEASE FIRE cuts any other step at its sub-step's end. Tapped, a digit is the normal tempo; with Shift rushed (× 0.6, −3, never routine); held, careful (× 1.5, +2); Fire held is hold and fire. Fire again inside the recovery is a follow-up at the same aim (tradecraft's tap: a double from Marksmanship 8, a triple from 18). X leaves the mode while choosing; V, A and W are lived steps outside it.
- **The rifle is a truth and your belief of it, and everything you see of it is the belief** (WEAPONS §4; the build sheet §8 item 27). The R-7's record (`src/sim/arms/firearms.js`, tradecraft's state machine) holds the chamber, the jam, the bolt locked, the selector, the hammer, the port cover and the coach's set round; your belief holds only what you did, saw or felt: a missed lock back leaves "?", a bad inspection leaves the chamber "empty" whatever is in it (the one silent failure, which leaves no trace in J or the toasts), a click sets CLICK, a shot that didn't cycle FELT, a look at the port shows what is there (a double's chamber a case, a round behind a locked bolt). The weapon card in the step panel (`src/art/weaponcard.js`, 180 × 148 at 144 px/m) draws the R-7 side on from the belief alone, at a whole number of device pixels a card pixel (cropped by its box, never shrunk: shrunk, "SEMI" had read "<-NT"), its words at twice the letter: the selector's mark and SAFE or SEMI, the port open or shut with a glint for a round believed in, the magazine's believed rounds behind its window and its count beside it ("≈3" below Weapon handling 15, its baseplate alone when uncounted; "MAG OUT" in the words' colour), the bolt locked back, a "!" felt or a "?" unchecked, a stovepipe or a double once seen. **Tradecraft's fumble ladder holds to the die**: its own experiment through `act()` gives its 1.847, 1.394, 0.923 and 0.590 s, 119, 13, 17 and 27 fumbled, 15, 6, 0 and 0 dropped and 12 discharges. A fresh R-7 misfires on 0.013% of pulls and stops on 0.063% of shots (1,000,000 pulls); in a dust storm 1.19% stop, worn 2.34% and 2.61% (dust, fouling, lube and heat are read; nothing moves them until 0.0.8).
- **Every shot is tradecraft's roll: a d20 against the card's DC 19 with every modifier, the same list on the plaque, in the log and in the hole in the paper** (the brief: "Shooting should play as in Tradecraft", pillar 2; WEAPONS §7). The DC is 6 + the rifle's 0 + the R-7's 0 + 7 for 25 m (5 a doubling of range from 10 m: tradecraft's per-metre terms saturated at 25 m) + 6 for the 25 m card; the modifiers prone +4, kneeling +2, standing 0, the aim held up to +4 (Marksmanship's rate: +4 after 1.33 s at 10) or −2 while it settles from the last, a follow-up's climb, the SO-4 at 1× +1, rushed −3, and your body's and needs' on Marksmanship's eyes, arms and breath. At Marksmanship 10 the roll passes the DC at 65, 75 and 85% standing, kneeling and prone, 85, 95 and 95% held; **the plaque's figure on a card is the chance of a hole** (the edge ring's 2 sits under the DC, at margin −3 to −1): 80, 90 and 95% unheld, 95% held, within 1.7 points of 2,000 pulls a cell (it had shown the pass at the DC, 15 points under the hole rate: the parity review's); a string of fifteen held scores 59.8 of 75 on average (49.0 unheld), from 49.0 at Marksmanship 6 to 70.3 at 18. Tradecraft's dice and formulas hold bit for bit: the first ten d20s of its parity stream 7, 7, 8, 4, 11, 10, 20, 3, 15, 9 and its own log line word for word; its aim times 874, 2,087 and 2,792 ms; its pull 416 ms. **The plaque over your card** (`src/ui/plaque.js`, tradecraft's ported) reads "PAPER 95% | 25 M · AIM 0.9 S" before the aim, then "… · +4 IN 1.4 S · 1 HELD FIRE" by the keys ("CLICK FIRE" after the mouse moves), with the odds as a meter along its foot; the DC's parts and every modifier show under the card for 2.5 s after an aim or a move of the brackets by the keys, and while the pointer is on the card or the plaque (by the keys they had never shown: the design review's); on a cold line or under a handling step the plaque goes and the brackets stay at half strength (it had counted through SHOW CLEAR's sub-steps). **The hole is placed by hash in its ring**, never by the dice (WEAPONS §7.11): a V from a margin of 13, a 5 from 9, a 4 from 5, a 3 from 1, a 2 from −3, a miss below; on the stage it is a pixel on the card (pale through the black, dark through the white, a puff of paper for 200 ms), the card drawn three times life size so a string's holes don't stack on two pixels; in **PAPER**, a new inset at the top centre (200 × 200 at 4 px a cm), your card with the newest hole numbered and ringed in red, and the string at its CEASE FIRE shot by shot, a miss "-": "STRING 2 · 4 - V - 4 · 13 OF 25 · 1 V" (every hole had been numbered, a knot in the black, and the line had left the misses out). Each career's card has its own pattern: a hole's place is hashed with the world's seed (it had been the same card in every career).
- **A stoppage the coach set comes in your second or third magazine, felt, looked at and cleared by its steps, not a button** (WEAPONS §11.3, the build sheet §8 item 6): a dud, an orange dummy (40), a light load that short-strokes into a stovepipe (35) or a round seated proud for a double feed (25), on the magazine's second to fourth round, drawn from the world's seed. The click or the shot that didn't cycle stops the step and **offers the reflex card beside the weapon card** (COMBAT §3.5 into stop motion; the card stays in sight while you decide): "Tap, rack" (IMMEDIATE ACTION), "Look at the port" (0.3 s) or "Nothing: stay on the card", R keeping your answer for 120 s. Tap-rack clears a dud or a stovepipe, the dummy or the case thrown to the ground; on a double it "won't go into battery", and REMEDIAL ACTION clears it by its own eight rolled steps, ticked in the panel as they go: lock back (the double stays seen, the loose round in sight), strip the magazine into the other hand, rack it clear three times (the first throws the loose round out), the magazine in (the same one, from the hand), charge, a look at the port (it had been one 5 s roll; lock back and a rack by hand did not clear it, and lock back called the rifle clean: the code and design reviews'). Once the gun holds no jam, case or dud the coach says what he set, in that same string (a double on a magazine's 4th round, its 5th stripped, leaves the bolt locked on the empty magazine: the line had come a string late, or never, on 8% of seeds). The thrown dummy, case and round fly and lie where they land, as a fired case does.
- **Range safety is the Corps': the coach warns, then stops the line** (WEAPONS §6.4, tradecraft's rules with three of the Corps'). In hand and over 60° off downrange a warning, over 100° the stop, loaded or not; an aim at a person the stop from the moment it is chosen; another lane's card aimed a warning, fired at the stop; off your firing point's box with the rifle up a warning, after 8 s the stop; a shot on a cold line a warning, a second the stop; a command acted on before the tower gives it a warning; the finger on while you move or change stance a warning (a move key in stop motion is no move: Q and D step the brackets there, and had drawn the fault); flagging the coach a warning; a negligent discharge the stop. A warning comes again no sooner than 20 s. The stop is "Cease fire! Cease fire!", the coach takes the rifle, and the scene ends. **On the line with the rifle in your hands the walking keys move you without turning you**, the muzzle held downrange as the Corps holds it; slung, they turn you as anywhere (a 120 ms tap of S had turned you 90°, a fault, and a tap of Q 180°, the line stopped at once: the design review's). A fault's words are the coach's in the radio box; its toast is a tag ("Fault: the muzzle off downrange", "Stopped: the muzzle uprange"). **The carry habit rolls once each time someone comes within reach of your muzzle** (WEAPONS §6.3, one roll an approach however long it lasts: at Weapon handling 4 a low ready flags 15% of approaches, 25% in a crowd and half of them exhausted; at 10 it is automatic but in a crowd; at 18 always; slung, a recruit's near misses are 5.5%); a negligent discharge follows the posture's line and is graded ND-1 (the berm, the ground ahead) to ND-3 (uprange, through someone's span: nobody is hurt before 0.0.7's wounds), counted in the issue as a round fired and its case thrown as a shot's is.
- **The R-7 is drawn on your figure, side on, in every carry and stance the scene uses** (VIEWS §3.4): slung front and back, low and high ready and aimed, standing, kneeling and prone, with the frames for raising it, the charging handle, the bolt release, the press check, the magazine out (drawn out of the well) and in, the prone rest; ear defenders on you, the plate carrier over utilities with its pouches. The flash is 1.2 times tradecraft's (Mars's thin air), and **your shot's flash is a star over your head**, 9 × 5 pixels for its first tick and 5 × 3 for its second (FLASH_MS 100: it had been 70 ms on a tick never drawn, so a tapped Fire showed none), the kick 140 ms, the picture shaken 2 px (1 px did not read), all on game time at the positions' own moment so stop motion freezes them; **the blast throws up the regolith** in front of a prone or kneeling muzzle, nine grey-ochre motes (five kneeling) up to 0.3 m that fall at 0.38 g and settle over 1.2 s; **each case arcs to your right and lies in the regolith**, two pixels turning end over end, white on its first tick out of the port, a kneeling one from the kneeling port: 0.867 s in the air and 3.2 to 3.7 m out from a standing port, 0.366 s and 1.4 to 1.6 m prone, at 0.38 G; a dropped magazine lies where it fell (`src/render/decals.js`, tradecraft's items ported, one instanced draw). **In the weapon mode the camera leads 0.55 of the way to the bracketed card**, keeping you 2.5 m inside the frame, so the card at 25 m is in the picture at 16 px/m. The rifle's frames have their own atlas pool (8 sheets at 16 px/m), so the base looks keep their 40.
- **Stop motion has its own HUD** (VIEWS §7.4, §7.5, §8.10). The step panel (STEPS, 720 × 148 on the instinct line) numbers what the panel offers on the digits: 1 Fire, 2 Aim or Hold, then the commands with the tower's current one marked "◉", the singles your belief allows, the stances, the postures, Wait; past ten, 0 pages the singles. Each row shows its time over its odds ("85%", or "routine"); a step that can't be used is struck through with why ("Fire at your card · 0.4 s (on safe)"); Shift shows the rushed times; hovering a row names its roll and its rushed and careful times. While a step runs the panel shows it alone, its sub-steps ticking as they are rolled (✓ passed, ◦ routine, ✗ fumbled). The radio box (RADIO, on the panel) gives the tower's lines as "◉ TOWER · GYSGT ROMERO" and the coach's as "◉ SGT TREVELYAN · BESIDE YOU", two at most, each held 3.5 s and 0.33 s a word on a clock that stops with time. "Time stands still." leads the instinct line, and the DI's eight range rules speak in every tone (T does the step its line names: "Tap, rack.", "Double feed. Lock, strip, rack.", "The tower said LOAD.", "Breathe out. Press."); the needs speak on the line only at urgent.
- **I shows what you carry; J folds each roll to one line** (VIEWS §8.3, §8.6). I (`src/ui/cards/inventory.js`, tradecraft's pockets ported) gives your kit by gear: the plate carrier's pouches with their magazines, each its believed count ("5", "≈3", "?") behind a window that fills as believed, each pouch's fill and its reach, the sim's own time for a magazine in at your Weapon handling, the step panel's (it had shown 1.4 and 2.0 s the game charged nowhere: the parity review's); it shows and does not move. C's file gives the two skills the line rolls on, "Marksmanship 9 · course", "Weapon handling 11 · advanced". In J each roll is what the action did in its own words, "Two rounds in the action: a double feed.", with "Look at the port: Weapon handling 17 against 4 · routine" under it, and opens to its full sum and the DC's parts; the coach's look is what he found, named and with no number ("Sgt Trevelyan looks in the chamber: a round in it."); a failed look leaves no trace (SKILLS §5.14); J keeps the scene's last 400 lines newest first, filtered by kind (everything, the rolls, what was said, the rest: the parity review's, where 40 had lost string 1 before the scene was scored); toasts carry shots (a hole's tone, a 2 on the edge a hole whatever its margin), failures that aren't routine and a look that finds a stoppage, each in its words, and range faults as tags, the voices going to the radio box. "It locks back: empty." at a string's last round.
- **The range is heard, every sound made by code** (VIEWS §11.3): the R-7's report at 65.9 kPa (−1.9 dB of air), through electronic ear defenders 25 dB down and low-passed at 2 kHz; the berm's slap-back 12 dB under it 0.171 s later (55 m of path at 322 m/s); the action's clicks at their moments in each sub-step (LOAD seats the magazine at 0.8 of its time, MAKE READY pulls the handle and slams it twice); each case's tick on the regolith as the clock passes its landing, a step's cleared case and stripped round too (a live round or the dummy duller, 3 dB down); the tower's chime before its lines. Nothing new sounds while time stands still.
- **The weapon mode has its own keys, read by physical position and labelled for AZERTY** (VIEWS §10.1, §10.2): V the carry (KeyV), A and W the stance up and down (KeyQ, KeyZ), the digits as press keys (a tap under 220 ms, Shift, held), Enter aims at the bracketed card, Q and D (KeyA, KeyD) or the arrows move the brackets, the mouse brackets on hover, aims on a click and fires on a click on the aimed card; in a reflex card a digit answers and R keeps. The keys card lists them.
- **A game with no rifle in it is made byte for byte as v0.0.4's, the rifle's work changes nothing in it as it plays, and its saves load as they stand**: G with no weapon still holds time for a digit ("Weapon ready: time stands still until you choose (a digit)."), the sandbox's, the barracks', test-shower's and test-night's new games unchanged, a barracks played with G and a digit v0.0.4's but for the eyes' record this release adds (below), and no upgrade (the weapons domain fills its keys as v0.0.4's stub did, and a save with no eyes' record reads as eyes at rest). Two v0.0.4 saves made by v0.0.4's own code join the v0.0.3 ones in `tests/saves/` (a career's afternoon, and one saved while G's hold waits for a digit). **A save made anywhere on the line, in stop motion too, loads and carries on byte for byte**: while choosing, mid-step, at the reflex card and in a chained wait, in node and in the page, and the page's game is node's.

Seed 48213 by the keys alone: 95.0 s of game time from the brief to "The line is clear.", 26 digit presses with G, W twice, A twice and Enter three times; 48 of 70 and 3 V (21, 13 and 14), the double felt, looked at and cleared by REMEDIAL ACTION, no fault; the three strings' frames 16.7 ms median and 16.8 ms p95, none over 25 ms of 3,034. The careless run on seed 7 (MAKE READY before the tower, X out, the muzzle swung north, then west): "Wait for the tower.", "Muzzle downrange.", then "Cease fire! Cease fire! Muzzle uprange." and the end card's "Stopped: uprange · 3 faults". What read wrong:
  - **Off safe is on the panel's first page**: the selector now leads the singles (canDo's order had put it on the second or third page, behind the magazine's five, at every commence fire), and **the plaque never asks for a click the panel refuses**: on safe it reads "+4 IN 1.4 S · ON SAFE", after the last round "· CHAMBER EMPTY", in a stoppage "· A STOPPAGE" (it had said CLICK FIRE), and it stays inside the picture over a card near the frame's edge (it ran off the page's right side). Fire's struck reason "nothing in the chamber" is "chamber empty", which fits the row.

  - **R offers nothing while stop motion runs** ("R wait 15 min" showed under a running LOAD, and R would have begun the wait mid-step), and **X leaves the mode from the wait for the tower**, which it cuts as any step does (X after LOAD did nothing until the next command).

  - **J names the action before each handling roll** ("Magazine in: Weapon handling 25 against 9 · routine"; a page of bare sums said nothing of what was done), and the toast of a failure says it too.

Aim."); **nothing speaks over the end card** below urgent ("Free time. Spend it like it costs something." had come back once the line was clear); **"Time stands still." is the line's lead only**, no longer a toast as well (the hud role's report).

- **Four reviews of the round read the code and played the scene by its keys at 16 px/m, and what they found is fixed** (the code, runtime, design and parity lenses; `tests/range-review.test.mjs` pins each, failing without its fix):
  - **"Carry on" never fires a round the rifle is known to hold**: after the coach's "Hold. There's a round in it." (or an inspection that sees one) the rest of the drill is the round out and the look again first, and the show again ("Carry on: Lock back, Inspect the chamber, Show clear, …"); it had offered the rest as it stood, and taking it fired the round into the berm (ND-1, the line stopped). Nobody pulls the function check with a round believed in: it stops there and says so. Lock back is offered on a locked bolt with a round seen in it.
  - **A tap after a string's last round is no follow-up**: Fire inside the recovery with the bolt believed locked back on an empty magazine pulls nothing (it had pulled on the open bolt and called a stoppage, the "Click." card offering "Tap, rack"); a pull on an open bolt is felt as the bolt back, no stoppage.
  - **With the hammer down, the panel strikes Safe "hammer down: charge first" and offers Charge first**: LOAD and CEASE FIRE charge before the lever, UNLOAD and SHOW CLEAR take it after the lock back, MAKE READY after its charge; the lever's refusal is felt at once, no roll and no fumble's time, and said (it had rolled, spent 372 ms and halted with nothing on screen). Charge is offered only to chamber a round from a magazine believed to hold some, to empty a chamber with no magazine, to clear a stoppage or to cock the hammer, never with a round believed in and a magazine behind it (tradecraft's rule: the singles run to three pages at commence fire, not five).
  - **The tower's ◉ marks only the drill that completes its command**: at UNLOAD, SHOW CLEAR only that row (plain UNLOAD, marked too and first, never shows the rifle to the coach and cost 4.1 s of the pace scored).
  - **R offers nothing on the range's line**, from the brief to the end card (R, R had sat out fifteen minutes on the tower's line unanswered, and the splash broke the scene's rhythm); the panel's Wait for the tower is the line's own.
  - **The end card opens on Score it, and for its first 0.4 s takes no Enter or Space** (Enter, read as the HUD's "end the scene", pressed Again and threw the run away).
  - **Sling it ends the mode**; **the thumb's work keeps the held aim** (the selector, the port cover, a look at the port: only the hands' work on the rifle resets it, tradecraft's list); **T aims and goes off safe but never takes the shot**: the press is always your own digit (33 presses of T had cleared the scene); **the DI's line goes as the step it names begins** (it read the line from before the step, so "Cold line! Safe it!" stayed over CEASE FIRE); **a struck row's digit says why** (its row lit and its reason in a toast, Fire on safe in WEAPONS §4.4's words: it had looked like a dropped key).
  - **The coach's tip follows how you shot**: haste lines only where a shot was rushed or not held to +4; a string held throughout hears "The hold was right. Same again.", a lone natural 1 "One went wide. That happens; the rest were good." (a careful shooter had been told "You are snatching the trigger" for the dice).
  - **A magazine in reads "one round"**, not "1 rounds".
- **Your eyes now adapt to the dark. Just in from a lit place the night bay is darker than v0.0.4 showed it and you see only round you; five minutes in it is v0.0.4's picture exactly; half an hour in it is clearly lighter and you see the whole room** (your ask, 2026-11-06, scoring v0.0.4's test-night 80, 80 and 60; DESIGN.md's log, BODY §23.4: "Would be nice to have eye adapting to darkness making things less dark after a while in the dark compared to when entering dark from a lit place"). Every body now carries eyes (`body.eyes`, `src/sim/body/eyes.js`) that follow the light they receive: the cones reach their plateau at 7 minutes, the rods pass them at 8.3 and reach full sensitivity at 34, 3.63 log units in all from the lit head (65 cd/m²) to the bay at lights out (0.015 cd/m²). The picture's exposure and the sight's reach read one number. In bay-2-1 at 23:00, arriving from Head A: the picture is 0.47× v0.0.4's at 0 s, 0.65× at 1 minute, 1.00× at 5, 1.20× at 10, 1.90× at 20 and 2.48× from 35 on. The reach in the dark is 3.3 m, 5.6, 11.7 (v0.0.4's), 12.3, 14.9 and 17.6 m, and the fog of awareness follows (42% of the bay in sight just in, 71% at a minute, all its walls allow from five minutes).
- **Asleep, your lids dim the room, so the klaxon wakes you fully dark-adapted: the squad getting up reads as people, not dark shapes, and the lamps keep their look.** The lids pass 3% of the light the cones use and 1% of the light the rods use. Asleep in the dark bay the rods sit at their floor. At the klaxon the eyes open at 2.4 to 2.8 times v0.0.4's gain for what the rods see (the dim surfaces) and at 1.07 for what the cones see (a lamp's lit pool, a red light); v0.0.4's eye showed the rising squad as reddish dark shapes. Risen at your rack's foot, the bay in sight is 1.34 times as light on the screen as v0.0.4's eye showed it. The tick you wake on is never blind: the eye the squad's moves are judged by is already open (the review of this round's first build: lightK 0 for that tick, every placed marine beyond 2 m judged unseen). This is a different klaxon picture from the one you scored 80 (my call, `REF_LOG`: v0.0.4's picture stands for the eye five minutes into the dark). The two are ready side by side to score, and the picture can be held nearer v0.0.4's if you prefer (VIEWS §18, question 12: keep the dark-adapted wake, or cap the rods' gain so the klaxon shows as scored; the default is to keep it, your own ask).
- **The lights coming on, or a door opened onto a lit room, dazzle you for a few seconds.** Reveille's overheads over a dark-adapted eye bloom the lit room toward white: 2.5 times at the first instant, 1.4 at 1 s and 1.05 at 3 s (the cones' gain; the dim corners take the rods' 4, 2.1 and 1.1), settled by 5 s. Head A's door opened from the dark hall does the same. Only a glare washes toward white, by how far the light is over the eye's adapted level; a dark-adapted eye at rest clamps as v0.0.4 did. Your sight keeps the lit room's reach throughout (my call: in this round's first build a dazzled second let the sim move the fire watch off his post unseen, a pop the tests caught). Staring into a lit doorway costs you your night vision, which comes back over minutes.
- **Deep in the dark the picture loses some colour and leans a little blue-green. A lamp's lit pool keeps its colour and stays near its brightness, and the fire watch's red lamp stays red and spares your night vision.** The rods' share of vision is 0 at five minutes, 0.59 at twenty and 0.87 fully adapted; dim pixels move up to half way to the rods' grey (rods barely see red, so reds darken). The picture takes two gains: the rods' for what the rods see of a pixel, and the cones' (at their plateau, 1.07 fully adapted) for the rest. The mix depends on how dim the pixel is and how much of it the rods see (0.16 of a saturated red). Through a fully dark-adapted eye the fire watch's red pool shows at 1.27 times v0.0.4's brightness, where one gain took it to 1.61 times and toward salmon (the review); the surfaces round it are 1.19 times lighter. Each light now has a scotopic to photopic ratio (`src/data/places/sky.js`): red LED 0.04, amber night lights 0.2, overheads 1.5 by day and 1.25 in the evening, lights-out fill 1.0, desk lamp 1.3, street lamps 0.62, floods 1.9, Mars sky 2.0. Ten minutes at the red lamp's core leave your rods 1.57 log units darker-adapted than a white lamp as bright would, and your eyes are back to the bay's dark in 18.5 minutes instead of 27.7. Still open: the red pool's hue leans toward orange at its higher brightness (19 degrees against v0.0.4's 11); the full fix is the light maps carrying each light's S/P.
- **The night's splash keeps the look you rated: the picture holds as it was when the splash began through its time-lapse, and eases to your eyes over its zoom out.** Your eyes stay open in the berth until the lids close, and in this round's first build the lapse pulsed 0.88, 1.13 and 1.0 within a second of real time as they adapted (the review). Now it holds at 1.000 throughout, and the klaxon's 0.5 s zoom out brings in the woken eye, 1.0 to 2.8.

The eyes keep adapting underneath, so taking the device off shows them as they are.
- **Getting in and out of the rack takes 1.6 times as long, and a little longer again when nothing hurries** (your note on test-night, 2026-11-06: "the whole scene looks rushed, slightly slower animations on getting in and out of rack"; PEOPLE §4.6). At the klaxon and at a training sol's reveille: up from a lower berth in 4.0 s (was 2.5), from an upper in 5.5 s (was 3.4), lying down the same, sitting down or standing up from a chair 1.8 s (was 1.2). When nothing hurries (the weekend's reveille, the stand-down, going to bed, getting up after a nap) everything takes 1.2 times as long (my call): 4.8 s, 6.6 s and 2.15 s. The squad's tempo is set per call; yours is set by the moment (a drill open, or the ten minutes after a training sol's reveille). Now: sitting up 1.4 s, on the edge 0.6 s, standing 0.5 s, then the steps out on the walk's frames at 1.02 m/s (0.85 m/s when nothing hurries). Lying down plays it backwards. The bench's range-line after the reviews: 16.7 ms median and p95, 16.8 at worst, none over 25 ms of 1,170 frames, 16 draw calls (the flash star's own draw; 14 before). Before the reviews: the quick suite 454 in 26.1 s at k 1.4 (65 s at k 3.2 deep in the band, over its 60 s there; one run measured k 1.11 and then slowed, and time-determinism's skipped-minute ceiling failed on that alone, passing alone and on the next run). New: firearms, drills, carry, arms-kit, arms-look, firearms-rates and nd (heavy: the rates over 1,000,000 pulls and the negligent-discharge grid) for the rifle; checks, skills-value, shot, shot-grid (heavy), steps, reflex, instinct-range, staff and scene-handling for the shot, stop motion and the scene; figures-arms and view-range (heavy) for the stage; ui-steps, ui-plaque and ui-weaponcard for the HUD; range-integration (the integrator's fixes above, each failing without its fix). Extended: game-domains (the action table; the sandbox's, the barracks' and both moments' new games equal to v0.0.4's digests, and a barracks played with G and a digit, the eyes' record left out), game-upgrade (both v0.0.4 saves), places-validate and kit-makers (the bay, its pieces, every firing point and coach's place reachable), render-bench and bench-browser (range-line), core-browser (test-handling by the keys from the Tests menu to Copy my scores; the weapon state's rectangles apart at four window sizes), core-input (V, A, W; the digits tapped, with Shift and held), ui-audio and audio-browser (the range by ear), browser (saves made in stop motion, choosing, mid-step, at the reflex card and in the chained wait, carry on byte for byte in the page, and the page's game is node's) and ids-check (every item held exactly once). **Goldens re-set on purpose, the ground grown over X3**: GROUND_AREA 000d6d7a726622 to 0620249a3a1ee1, MELAS_PLAN 0f9ae504ac4dbc to 07d03f4ca4f3c7 and MELAS_PLAN_AS_WRITTEN 140c63b80bc327 to 05d1dbf22c101d (the bay's zone, melas.kd-25); KD_RANGE 012fb0731f2139 and RANGE_PIECES 0369e5d085a39f are new. The sandbox's, the roster's and the walk's did not move, nor Echo's floors' in that round. **The patch round** (built apart on v0.0.4's tree: 437 passed in 692 s in the background band, the quick suite 382 in 39.1 s). eyes-model is new, 11 tests: the curve minute by minute, the glare, the red lamp, exact composition at 1,000 cuts, lived against skipped across taps and through test-night's night to the klaxon (heavy) byte for byte, a sleeper waking adapted, v0.0.3 and v0.0.4 saves loading with eyes at rest, the light's S/P against lightLevel at 2,000 points, the night-vision flag, and a day skip stepping by the hour except at the sky's minutes, with the same eyes. eyes-sight is new, 4 tests: the reach by minutes in the bay, woken, the eye on the wake's tick never blind, and the fog following the pose shown with your own berth in sight. eyes-browser is new, heavy, 4 tests: shots of bay-2-1 at 0, 1, 5 and 20 minutes and at the klaxon against v0.0.4's eye, with the screen's number checked against the sim's; the red lamp through a dark-adapted eye at 1.27 times v0.0.4's; and the night's splash held at 1.000 through its lapse. people-sight's 23:00 reach test is now pinned to v0.0.4's eye, the five-minute reference. time-placement gains "getting in and out of the rack, a little slower (the user, 2026-11-06, ...)", "lying down in sight before the night" and "R's lying down can be taken back". moment-night is re-pinned (mustered at 02:32:42.091, was 02:32:40.091; the dawdle back at 02:43:10.091, was 02:43:09.091) and prints the drill's timeline beside v0.0.4's. The moment bots' R sends the lead as the page's does. view-squad (heavy) now pins the drawn picture. You rise too (T two seconds in), and no figure off its berth outside the walk moves over 0.3 m in a frame faster than 3 m/s (9 hops of 1.03 m without the fix). In the dressing splash nobody drawn as last seen jumps (3 without the fix). places-stairs is new (4 tests): the three wells' drawn landing, mark, well floor and 17 guard boxes against openTopOf and blockRectOf at 16 and 24 px/m; a well turned four ways, its mark upright; bots onto each landing from three sides and up each flight; and the floor named. places-validate: arrive-face and stair-top are each broken once on purpose. kit-makers: a flight is two grounds. core-browser: the place line reads "Echo Company barracks, ground floor · 1st Squad, 2nd Platoon", and the PERSONNEL card is checked for the rank the scene drew (a scene from the Tests menu draws its own seed, and a Lance Corporal failed the old /Private/ check). **Goldens re-set on purpose**: ECHO_1's from 11196730cf23f2 to 0784d8b14248b8 and ECHO_2's from 0beba02edd957f to 1127fea3c6e702: the floors' floorName and ECHO_2's new name. No other golden moved. squadbay-night 16.7 ms median, p95 16.7 at 16 px/m; splash-sleep 16.7, p95 16.8; the bench's sim 0.12 to 0.15 ms a tick.

## v0.0.4 (2026-11-06)

- **You have a body that runs every second of the sol, lived or skipped: hunger and the gut, thirst and body water, the bladder and the bowel, grime and stubble, sleep pressure and the body clock, stamina, stress and morale, and the core's warmth** (ROADMAP §4.1's "0.0.5 needs and the first skip" row, cut as v0.0.4; the build sheet `docs/build/0.0.4.md`; BODY §2 to §9). 0.0.3 had no body: the needs, the heads and the racks were scenery. Your record lives on you (`people[pid].body`, v 2), saved as what differs from a template; this round only yours is simulated, the squad's carry their height and build.
