Every rate is worked out at the start of a game minute (or when what you do changes, or the body changes state: the gut empties, sleep comes, you wake) and held to the minute's end, each ledger by its exact formula inside it, so a body advanced in one go or cut anywhere is the same body (3e−14 apart), and the next crossing found by solving matches second-by-second stepping exactly. **Every need runs through every gap** (your report from tradecraft, 2026-10-28: "when sleeping, the hunger meter is frozen that's a bug"): a whole reference sol ticked 1.78 million times at 20 Hz matches the same sol skipped, on every printed digit. A night's outcome skip (21:36 to reveille, 8 h 34 min) takes 16 to 19 ms warm here at k 1.00 (TIME §5.10: 25 ms); it took 22 ms until the record's copy, a third of the time, became a plain loop (the integrator's), and the first, cold one takes 35 to 45 ms.
  - The reference sol (the range day on TIME §9.2's times) burns 2,777 kcal at Melas's 0.38 G (BODY's 2,770), 3,303 at 1 G (3,280) and 2,569 on Ganymede's 0.134 G (2,570); a basal 1,763 kcal a day for the reference man, 1,392 for the woman. Water on a training sol: 2.0 L drunk, 0.84 L in the food and 0.36 L metabolic in; 0.82 L breathed and sweated off and 2.32 L of urine in 6 voids out; the balance holds at −0.20 L every sol. With the three meals hunger never passes "You could eat."
  - The bladder fills by the water's balance and wakes a sleeper at 450 mL; at 700 mL you are bursting and an accident comes at 3% a minute, at 850 mL at once. A bladder at 250 mL at taps reaches 393 mL by 05:30: no wake. Three minutes under the water take grime down by exactly 30, 8, 2, 1 and 0 for the first to the fifth minute; the hair stays wet 20 minutes ("Fresh. Hair wet.").
  - **You walk at the body's gaits**, BODY §16.1's helper read each tick (0.0.3 had three constants): 1.14 m/s walking, 2.69 running and 0.75 strolling on Mars, unloaded and rested, as before; Shift runs only while you can, and the feet stay put while you lie, sit or do something.
  - My calls and the role's, for you to overrule: a meal's water comes as the gut absorbs it (without that every meal sent the bladder to urgent 1.5 h later); the reference sol's meals 900, 900 and 1,000 kcal and a head about every two hours; the start body is the template walked along that sol to the start's hour, and the template is that sol's own steady state at reveille (bladder 160 mL, grime 32). **Open: the sleep balance misses BODY §7.1.** An 8.0-hour sleeper reaches reveille on Wednesday with 1.6 h of pressure left (BODY: 0.4 to 1.0) and is tired from 20:48 (BODY: 21:10 to 21:40), because the body clock puts 22:00 inside the wake-maintenance zone and falling asleep at taps takes 18 minutes; never tired in training still holds. Count the zone on the civil clock, move taps to 21:45, or correct BODY's text: your call.
- **R at a shower stall, a basin, a urinal or toilet stall, your rack, your locker, a tap, a drinking fountain or a machine starts what it is for, and anything longer than 45 s passes behind the splash** (the brief: "taking a shower will take a chunk of time forward on the clock"). R says what and what it costs before you press it, "shower · 10 min, done 19:40" (", the count 02:35" during a drill; in the slip "done 24:00 +12", as the watch shows it, and the title's save line likewise); held, it opens the choices ("sleep until reveille", "sleep · 2 h", "nap · 45 min", "dress (utilities)"). **R does nothing while you are getting up** (the review: R pressed again as the rise played started the dressing and the figure jumped 1.6 to 1.85 m to the rack's foot; now no offer shows and the activity is refused until the rise has played, and the move keys no longer swing your facing round mid-rise).
  - The activities (TIME §8, at Melas): a shower 10 minutes (1 in, 3 of water, 6 to dry and dress); the basin 3 minutes, 7 with a shave once stubble shows (12 h) on a face that grows it; the head 75 s, offered only while the bladder holds more than 50 mL (an empty one had "the head · 1 min" all the same); a stall 6 minutes; dressing 0.6 × the old outfit's minutes plus the new one's (PT kit to utilities 5.2 minutes, back 4.4), half that into utilities while a drill is open (the alarm's tempo: 2.6 minutes, a brief splash with the watch running); sleep until reveille or 1 to 8 hours, a nap 20, 45 or 90 minutes, both from your own rack's foot, and none that reveille would cut short (at 05:12 the rack had offered "sleep until reveille · 24 min, done 05:36", at 05:29 "· 12 h 4 min, done 17:33"; now within 24 minutes of reveille it offers no sleep, at 21:55 the +8 h one ends by 05:30 or is not offered). **The morning routine is gone this round** (the review: it had you urinating at a sink and vanished your figure in place for the stall's six minutes): a sink offers the basin and a drink, the urinal and the stall their own; a drink (0.5 L) or a snack bar (250 kcal, free until there is money) 30 s, lived, at a tap or the machines; and R twice, 15 minutes standing by, with "carry on" for 15 more.
  - **Each wing's hallway has a drinking fountain**, on the south wall between Head A's door and the bin (the integrator's: the activity took one and none was placed): "drink · 30 s", half a litre, the tap's sound. The place is authored, the same in every career; ECHO_1's and ECHO_2's goldens moved for it.
  - **Backspace stops a skip where it can, and what was done counts**: a shower stopped at minute 2 lands you in a towel with "finish your shower · 7 min" on R for 10 minutes, **and finishing it puts back what you wore into it** (the review: a resumed shower had ended in the towel it was cut in, the log saying you dressed): the water counts once across both halves (0.57 then 2.43 minutes, 3.00 in all; the resume had credited its water part whole again). **"Fit it in" fits the activity, not its effects**: a shower fitted to 138 s at the klaxon lands you in a towel, cut short, with its 0.43 of the water and a resume left (it had given the whole shower's effects in a fraction of its time, the dress part's six minutes in 30 s).
  - Under 45 s an activity is lived (a move key cuts it at its share); under two minutes it fast-forwards at the engine's pace; longer, it is skipped to its outcome. The lived and the skipped land on the same second with the same body: both bodies brought to their clocks, the shower, the night to the klaxon and the wait agree to 0.001 kcal and 0.0002 mL (the review: the 1.0 kcal and 0.97 mL quoted before were the fast-forward's last unsynced minute, not the engines), and time-parity now fails on a drift past 0.01 kcal or 0.01 mL, well inside BODY's tolerances.
- **The splash: the picture zooms to you, an iris closes round you, the watch runs fast, and a strip says what changed before the zoom back out** (TIME §6, VIEWS §5). In 0.0.3 a skip jumped the clock. The full form (a skip of 3 minutes or more): zoom in 0.4 s to 3× (13.3 × 7.5 m of the room, on whole pixels), the time-lapse at TIME §6.2's pace (the 10-minute shower 2.59 s, a night 5.98 s, eased in and out at 40% of the middle's rate), the outcome strip 0.8 s (each need that moved, before and after, the wet hair, the towel), the zoom out 0.5 s. Everything beyond 3.5 m goes to ink through a 1.5 m dithered edge; a short fast-forward only dims beyond 6 m. The light is the light of the time shown, so the bay goes dark at 22:00 on the way through. No game time passes from the landing to the end of the zoom out, and PAUSED never shows for it.
  - Measured in Chrome at 60 Hz (the stage role's run): the shower's whole splash 4.28 to 4.30 s (TIME §6.2's 4.29 s; in 0.38, lapse 2.62, strip 0.80, out 0.50), the watch running 19:30 to 19:40; test-night's night planned at 7.68 s, its klaxon's splash 5.42 s (in 0.40, lapse 3.57, decel 0.33, beat 0.60, out 0.52). The integrator's run in the foreground at 60 Hz: the shower 4.30 s (in 0.40, lapse 2.60, strip 0.80, out 0.50; frames 16.7 ms median and p95, none over 25 ms of 281; 25 draw calls, 482.5 KB uploaded in a frame at most), the klaxon's 5.42 s landing on 23,532,601,000 exactly (frames p95 16.8 ms, none over 25 ms of 346; the beacon lit in all 108 frames after). Played by the keys in macOS's background band, where headless Chrome paces 10 to 20 frames a second: the shower 4.28 s and 4.37 s, the klaxon 5.32 s. The controller fed frames of 4, 16.7 and 33 ms: 4.292, 4.300 and 4.323 s. Space, Enter or a click go to the end over a 0.25 s whirr; Esc opens the menu and freezes the splash; T on the strip takes another chunk without zooming out.
  - **Behind the iris, the vignette of what you are doing** (`src/art/kit/vignette.js`, 12 pieces painted by code, 2 instanced draws): at the shower you stand in the stall facing its head behind a steam cover, the wet head above it and shower shoes below, steam rising, water falling only while it runs, the timer lamp lit and a towel on the partition; **while the iris is closed the stall and the floor to its spot are cut down to 1 m, as the cutaway cuts walls, and the see-through's dither lets go**, so you read whole, not as the checkered shape of the first shots (the review); you stay in the stall through the six minutes of drying and dressing (towel, wet head), the steam thinning behind you, and step out to its spot over the last of them, the steam clearing, so at the strip you are seen clean in utilities; every piece of the vignette dissolves in and out with the iris, none there at zoom 1 (they had popped on the splash's first frame and off after its last); at a sink the tap runs and you brush, shave with foam on the jaw or lean over the basin; at a toilet stall the boots under the door and the OCCUPIED slide in red (on the south side of a stall whose door faces east or west, where the camera sees them); your locker open while you dress; the rack's sleeper breathing at 0.2 Hz, the curtain closing over 1 s, the blanket thrown back on sitting up. Inside a closed iris the fog lifts, so a sleeper sees their vignette (my call, the stage's).
- **Nights have taps, reveille and, one night a week, the alert drill, at a night and minute drawn from the world's seed** (your rule, 2026-11-05: what happens in the places is procedural). Taps at 22:00 ("Taps. Lights out."), reveille at 05:30 (07:00 at the weekend) with the bugle, and the drill one night a week between 00:30 and 04:30: over 1,000 seeds every one fell inside it, so the timeslip lies inside the night and the squad sleeps. **The klaxon stops your sleep at its exact second**: test-night's night from 21:55 lands at 23,532,601,000 ms, 02:30:00.091 on the watch, on the tick, after 314 game minutes across the slip (40 of the night's 349 steps read 24:00); the clock decelerates to it over 0.35 s, the beat holds it 0.6 s (red through the curtain, ALARM on the plaque), and the zoom out takes you into the dark bay. **A night that ends at reveille shows its strip** (the review: reveille, stopping "sleep until reveille" at its own end, had been drawn as a surprise, the strip skipped and the night's hunger, thirst and bladder left to the log): the lapse runs to it, the beat (REVEILLE), then the 0.8 s strip and the zoom out, 8.30 s in all (7.82 s before, with no strip). The ticker's last line goes after three of its 0.8 s gaps and at the strip ("Water off." had stood through the dressing and under the result), and on landing only what came at the landing's second and the activity's own line go up as toasts ("Taps. Lights out." had come up at 02:30, four and a half hours late; the 15-minute wait no longer toasts twice). A drill is an alert (TIME §7.1's A): it stops every activity; reveille is an order (O): it stops sleep, and waits behind a shower until it is done.
  - **The drill: up, utilities, the foot of your rack in five minutes** (my call on the review's: at ten, with a 5.2-minute dress behind a full splash, the scramble had no tension; now the count is at 02:35 and dressing into utilities in a drill takes the alarm's tempo, 2.6 minutes, the squad's too, so the head still fits and a stall or a shower makes you late). The horn in hall-2 sounds for 45 s, its beacon red; the squad leader counts the bay at 02:35 ("13 of 13"), the stand-down is at 03:00, and five minutes after it everyone is lying again. How late you were is counted from the count, by TIME §9.5's classes (on time, late-minor up to 2 minutes, late up to 10, very late up to 30, else missed) and kept in `time.attend`; T, T from the landing musters you at 02:32:40, 140 s early. **Dawdle and R warns you**: a shower at 02:30 opens the late card ("fit it in · 2 min", "do it anyway", "not now"); "do it anyway" brings you back to the rack at 02:43:09, late by 8.2 minutes. **The count counts who stands at their rack at its second** (the review: mustered once, you could go to the showers and still be "13 of 13"): gone, it says 12 of 13 and marks your arrival left; back before the stand-down you arrive again, late. **Mustered, R at your rack's foot offers "stand fast · until the stand-down"** (the review: it had offered "sleep until reveille", and the scene's only way to its end was 25 real minutes of standing): the count and the stand-down are now orders (TIME §7.1's O, "a muster you are due at"), so standing fast lands at 02:35 for the count's beat (THE COUNT on the plaque) and again at 03:00 for the stand-down's (STAND DOWN), the squad lying down in front of you; anything else in the drill, sleep included, brings the late card, and past the count every one does ("the stand-down at your rack, 03:00").
  - **The career's first drill comes on one of its first two nights**, still drawn from the seed (my call on the review's: CAREER §5.2's first-night spike, which drawing the week's night had made a 1-in-7 chance; seed 48213's career begun Monday now has its drill at 02:57 that night).
- **Your squad sits up, climbs down, dresses and lies down again in front of you, and nobody pops** (PEOPLE §4.6; 0.0.3's rule that a change happens only out of sight holds for every change but these). At the klaxon each marine rises after their own delay, 3.3 to 19.1 s, a rise taking 2.5 s or 3.4 s from an upper berth (the climb), dresses in 2.6 minutes at the alarm's tempo and stands at their rack's foot, an upper berth's 0.6 m beside it so no two share a spot; all twelve in utilities by 02:32:58. **At the stand-down you see them undress before they lie down** (the review: their outfits had flipped from utilities to PT kit half-way through 4.4 minutes of standing still, the dressing frames held back by the lying down still to come), and **an upper berth's sleeper stays in their berth as they sit up** (the review: from the klaxon's first frame every upper sleeper was drawn in the lower berth, hidden under the upper, until they climbed): the berth drawn is the pose's own. Watched through the drill and five minutes past the stand-down, no outfit changes in sight but in a dress frame and no berth frame off its berth. Watched 30 minutes through the drill: 150 changes seen, the biggest move between two ticks 0.201 m; in the page, 0 figures appeared or vanished and the most any moved in a frame was 0.147 m.
  - **Woken in your rack, you see out of it** (the integrator's call: the stage's report). A rack is a blocker at a lying eye's height, so a woken player saw only their own berth and 12 of the 13 rose unseen, drawn as last seen; now a lying, awake eye looks past the rack's foot along its facing (at most 2 m, through the cells of the berth), and the squad across the aisle is seen getting up and dressing (3 of 12 within 25 s of the klaxon from rack n1's lower berth, the rest as you rise and turn). At the bay's night light (0.04) they are dark shapes from the berth; standing, you see them dressing round you.
  - **Lying in your berth you stay in it** (the time role's report): the walk layer put a player whose point it may not stand on onto the nearest free cell before it checked whether the feet were held, so every tick in bed moved you out of the rack, (52.075, 1.325) to (52.875, 1.375); a lying player's keys no longer turn the figure either.
- **The instinct speaks: one line in the drill instructor's voice, its tone as its border, and T does what it says where it can** (SKILLS §14; tradecraft's `settle()` ported with a 3 s minimum, no repeat within 60 s, and a chip for the next). Eleven rules this round: the alarm (urgent: "Klaxon. Up, utilities, the foot of your rack. Five minutes."; T rises you, then dresses you at your rack; past the count, "You missed the count. Utilities, your rack, now.", never "The count is at 02:35" at 02:41; mustered and gone from your rack, the klaxon's line again), the bladder, the bowel, thirst, hunger ("The mail room's machine has bars."), sleep and grime at their bands, awake in the rack, ten minutes to taps, stubble, and the calm situation line. T held takes the chip ("T held Bladder"), and the chip shows "T held" only where it has something to do (the review: "T held Shave" away from any basin did nothing). Mustered in a drill, the calm line's T stands you fast until the stand-down. **The DI keeps the night quiet**: no shave or shower lines from 21:50 to 05:00; woken in the night, "The head, then back in the rack." or "Back to sleep. Reveille is at 05:30.", whose T sleeps you again in your berth; tired before taps is a note, "Taps at 22:00. You will sleep tonight.", the warning kept for exhaustion or tired past taps; and by day the calm line no longer sends you to a squad board you cannot read. R and T are press keys: tapped they act on key-up under 220 ms, held at 220 ms, never both. Silent in a splash; only urgent lines while time stands still.
  - **While a drill is open the instinct keeps you at your rack**: the needs, the stubble and taps keep quiet (the bladder and the bowel speak only at urgent), and once you are counted the calm line is "At your rack, eyes front. The stand-down is at 03:00." (the integrator's: mustered at 02:35 the DI had said "Shave. Head A has mirrors.", and "T held Shave" waited on the chip through the rise).
  - While the rise plays, the alarm line still says "Up" and T waits; the dressing line and T's act come together once you stand (the hud's report: "PT kit is not the uniform of the day" showed for 2.5 s with a T that did nothing). R's held choices no longer say their length twice ("nap · 45 min, done 22:40", was "nap 45 min · 45 min").
- **A woman's body grows no stubble** (the review: about 30% of careers are women, and PFC Marangoni's sheet had said "Shave: A shadow on your jaw."): no stubble band, no dots on the doll's jaw, no Shave row, no DI line about a razor, and the basin is 3 minutes with no shave.
- **C opens PERSONNEL: your figure as you are, what you feel in words, and your file** (VIEWS §8.2). The doll shows the PT kit or the towel, wet hair, grime thickest low on the legs, sweat patches, stubble, tired eyes and dropped shoulders, and past 0.6 the bladder's knees-in with a hand at the belt and hunger's hand on the stomach. ON YOU gives BODY's words for each need, each in a row headed by its glyph in the strip's order (the review: nine glyphs in one row above a table of ten had matched nothing, so the watch's folded glyphs could not be learnt), never a number ("Fed two hours ago; thirsty."; after the shower "Fresh. Hair wet.", "Fresh. Head wet." on a shaved head); THE FILE your name, rank, MOS, unit, billet, rack and time in service. The needs that have left fine fold into the watch's time row as 5 × 5 glyphs.
- **The Tests menu on the title plays the two moments to score: "Ten minutes under the shower" and "The klaxon at 02:30"** (ROADMAP §2.2 and §4.4's moments 3 and 4; `?play=test-shower`, `?play=test-night`, seed 48213 unless `&seed` says otherwise). Each shows its goal for 8 s, saves nothing, and ends on the shower's end or the drill's stand-down with Again (the same seed or a new one), Score and the title, **6 s of play after the splash, or Enter** ("Enter: end the scene" on the line, C and J open over the world meanwhile; the review: the card had covered you on the frame the zoom out ended, so you never saw yourself clean at 19:40 or the squad lie down). The score card has three aspects from 0 to 100 in fives, a star and a note; **Copy my scores** gives the line to paste back, `OLYMPUS v0.0.4 · test-shower · seed 48213 · splash 85 · vignette 70 · pace 60 · ★ no · note: …`, and every score stays in this browser (`olympus.scores`, the last 200).
- **The night is heard, every sound made by code**: the shower's water only while it runs, the tap at a sink, the flush when you go, reveille's bugle on every room's speaker, and the klaxon's whoop, 600 to 1,100 Hz, from the horn through Melas's air and the walls (+10.2 dB over the recipe's peak beside it, −12.9 dB through bay-2-1's door, −30.1 dB two rooms off). In a splash the world is muffled (900 Hz, −9 dB, with the iris) and the watch ticks once a shown minute, at most 10 a second. The sounds are now rendered a layer at a time across frames: the HUD and sound cost at the rack went from a 3.1 ms p95 and 14.4 ms worst frame to 0.8 and 2.2 ms.
- **The figures gained the moves this round needs**: stroll, the stairs up and down, look and glance, sitting up, standing up, the climb from an upper berth, lying down, two dressing frames, leaning at a basin, brushing and shaving; the towel (at the waist, at the chest for a woman, with shower shoes); wet hair darker with a sheen (a glint on the scalp of a shaved head, the integrator's: seed 48213's player is bald, and the shower left no mark on him), and the tired walk (the head down a pixel, the arms swinging half as far). The figure sheet is 512 × 752 at 16 px/m, so the 4,096² atlas holds 40 looks (was 64); a sleeper in a berth needs none, and the squad in PT kit and utilities at the klaxon about 27.
- **The bench card times frames by the browser's frame clock** (the timestamps requestAnimationFrame passes in; the review: `performance.now()` taken in the card's own callback, queued after the game's frame, measured the game's main-thread work as jitter, so the card read p95 18.5 to 18.8 ms where the frames themselves were 16.7 ms and none over 25 ms). The numbers below are the old card's; a rerun, and your Windows ones, will read by the new.
- **The bench card has a second scene, `?bench=splash-sleep`**: R at your rack at 21:57 and the night's whole splash, its frames timed. On this Mac at 60 Hz (the stage and hud roles' runs): the splash's frames 16.7 ms median, 16.7 to 18.1 ms p95, 1 of 465 over 25 ms, 23 to 25 draw calls, at most 482 to 521 KB uploaded in a frame (TECH's 1 MB), no program linked; squadbay-night still 60 fps (16.7 ms median and p95, 53 draw calls, 52.5 KB uploaded a frame at most, the view's CPU 1.20 ms at the median). The integrator's, in the foreground at 60 Hz: render-bench's squadbay-night 16.7 ms median and p95 at 16 and 24 px/m, none over 25 ms of 1,200, 53 draw calls, 51.1 KB uploaded a frame at most, the view's CPU 1.20 ms median; splash-sleep's splash 16.7 ms median, 16.8 ms p95, none over 25 ms of 470, 23 draw calls, 336 KB at most; the bench card's own runs in the same minutes, at 1280 × 720 with the HUD, 16.7 ms median and 18.5 ms p95 (squadbay-night, 0 over 25 ms) and 18.4 ms p95 for the splash (2 of 470 over 25 ms). Your Windows numbers are the ones still missing.
- **A v0.0.3 save loads and plays on, its body filled in**: every body is raised to v 2, yours given the start body at the save's clock (`mig:body-v2`), and tonight's taps, reveille and drill posted (`mig:time-pod`); a save made inside a v0.0.3 wait finishes it as saved. A save made anywhere carries on byte for byte, in node and in the page, mid-splash included.
- Tests: 411 in 82 suites, all passing (823 s with the heavy ones, run in macOS's background band; the quick suite 361 in 51 s in the band, against its 60). The review's findings, each pinned by a test that fails without its fix: time-review (new: the resumed shower's outfit and water, the fitted shower, R during the rise, the count by who stands there, stand fast and sleeping in the drill, no sleep past reveille, a woman's stubble, the late alarm and T with no line); time-placement (no outfit changing in sight but in a dress frame, no berth frame off its berth, through the stand-down; its quiet night takes the career's first drill out); ui-splash (reveille's beat then strip); ui-instinct ("T held" only with an act); time-activities (the empty bladder's offers, the hurried dress, the basin alone at a sink); time-pod (the career's first drill on its first two nights); time-parity (both bodies synced, the engines held to 0.01 kcal and 0.01 mL); core-browser (the scene's last picture held with "Enter: end the scene" and C over it, the wait's one toast); moment-night re-pinned (on time at 02:32:40, the dawdle back at 02:43:09, late by 8.2 minutes); instinct's scripted morning on a quiet night; body-sleep's title names its 0.4 to 2.0 h bound beside BODY's 0.4 to 1.0, the open question. New: body-energy, body-water, body-elimination, body-hygiene, body-sleep, body-skip, body-gait, body-mods and body-thermal (the body; `tests/bots/sol.mjs`, the reference sol); time-activities, time-interrupts, time-pod, time-splash, time-placement, time-parity (heavy), time-determinism, time-stillness and instinct; moment-shower and moment-night (heavy: ROADMAP §4.4's moments 3 and 4 played by bots, `tests/bots/routine.mjs`, each beat saved in `.run/moments/`); figures-frames, render-splash, view-splash (heavy) and view-squad (heavy); ui-splash, ui-instinct, ui-scores and ui-sheet; game-upgrade (two real v0.0.3 saves, made by v0.0.3's own code from a worktree at its tag and kept gzipped in `tests/saves/`). Extended: core-browser (both scenes played by the keys from the title's Tests), audio-browser (the water, the flush, the klaxon, the bugle), bench-browser (splash-sleep's row), browser (the v0.0.3 save and a save made mid-splash, started again in the page: byte for byte), figures-doll, figures-art (40 looks in the atlas), kit-makers, render-bench, ui-audio, ui-watch, game-domains (body in the order, activity to time), game-targets, places-portals, game-walk and the page's walk (the walk now sleeps to reveille at the rack), time-activities (the fountain's offer), syntax (`SIM_FOR_VIEW` gains `alerts.js`). The integrator's fixes: people-roster compares a game's roster with a bare roll's by identity, the bodies' ledgers aside; store-browser's synthetic save writes its bodies at v 2 (a load raised them, so the round trip changed bytes); figures-render counts the lineup from the outfits (21 with the towel, was 20); view-splash's sampler starts the activity from its own first frame (in the band a round trip to the page had cut 0.18 s off the zoom in it measured). Goldens re-set: the sandbox's new game at seed 48213 from `06e4132f321ebc` to `0d3ad82fd1c184` (the player's start body and its posted crossing; time's pod, drills, attend and prefs; the sol director's job); the roster at seed 48213 from `015b827a61b0dc` to `034dbe60c69d1f` (every body written at v 2); ECHO_1 from `08f56e69dfa150` to `11196730cf23f2`, ECHO_2 from `1afda0fc95253b` to `0beba02edd957f` and ECHO_1's C0 grid from `14b1247ac344b4` to `1201fe98a1ceda` (the hallways' drinking fountains); unchanged: TEST_AREA `1b598ff4fcfa65`, its C0 grid `189fefcff571bf`, GROUND_AREA `000d6d7a726622`, MELAS_PLAN `0f9ae504ac4dbc`, MELAS_PLAN_AS_WRITTEN `140c63b80bc327`.

## v0.0.3 (2026-11-06)

- **New career opens in Echo Company's barracks: you are a marine of the 1st Squad, 2nd Platoon, standing at the foot of your rack in bay-2-1 on Monday, Sol 590 of MY 211 at 12:39, your squad sitting and standing at their racks** (ROADMAP §4.1's "a walkable barracks on a laid-out base", cut as v0.0.3; the build sheet `docs/build/0.0.3.md`). In 0.0.2 New career walked the test rooms; `?play=sandbox` still does. `?play=barracks&hour=<h>&seed=<n>` starts at another hour (the squad asleep at 23:00), and `&area=<id>&at=<x>,<z>&face=<f>` somewhere else (`&area=melas.ground&at=522,224` is the company street). `&nostore=1` neither writes nor reads the database, the sound's saved settings included, so a bench or a shot opens none.
  - **A new career is saved the moment it starts, and leaving the page saves where you are** (the review: a career was lost on a reload or a closed tab until its first autosave, five minutes of play or a door away, and the leave-the-page save never landed). The launch writes the autosave at once, so a reload's Continue finds it; on leaving the page or hiding the tab the save is written with no read first, every record issued at once and committed (`store.autosaveSync`), the rotation to auto-prev planned from the records the page last wrote. Measured in Chrome: New career, then a reload at once, Continue; 3.2 s of play, then a reload, Continue carries on from them.
- **Echo Company's barracks is built whole, both floors, and it is the same building in every career** (your rule, 2026-11-05: "game world is handcrafted"). The ground floor holds 36 rooms, 38 doors and doorways, 392 fixtures and 148 lamps; the first floor 28 rooms, 28 openings, 413 fixtures and 135 lamps: two wings of three squad bays, heads, laundries, lounges and platoon offices on each floor, the company offices, the quarterdeck with its duty desk, the mail room and the duty room in the core, the classroom and the study room upstairs, and three stairs.
  - Every squad bay has 15 berths with a locker each, a study table and four stools, a wall clock, two windows and its squad's stencil, in one of three authored layouts used on every wing: A, an open bay with footlockers; B, cubicles; C, pods. Your berth is rack n1's lower; the fire watch's post is bay-2-1's study table, under a red-lensed lamp.
  - Every spot is reachable from every door and stair for all three body widths (366 spots downstairs, 391 up, none unreachable), and the walker bot reached all 757 through the movement layer: 2,345 m, 39,668 ticks, 44 doors opened with R.
  - Nothing in a place reads a seed any more: a site records only its template (0.0.2 kept a site seed). A v0.0.2 save drops its seed on load (`places:no-site-seed`) and plays on; a new save is unchanged by the upgrade.
  - The training sol, with Echo's interior measured by A* on the real floor, walks 793.3 m in 11.60 real minutes at 1.14 m/s (808.7 m and 11.82 minutes by 0.0.2's polylines; ROADMAP's limit 12).
- **You can walk out of the front door and across Base Melas to its fences.** The ground is 37 buildings standing as blocks of their heights, 41 doors (Echo's three open into the building: a reveal 0.8 m deep, shut while the door is), 71 paved roads, paths and decks with regolith between, the fence and its four gates, closed this release (the gate posts open them at 0.0.12), 179 fixtures and 173 lamps: street lamps every 20 m along the lines, floodlights every 40 m on the fence, Echo's guidon post and the company's plates by its doors.
  - It is walked on windows of 3 × 3 chunks of 32 m built as you go, at most one chunk ahead a tick and always 16 m short of the window's edge. The walk to all four gates is 4.2 km; under way the player's layer costs 0.006 ms a tick on average, at most 2.2 to 2.6 ms, and no tick builds more than one chunk.
  - A* on a ground window: 1,000 pairs a chunk apart at most, p95 836 µs in a foreground terminal's terms (2.5 ms measured in macOS's background band, below), 4,230 expansions at p95.
- **R is what is at hand: "R open the door" within 1.2 m of a door you face, "R close the door" once it is open, else "R wait 15 min"** (0.0.2's R only waited). A door's leaf blocks until it has swung open, 1.0 s for an inside door and 1.2 s for an outside one, and shuts at once; a door is one state, read from both sides (the floor's and the ground's). Doors sound where they are, Echo's outside doors on the street too when you turn them from there (they were silent from the street: the review).
  - **R waits only when asked twice**: away from a door the first R says "R again: wait 15 min" and a second R within 2 s waits (the review: R a step short of a door, or just after walking through one to close it behind you, waited fifteen minutes, which in Ironman cannot be taken back). R at a door still acts at once. Each R goes to the sim as what the line said when the key went down, and is dropped if the offer has changed kind by its tick, so a door that walked out of reach never turns into a wait.
  - A door turned while no built chunk of the ground held its reveal left the ground's record of it stale, so the walk out through it depended on the walk's history (stuck at the reveal unsaved, through it once saved and loaded); every record of the leaf is now set as it turns (pinned in places-portals).
- **Doors and stairs take you between the floors and the ground, and nothing pops in on the far side.** A portal fires 0.4 to 1.0 m past its line; each crossing is logged, autosaved and cut. **Behind the cut the last picture holds** until the new area and everyone in it are up (the review: a frame of the old floor at the new area's coordinates, the camera slammed to its clamp, then one or two frames of the street without you). The view keeps the ground and both floors built, so a door or a stair cuts in 3 to 11 ms once you have stood near it (the first cut after launch is the area's build: 500 to 940 ms measured in the background band over the round, 498 and 559 ms in the fixer's runs, against CUT_MAX_MS 1,000). Going up or down a stair is no cut at all: the floor you leave dissolves outward from the stair over 0.4 s while the other comes in, the stair and you on the same pixels (both floors share the camera's bounds).
  - **The cut into an area now waits for the figures of the people met there**, so none appears after it lifts (the stage's ask). The area-enter fact comes in the tick's player layer, before the actors layer makes that area's people, so their figures are asked for once the tick is over.
- **Everyone in Echo Company is generated from the world's seed, you included** (your answer, 2026-11-05: "All people generated"). The company HQ (13) and three rifle platoons (5 and three squads of 13 each): 144 marines and corpsmen, and you in the one billet left, each drawn on their billet's own stream, so one billet's dice never move another's. Each has a name from SETTING's pools (no full name twice in the world, no given or family name twice in a squad), a sex (30% women in every billet), a rank by billet, an age that fits the rank, a home, a culture and background, six traits, two tags, a quirk and a nickname by tier, a body and a look. Every pair in a squad differs in at least two of skin, hair cut and hair colour where the kit allows it: 1.67% of squads over 80 worlds are 13 men or 9 or more women, which those three cannot set apart, and keep the clashes fewest. At seed 48213 the company takes 8.8 ms to generate and 90.5 KB of the save, 30.3% women. The Weapons Platoon is not generated yet (PEOPLE's billets for it disagree with its own total).
- **The people you can meet are where the hour puts them, and change only where you cannot see them.** Placed this round: your squad's twelve, the fire watch (a private to lance corporal of 2nd Platoon's 2nd or 3rd Squad, drawn once a world) and you; everyone else exists and is away.
  - From taps (22:00) to reveille (05:30 on weekdays, 07:00 at the weekend) the squad lies in its berths in PT kit, rack curtains drawn on half the nights, and the fire watch sits at bay-2-1's study table under the red lamp. By day each sits on the rack's edge or their footlocker or stands at their locker, a pose every two hours.
  - A pose changes only where perception's visibility is 0 both where they are and where they go: through 22:00 lived facing the squad no pose changed in sight and 420 changes waited; turned away, all twelve lay down within a game second. **A skip keeps the same rule**: R's wait across taps facing the squad changes none of the 7 you see (they lie down once you look away), where in the first build nine changed in one frame at reveille, seven of them in sight (the review). Behind a cut, and for anyone you cannot see after a skip, everyone is where the time has put them.
  - **People out of sight are drawn as you last saw them** (VIEWS §2.11's memory, the view's own, never saved; `src/render/memory.js`). The view draws everyone and the fog only dims, so without it each change made out of sight showed in the fog's dimmed cells: through 22:00, 13 of 13. Now a figure changes only where you look, as the fog lifts there.
- **The fog of awareness is perception's own sight function, cell for cell** (VIEWS §2.11: "the same visibility function draws the fog and decides the dice"). It is computed over 192 × 128 cells of 0.25 m round the camera (0.5 m in the first build of this round) when your eyes move 0.25 m or turn 3°, a door moves, and once a game second; you see 10 + 42 × the light metres ahead in a cone of 110° and all round within 3.5 m. Racks do not block a standing eye, lockers do, closed doors and walls do. sightMask equals visibility on 614,400 cells over 50 scenes in node and on 3 × 24,576 cells in the page, 0 mismatches; the page's recompute takes 2.0 to 2.6 ms indoors and 5.6 ms on the street measured in the background band (about 0.6 and 1.5 ms in the foreground), the mask 48 KB when it changes.
  - **A wall hides what is behind it even when you stand against it** (the review). Sight lines are walked over cells of 0.25 m, the nav's lattice, and the fog is drawn on the same cells, where the first build used 0.5 m for both: a walker pressed to bay-2-2's west wall had its eye in the cell that held the wall, whose own cell never blocks, and saw 250 cells of bay-2-1 and ten of the squad through it; 70 standing pairs 0.6 to 0.8 m apart on the ground floor saw each other across walls or closed doors. Now 0 cells, 0 of the squad, 0 of 462 pairs; a wall's face still lights. The lit patches of hall floor past the bay's south wall at night are gone (the integrator's report), and the sleepers' heads against that wall stay lit rather than taking the hall's dark (a 0.5 m fog cell held both); what is left is a faint dotted line, one dither row, on the cut wall's own hall-side face. sightMask over the page's own fog mask, 192 × 128 cells of 0.25 m, takes 1.3 ms indoors and 2.1 ms outdoors at p95 in a foreground terminal's terms (4.6 and 7.3 ms measured in the background band, 3.5 times slower; the limit 3, scaled by the band like the other CPU ceilings now: lightLevel's and the actors layer's too). people-sight timed a 96 × 128 mask of 0.5 m until the last check of this round; it times the page's now.
  - On the ground the sight window is 192 m wide and kept while you are 56 m from its edge, past the farthest anyone sees (54 m by day), so which buildings hide what no longer depends on where the window was last built (the review: 919 cells of a 120 m mask differed after a 43 m walk).
  - **Into another area the fog shows the new area's sight at once** (the review: the vestibule's hourglass of sight was stamped on the street and faded over 0.4 s), and when its window moves a cell the eased values move with their cells, so the fog's edge never lags the world.
- **The light follows Melas time and the sun over the canyon's wall.**
  - Rooms by their lighting: a squad bay 0.75 by day, 0.65 in the evening, 0.04 at lights out; heads 0.65 at every hour; halls keep their night lights at lights out. The lamps flip exactly at 22:00:00 and 05:30:00 (07:00:00 at the weekend). The fire watch's red lamp lights its core to 0.29.
  - The sun is mean solar time and Ls's declination, with the canyon's walls and Mars's curvature: at the start's sol a flat horizon's sunrise is 05:36, sunset 18:23 and noon 83.42° up; the canyon's sunrise is 05:44, its sunset 18:15, a day of 12.53 hours, 31 minutes shorter at Ls 270. **The sunrise comes in over the 87 s the disc takes to clear the wall**, not in a frame (the review: at 05:44 the key light went 0 to 0.62 and the street 0.35 to 0.454 between two frames 16.7 ms apart): the sun's disc is 0.35° across from Mars, and the key light, the shadows and the street's step to 0.47 come in with its share over the rim, the biggest change in a 50 ms tick 0.0013 of the key light and 0.0007 of the street's level. The key light's height and colour follow the sun; its direction on screen is the art's fixed one, from the upper left, so the dawn's long shadows run up and to the right, whatever the sun's azimuth (106° at 05:56: whether cast shadows should follow the real azimuth at dawn and dusk is your call).
  - Ceiling lamps make a room's light, not pools on its floor: the look round's blotches in L1 and L3 are gone. Pools are the night lights, the red lamp, desk lamps, street lamps and floods.
  - The picture shows the light through one eye-adaptation curve, exposure(L) = min(1, (L / 0.75)^0.6): the bay at its daytime 0.75 shows its painted colours, at lights out (0.04) about a sixth of them; outdoors at night the sky is lifted to match (my call, the stage's: your eye should judge it).
- **The game is drawn at 16 px/m with the chibi figures, your pick** (2026-11-06: every star of the look round on 16 px/m chibi; VIEWS §2.3). New career, `./play.sh` and `?bench=` open at 16 px/m (0.0.2 and the first build of this round, 24); `?ppm=24` stays as a test parameter. The soldier figure (B) is gone from the game, `?prop=` with it; the look round's gallery keeps its shots and your scores as they are (`samples/look/sample.json` is frozen, so `tools/gallery.mjs` never reshoots them), and the live sample pages draw the chibi only.
- **The stage draws Echo's floors and the base.** A floor builds in 120 to 175 ms in the paint worker (the ground floor: 2,086 boxes, 12,609 quads, 35 moving door leaves, 8 wall clocks, a 2.5 MB atlas at 24 px/m); the ground streams in 32 m chunks built within 56 m and dropped beyond 96 m, none first drawn in the picture. A building, a wall or a fixture between the camera and you dissolves round you over 0.25 s, and a roof over you lifts. Sleepers are drawn lying on the mattress, head on the pillow, or behind a drawn curtain (the look round's L5: a body lying read as one standing). Stencils are painted whole in both alphabets: the look round's "2ND SQUAD" read "EUND".
  - **Behind a tall building you see where you walk**: hidden by a mass over 3.2 m tall for longer than the see-through's 0.25 s, the ground's geometry above 1 m south of you is cut away over a disc of 3.5 m round you, with a dithered rim, as the interior cutaway cuts walls down (the review: north of Echo the see-through opened only a box your size, and the ground and the fence round you stayed under the roof). A glimpse behind a lamp post keeps the figure's box.
  - Echo's outside doors carry the company's plate, ECHO in the Corps' red, on the street side; the green EXIT signs hang inside over the doors out (the review: the front entrance read EXIT from the street).
- **The wall clocks agree with the watch, and you can read them**: their dials are drawn 9 px across, 0.56 m at 16 px/m, bigger than life as VIEWS §2.2 allows (the review: a 5 px dial's hands were a smudge), a three-pixel hour hand and a four-pixel minute hand; their hands come from the watch's own helpers, every game minute; the duty desk's two-faced clock shows Melas time on the front and UTC on the back; through the timeslip they read 24:00 with their hands at 12. 1,084 random times, 0 disagreeing.
- **The HUD sits in VIEWS §7.5's five rectangles and none meets another**, at 1920 × 1080, 1440 × 900 and 1024 × 768 alike (`--ui` 1.5, 1.13 and 0.85): the watch top right (Melas time large, the weekday and the sol, UTC, where you are: "Echo Company barracks · 1st Squad, 2nd Platoon"), what is said top left (three lines at most, 16 s of play each), R's offer bottom centre, "? keys" bottom right, PAUSED at the top centre. While G holds time the offer's line says "Time stands still: a digit to choose.", in place of 0.0.2's banner. A debug skip says so once ("[debug] skipped 1 h"), not twice (the review: six $ presses filled the three lines with duplicates). The cards pause the game and hide the HUD under them: Esc the menu, now with the master volume and mute, kept in your profile across a reload; ? the keys, only the ones this build uses, labelled for AZERTY; J the log, its last 40 lines. The HUD and the sound cost 0.10 ms a frame at the median.
  - **The sound settings are written in one transaction with the profile**, where the first build read it and wrote it back in two, and a save landing between could lose a world's last-played time (pinned in core-store).
- **Footsteps sound by what is underfoot, made by code**: tile and vinyl tick, concrete, paving and stairs slap, regolith crunches; a foot-fall at each half of the drawn walk cycle (3.56 a second at 1.14 m/s, 2.98 running); at Melas they are 6.1 dB under the same step on Earth (4.2 dB of weight at 0.38 G, 1.9 dB of the 65.9 kPa air) and low-passed at 5,204 Hz. Nothing sounds before your first key or click (the browsers' rule). The sound is made at boot behind the title, silent, and the first key only wakes it: 0.2 ms, where making it on the key blocked the first step for 219 to 233 ms (the review). No sound is recorded: each is synthesised from `src/data/sounds.js`.
- **The bench card**: `?bench=squadbay-night` (or `?bench=1`) plays the bay at 23:00 for 20 s, the aisle walked east, west and back, then shows the frame times, draw calls, triangles, programs, uploads, view CPU, the renderer, the screen and the threads, with Copy results to paste back. It saves nothing and opens no database. On this Mac: 60 fps at both densities, p95 16.7 ms (the moment's limit 18), 51 to 53 draw calls at most, 50,218 triangles, 51 KB uploaded a frame at most (the fog's 48 KB mask while it eases: 15 KB with the first build's 0.5 m cells), 1.0 ms of view CPU at the median. Your Windows numbers are the ones still missing (`play.cmd --dev`, the same URL).
- **A save made anywhere on the walk loads and carries on byte for byte, in node and in the page.** The walk (`tests/walk-moment2.mjs`): the bay at 23:00, the morning by six debug hours and R's waits, out of the bay and up the east stair, down the main stair, the vestibule's two doors, the street until the sun's disc is wholly over the wall, north round the barracks to the fence, and back to your rack: 21,018 ticks. Saved at 16 chosen ticks (mid-aisle, between the debug skips, a tick either side of the stair's and the front door's portals, at the fence) and every 499 ticks (42 more), each loaded into a fresh game ends on the same tick with the same digest; in Chrome, 8 saves started again through the debug handle do too, and the page's game is node's, digest for digest.
- **The test runner says when it runs in macOS's background band, and CPU-time ceilings allow for it** (my call). A terminal behind another app (a game in front) runs at priority 4 on the efficiency cores, 3.5 to 4 times slower (a fixed loop, 114 to 130 ms against 33.7 ms in the foreground), and it cannot leave the band from inside; `tests/helpers.mjs` `machine()` measures that factor once, and the ground-window A* bench holds its 2 ms p95 times it (2 ms on a quiet foreground machine). Headless Chrome started from there paces its frames at 10 Hz, which the browser tests already allow for. places-walkers' worst tick and view-areas' chunk work per frame are scaled the same way (the first release run of v0.0.3 failed on each in the band: one tick of 74,496 at 7.05 ms against 4, one frame's chunks at 4.40 ms against 3; 1.75 ms and 0.50 ms in the foreground's terms on the rerun).
- **The design now holds your two rules everywhere: the world is handcrafted, and every person is generated** (your words, 2026-11-06: "game world is handcrafted, events and world politics, assignments, opportunities, mission details are all procedural"; "All people generated"). PLACES §9 is authored variety now: every base, hull, town and ground is laid out once, committed as data and finished by hand, the same in every career; layout tools only propose while authoring; no site, area or ground record carries a seed, and fine variation comes from a hash of coordinates (the per-site seed of 0.0.2 is gone from saves, dropped on load). A large authored place tags at least three spots of each kind (objective, barricade, hostage, insertion, extraction; PLACES §9.8), so a procedural mission is placed in it without rolling a layout. Public offices, the Commandant included, have generated holders (SETTING §7.7), generated households are drawn into authored homes (PEOPLE §12.4), and the docs' named characters are now examples of what the generator draws ("Doc" is whoever the roster made your corpsman). CONTRACTS §19 has nothing left open; the frigate's detachment is 15 (72 seats for 67), the people atlas's GPU budget falls from 128 MB to 48 MiB, and Base Melas's plan and walks are written as built (the training sol 793.3 m, 11.60 minutes).
- Tests: 287 in 52 suites, all passing (598 s with the heavy ones; the quick suite 252 in 26.4 s). The review's findings each pinned by a test that fails without its fix: people-sight (an eye pressed to a wall, 462 standing pairs across walls, the ground's window kept to its edge), places-portals (a door turned with no chunk holding its reveal, the street door's fact, R's want), game-targets (the loop's mode by the skip's engine), people-poses (a wait across taps facing the squad), places-light (the sunrise tick by tick), core-store (the leave-the-page save, no read before its puts), store-browser (New career, reload, Continue), audio-browser (one context at boot, the key's resume, the street door's sound), core-browser (R twice to wait); kit-makers takes the 9 px dial, figures-art, figures-render, render-worker and samples-look the chibi alone, view-browser loses the soldier's two runs, and view-areas prints the longest frame with its counters and the frames a cut held. New: game-walk (the walk, saved anywhere), render-memory (the view's memory through 22:00), people-roster, people-sight, people-poses, places-light, places-portals, places-walkers (heavy), kit-stencil, render-lightmaps, render-fog (heavy), render-bench (heavy), view-areas (heavy), ui-watch, ui-audio, audio-browser (heavy), bench-browser (heavy); extended: places-validate (Echo and the ground by PLACES §9.6's rules, each rule failing on a sample made to break it), places-reach, places-nav, places-walk-sol, game-domains, view-browser, kit-makers, kit-materials, core-browser, core-store (the profile race), browser (the walk in the page against node), syntax (`SIM_FOR_VIEW` gains sun.js, clocks.js, doors.js, perception.js and figure.js; the WebAudio constructors are kept out of the sim). Goldens: the sandbox's new game at seed 48213 re-set from `05fa96c32a3dbf` to `06e4132f321ebc` for the site seed's removal alone (putting `places.sites.test.seed` back gives the old digest exactly); TEST_AREA re-set from `089faeb83aa5ed` to `1b598ff4fcfa65` for the office lockers' look seeds (54 and 55 became 11 and 12: at most six authored seeds a kind); new: the roster at seed 48213 `015b827a61b0dc`, ECHO_1 `08f56e69dfa150`, ECHO_2 `1afda0fc95253b`, GROUND_AREA `000d6d7a726622` (first pinned at `131d1b63b7f5b2`, re-set for the street doors' plates in place of their exit signs and exit lamps), ECHO_1's C0 grid `14b1247ac344b4`; unchanged: TEST_AREA's C0 grid `189fefcff571bf`, MELAS_PLAN `0f9ae504ac4dbc`, MELAS_PLAN_AS_WRITTEN `140c63b80bc327`.

## v0.0.2 (2026-11-06)

- **`?play=sandbox` and New career are a free walk in the test rooms on the pixel stage, with the samples' figure** (ROADMAP §4.1's skeleton: "a free walk in a test room with the samples' figure"), where 0.0.1 walked a marker on a 1 m grid in 2D. The test rooms are a 48 m hallway, the squad bay and the office, with 31 fixtures (three racks with their footlockers and wall lockers, desks and chairs, the study table, the rifle cabinet, bins, emergency lights).
  - Z Q S D by physical key (the arrows too): the walk is 1.14 m/s, Shift runs at 2.69 and Alt strolls at 0.75 (BODY §16.1's Mars row; 0.0.1 had tradecraft's Earth 3.6 and 0.95). Walls and fixtures stop you and you slide along them; the bay's doorway and the office door pass.
  - **You collide as a body 0.27 m in radius, so you stop at the same distance from every wall and fixture** (collide.js: the class's circle against the very walls, fixtures and closed doors the nav grid is built from), where the review's build tested your point's 0.25 m cell and stopped you 0.21 to 0.32 m from a wall face by where the cells fell (at the bay's west wall the figure's cap crossed the wall line).
  - **The office's 0.9 m door lets you through when you miss its middle.** Its clear band is 0.36 m for you (one column of cells, 0.22 m, before), and a door assist slides you up to 0.3 m sideways when one key presses you into a jamb. Pressing S from the hallway at x 37.55 stopped you dead; now you pass, and S+A from (39.0, 1.2) no longer wedges you beside the west jamb.
  - **A door closed on you, or a position inside a wall, puts you on the nearest free spot at once**, keys or none; before, such a "loose" walker could walk through walls and off the area for good (20 s east from a closed office doorway ended at x 60.65, 12 m past the edge).
  - **When time stands still (G), the picture stands still.** The figure, its stride and the camera were drawn between your last two positions while the held ticks ran, so they shook by a step at 20 Hz (5.7 cm walking, 13 cm running: 3 px at 24 px/m). And the camera's lead is snapped to the pixel grid before it is added, so once it settles you stay on one screen pixel, where you flipped between two each frame.
  - The camera follows you east and west with VIEWS §2.4's lead and holds north and south, where the rooms fit the frame. The figure is the chibi until you score the look (`&prop=soldier` for the other); `&ppm=16|24`, `&mood=`, `&hour=`, `&fog=1`, `&nostore=1` and `&perf=1` for tests and shots.
- **The watch reads SETTING's calendar: a career opens on Monday, Sol 590 of MY 211, at 12:39:34 Melas time** (2351-12-19 02:20 UTC), and 12:40 comes 25.7 s (25.665 s) later. 0.0.1 started at the epoch (2351-03-22 08:00 UTC, 72° W), and its calendar read the start as Sol 606 · 12:59.
  - The local clock is TIME §2.4's: longitudes in [0°, 360°), and the 39 m 35 s slip centred on mean midnight, so civil noon is mean noon (0.0.1 ended each sol with the whole slip, and its noon fell 19 m 48 s early). Through the slip the face reads 24:00 with the minutes into it: "Sol 590 · 24:00 +12".
  - The year is SETTING §3.3's: Sol 1 of a year is the Airy-0 sol holding Ls 0, and MY 211 to 216 hold 669, 669, 669, 669, 668 and 669 sols. 0.0.1 used a mean year of 668.5991 sols from 1955.
  - `src/sim/time/dates.js` binds core's arithmetic to SETTING's definitions, now in `src/data/setting/calendar.js` (TIME §1). The watch, the weekday's name and the weekly jobs read one weekday helper, which takes SETTING's week offset (0). SETTING §3.8's vectors all hold: the epoch on Sol 326 at 00:30:38, a Wednesday, 19 Gleaning, Ls 174; the start on a Monday, 5 Patience, Ls 316.4; MY 212's New Year a Thursday, 2352-03-09 18:27 UTC.
- **The game is put together in one place, `src/sim/game.js`** (TECH §2.5). It runs eight domains in order: tech, time, places, people, weapons, combat, world, career, the last five stubs for now.
  - Each of the state's 25 keys is claimed by one domain, and a second claim throws, naming both.
  - A tick runs TECH §3.1's twelve fast layers (input and offers run while time stands still), the slow layer runs once a game minute, and there is no hourly layer. Actions are routed by prefix, and an unknown one is counted, never thrown.
  - Under it are ids (27 kinds, `p9` before `p10`), the event queue saved as its heap, recurring jobs, 24 registered facts and the log (1,500 records). The queue fuzz runs 100,000 random operations at 341 a millisecond, with the same 21,146 pops as a sorted reference. The `local@00:00` job asks the sol's autosave at its first second.
- **Time passes in skips sliced across frames, which land the same however they are sliced.** R (a 15-minute wait) and the debug keys ^ and $ (AZERTY's labels of the keys QWERTY calls `[` and `]`) begin an outcome skip; in debug mode the `?` card lists them, and it says what R does in this build (wait 15 min) where it said "what is at hand". It steps whole game minutes; a `day` skip steps hours while every slow process is steady, and a `world` skip steps from event to event. Under 120 s a skip is a fast-forward of lived ticks, at most 40 ticks and 8 ms a frame.
  - An 8-hour sleep stopped 5 h in gives one digest (308 steps) under three splash curves and when run at once. A skip saved after its third slice resumes to the same bytes. A hold set during a skip stops it.
  - The loop has four modes: paused, live, fast-forward and outcome.
  - Every skip step ends on the 50 ms tick grid: a process's next change is rounded up to a whole tick, and so is a chunk added to a wait. In the review's build a fractional change left the clock at …678.9 ms, where live play never met a whole second again and the live autosave never came. An event due on the skip's first clock is handled at its own second, not one later.
  - A slow process that breaks loses only its own minute: the others were stepped over the same minute twice.
  - **The slow processes are stepped over each moment of game time once, lived or skipped**, from a saved mark, `time.slowToMs`. Store's test found that 90.5 s lived, a 5-minute skip and 90.5 s more stepped [60, 90.5] s never and [360, 390.5] s twice.
- **Saves are kept per world, and a damaged one hands over to the save that stands in for it** (store v2: IndexedDB `olympus` version 2, with saves, profile and cache).
  - Slots are named `<world>:<name>`, 15 names in all. Ironman saves only to `ironman`. Each record carries its digest, which is checked on load.
  - A damaged save offers Standard's auto-prev, day or newest manual save, or Ironman's ironman-prev, which marks the career recovered.
  - TIME §13's autosave triggers replace 0.0.1's save every 30 game minutes: a skip's end, the sol's start, and five minutes of live play. The sol's start and live play wait for a quiet frame (TECH §7.4), up to 10 s. `auto` rotates to `auto-prev` 10 game or 5 real minutes apart, in one transaction.
  - A v0.0.1 save opens in the new database, lands at the spawn and plays on, its player record made again (0.0.1's name, x and z no longer linger in it). A damaged v0.0.1 save offers its stand-ins by their own keys ('manual-3'; the review's build offered 'w-legacy:manual-3', which no record has).
  - **Rolling back keeps the saves in reach from now on:** a database a newer OLYMPUS has taken to a higher version opens as it stands (IndexedDB refuses a lower version by number), so `tools/promote.sh <older tag>` back to 0.0.2 or later still lists the saves. **0.0.1 cannot do this:** once 0.0.2 has run, a play copy rolled back to v0.0.1 finds no saves until it is promoted forward again. Nothing is deleted.
  - A 469 KB save writes in 19 ms and loads in 8.5 ms. Loading parses with no reviver unless the save holds an encoded Infinity or NaN: 3.2 ms for 499 KB, against 27.5 ms with one.
  - Report a problem anchors on the state you started from until the first autosave. After a Continue it had no anchor. `__olympus.report()` gives the same report as the menu now (it had no anchor, renderer, engine, screen or density).
  - An error while drawing is told and reported, but never written into the save: the review's build wrote 'render: …' into `meta.errors`, even paused, so the page's save parted from a node replay exactly when something broke.
- **Places can find a way and be walked** (PLACES §2). A* runs on 0.25 m cells for three body widths (C0 0.27, C1 0.33, C2 0.40 m), with whole-number step weights 10 and 14. Over 1,000 random pairs it takes 145 µs at the median and 690 µs at p95 in the test rooms, 393 µs at p95 on a 64 m window of ground, and allocates nothing per solve.
  - Path requests are delivered on a tick their cost fixes, and the same after a save and a load. Closing a door re-marks 13 cells, and leaves the grid a rebuild from the save gives even where two rooms share an edge on a cell's centre (the review's build left 2 cells different there). The "locked" answer (a way only through a closed door) reads a cached all-open grid instead of building one per request (0.25 ms each).
  - Every spot of the test rooms is reachable for all three widths, and the walker bot reaches each one through the movement layer.
- **Base Melas is drawn closer together, so the training sol walks in 11.82 real minutes at 1.14 m/s**, against ROADMAP §4.1's limit of 12 (808.7 m; the gym evening 13.86 minutes). PLACES §4.2 as written walks 880.1 m in 12.87 minutes.
  - The armoury moves from (420, 190) to (444, 190), the cleaning canopy from (420, 216) to (446, 216), and the mess hall from (488, 246) to (488, 242). From Echo's door the armoury window is now 62 m (86 m before) and the mess 27 m (31 m).
  - The plan has 43 buildings, 47 doors, 150 waypoints and 166 edges, and every ground edge is within 0.17% of A*. It is built from PLACES §4.2's tables by `src/sim/places/siteplan.js` (the data module holds only the tables, TECH §2.4); its goldens did not move.
- **The stage paints its areas and figures in a worker, and nothing it uploads mid-play is over a megabyte a frame.**
  - The test rooms are laid out, painted and packed in the worker in 35 to 60 ms: 129 boxes, 783 quads, one 864 KB atlas page and one draw. Two workers run on 8 threads or more.
  - Uploads are held to 1 MB and 2 ms a frame, the player's first. The shader programs compile behind the title (8 in about 60 ms), and none links while you walk.
  - A new area builds behind a cut held at most 1 s: 300 to 330 ms measured on a `?play=` boot, of which the area's own build is 42 to 48 ms (the worker's job 39 ms, the meshes and upload 2 to 9 ms) and the rest waits for the worker to start. The build's 250 ms budget is now held to the build alone, so the warning that came on every `?play=` boot (263 to 402 ms) is gone.
  - `?perf=1` times the GPU by pass. `MAX_PEOPLE` rises from 160 to 256.
  - In the sandbox the stage draws in 5 calls standing and 6 walking (3,152 triangles), with 0.6 ms of main-thread work a frame (p95 1.0 ms).
- **Two things the shots showed are fixed.** The "TEST ROOMS" stencil moves from 1.95 m to 1.6 m up the wall, because the emergency lamp at 2.2 m covered its R. PAUSED sits at 9% of the screen's height, where at 18% it covered the top edge of the picture at 1280 × 720.
- **The title and the enlistment page work from the keyboard.** The arrows (and Z and S, by where they sit) move between their buttons, Tab too, and Enter or Space presses one; the game takes Tab, Space and the move keys only in play with no card up, where before it took them everywhere and only the first button could be used without the mouse. A move key let go while Cmd is held no longer walks on by itself (macOS sends no keyup for it: letting Cmd go now lets every key go).
- **The design's shared numbers live in one place, `docs/design/CONTRACTS.md`**, and the twelve sections agree with it: the clock constants and the start, the Plan of the Day, the job slots, the budgets, people per scene, the state's keys, ids, the bus's facts, the save slots, voyages, unit sizes, the air and the names. Every section cites it rather than restating it. 126 edits aligned the sections with each other. Among them: the Coprates-class transport is PLACES's 240 m hull, with eight Simooms and a wave of 352 marines where SETTING had 384; Europa's towns breathe Ice standard air at 75 kPa where SETTING had 55; a frigate carries 15 marines. The disagreements still open are listed in its §19.
  - **Your two rules are in the hub:** the world is handcrafted, the same in every career, while events, politics, assignments, opportunities and mission details are procedural (your words, 2026-11-05). **All people are generated**, your squad and chain of command included (your answer, 2026-11-05). The sections that still roll layouts per seed or name written characters are corrected in the next round.
  - The hub's platoon is now 44 and its company 191, PEOPLE's billets, where it gave 43 and 190. The order of precedence when two documents disagree is written under the hub's Sections. `docs/build/0.0.2.md` is this round's build sheet.
- Tests: 206 in 35 suites, all passing (159 s with the heavy ones; the quick suite 186 in 14 s, which `tests/run.mjs` now prints and fails above 60 s, `QUICK_LIMIT_S`). New: core-queue, game-ids, game-domains, game-targets, game-slow, game-determinism (lived, saved, skipped, sliced, fast-forwarded, key order reversed, two games in one process, both densities: one digest each way, every save's ids clean), store-browser, places-nav, places-move (the walker's collision, a door closed on it, the office door missed by its middle, the stand-off at every wall face), places-reach, places-validate, places-walk-sol, render-uploads, render-worker and view-browser; the review's findings each pinned by a test that fails without its fix (the tick grid of skips and an event on a skip's first clock, a slow process that throws, a render error kept out of the save, a database at version 3 in node and in Chrome, a damaged v0.0.1 save's stand-ins, the shared room rule, time standing still on screen, the title from the keyboard, Cmd letting the keys go); core-calendar takes SETTING §3.8's vectors and TIME §2.4's slip. The browser tests take `OLYMPUS_TEST_PORT`. Headless Chrome paces its frames at 10 to 60 Hz on this Mac, changing from page to page, so on a slow clock view-browser holds the main thread's work to 4 ms (0.6 to 0.8 ms measured), and at both densities, whatever the clock, 40 frames driven back to back and each finished on the GPU to 16.7 ms at the median (0.1 to 0.3 ms measured). Helpers: `tests/game.mjs` (the game headless), `golden.mjs`, `game-dummy.mjs` (a world with its own dice), `ids-check.mjs` (the id walk; an answered path request counts) and `bots/`. Goldens: the sandbox's new game at seed 48213 is `05fa96c32a3dbf` (pinned first at `0801d75ed47041`, re-set for the calendar and the slow mark); TEST_AREA `089faeb83aa5ed` (pinned first at `1817a37d161566`, re-set for the stencil), its C0 grid `189fefcff571bf`, MELAS_PLAN `0f9ae504ac4dbc`, MELAS_PLAN_AS_WRITTEN `140c63b80bc327`.

## v0.0.1 (2026-11-05)

- **The method, carried over from tradecraft and longhaul** (CLAUDE.md): the play copy (`.play/`, a git worktree pinned to a release tag) served by `./play.sh` on :8200 and moved only by `tools/promote.sh` on the user's go, with `--status` and rollback to an older tag; the working tree on :8201 (`./play.sh --dev`); on Windows, `play.cmd` (through `tools/play.ps1`: play, `--dev`, `--promote [vX.Y.Z]`, `--status`, the tags fetched from GitHub first). The ports keep the saves apart: one browser origin each, the gallery on :8212, the browser tests on :8223, agents' smoke runs on :8230 to :8239.
- **Releases** through `tools/release.sh`, on OLYMPUS's numbering (the user, 2026-11-05: Claude cuts patches and bumps the minor at tested milestones; 1.0 is the user's call): `patch` (0.0.1 to 0.0.2), `minor` (0.0.9 to 0.1.0), `rc --major` (opens 1.0.0-rc1), `rc` (the next candidate), `major --user-validated` (1.0.0-rcN to 1.0.0). It refuses an empty Unreleased, an existing tag, a branch other than `main`, and any untracked file outside the shipped top-level paths; it backs VERSION and CHANGELOG.md up in `.run/`, runs `npm test` (`QUICK` and `SKIP` pass through), and puts both files and git's index back if the tests, the commit or anything else before it fails. Then the commit `vX.Y.Z: <summary>`, an annotated tag, and the push to github.com/mramad3us/olympus. Checked in a scratch repository: 14 version transitions and 10 refusals, and each safeguard (failing tests, a failing commit, a stray file, Ctrl+C, a push that fails).
- **The file server** (`tools/serve.py`): `Cache-Control: no-store` (a cached module mixed with new ones fails the load), a listen queue of 128 (the standard 5 resets the odd module request), bound to 127.0.0.1, file types set in code (Windows' registry can serve `.js` as text) for scripts, images, fonts and sound.
- **The test runner** (`node tests/run.mjs [filter]`, `npm test`, `npm run test:quick`): `{ name, fn, heavy?, timeoutMs? }` per test; `QUICK=1` leaves out the heavy ones, `SKIP=a,b` names; a test that hangs is stopped after five minutes (`TIMEOUT_MS`), and a test file that doesn't load is a failure, not a crash. `tests/browser.mjs`: `withGame(fn, { port, query })` serves the tree on a port of its own, drives the system Chrome headless on the GPU (playwright-core 1.64.0), waits for `window.__ready`, and fails on page errors, console errors and HTTP errors (at once, when they come before the page is ready).
- **The rules of the code, checked:** every module in `src/` parses and opens with a header that explains it; no `Math.random` in `src/`; the sim (`src/sim`, `src/core`) never touches the DOM or three.js, and the two core modules that talk to the browser on purpose (`src/core/store.js`, `src/core/input.js`) are named and imported by nothing else in the sim; no em dashes in `src/`. `tools/shot.mjs` screenshots a page in headless Chrome and fails on its errors. `tools/awake.sh` keeps the Mac awake on a lease.
- **The game is designed end to end** (`docs/DESIGN.md`, the hub, and twelve sections in `docs/design/`, about 390,000 words): the setting of 2351 for a marine (Base Melas at −4.5 km and 65.9 kPa in Melas Chasma, the Corps from fire team to division, the Jovian Accord and the Europa stabilisation force), time (Mars sols with the 39 m 35 s timeslip, skips with splashes and interrupts, chapters), the body (air by inspired O₂, water, food, hygiene, bladder and bowel, G-load and G-conditioning, radiation, wounds and healing), skills, weapons, combat from the squad to ship combat seen from a couch, people with lives, the career, the places, the views and controls, the tech, and the roadmap to 1.0. The career opens on Monday, Sol 590 of MY 211, at 12:40 Melas time.
  - Written by eleven section writers and a roadmap writer, read by five critics (consistency, realism, fun, feasibility, your tastes), then corrected: 240 findings (24 high), 225 applied.
  - The hub now quotes your brief whole, and takes the critics' hub findings: **the first standby is lived sol by sol at 1:1** (your brief: "full days (Mars days not 24h) moving in real time until we do things that spend time like eating"), the air table by inspired O₂ (Olympus Mons' summit: unconscious in under a minute), the warmth of the thin CO₂ air explained (perfluorocarbon plants), and your carried-over tastes from LONGHAUL and tradecraft listed in one place.
  - No image generation, ever: every image and sound is drawn by the game's code (your words, 2026-11-05).
- **The engine core runs** (`src/core/`): game time in integer milliseconds from LONGHAUL's epoch (2351-03-22 08:00 UTC), a fixed 20 Hz tick (`TICK_MS` 50) over the browser's frames, at most 5 ticks a frame, with fast, per-minute and hourly layers.
  - **Three clocks from one time:** Mars local time with the timeslip after midnight (the Allison and McEwen Mars Sol Date; Mars24's worked example to 0.0001 sol), the Mars Year (Clancy et al.), and UTC as ship time, all in whole milliseconds with no Date object (checked against Date on 20,000 random times from 1600 to 3000).
  - **Skips** move a minute at a time, run the layers they cross and stop on an interrupt: a week-long skip steps 10,080 minutes in 0.5 ms.
  - **Saves:** plain JSON in IndexedDB (`olympus`), keys sorted so equal games give equal bytes, `$` keys stripped, export and import as a file; Standard (save anywhere) and Ironman (one autosave) slots; an autosave every 30 game minutes and on leaving the page.
  - **Randomness** from tradecraft's seeded sfc32 (bit for bit), in named streams so one system's draws never move another's rolls; cosmetic choices by hash.
  - **Keys** by physical position, labelled AZERTY; the binding table is data.
  - **The page:** the title paper (Continue, New career: enlist, Load a save file), the enlistment paper (Standard or Ironman), the Esc menu and the `?` keys sheet. A stub sandbox (`?play=sandbox`) walks a marker on a 1 m grid with Z Q S D and shows the three clocks; the stage replaces it in a coming patch. `window.__olympus` is the debug handle.
- **The pixel stage is ported from tradecraft and made cheaper** (`src/render/`, `src/art/`): an orthographic camera looking north at 45°, every pixel snapped to the palette, outlines from part IDs, toon light with 10 lamp-pool slots, cutaway walls (north and side walls 2.8 m, the rest cut to 1.0 m), the fog of awareness, and seven moods (day, dusk, night, ship's normal light, battle red, emergency amber, blackout).
  - One scene pass writes colour and part IDs together (WebGL2 multiple render targets), where tradecraft drew every mesh twice; the palette snap reads a lookup baked on the GPU (the same answer as tradecraft's full loop on 99.97 to 100% of cells, at a thirtieth of the work).
  - An area's walls, floors and furniture batch into one draw: a furnished room draws in 5 calls a frame (tradecraft's flat: 821), 0.35 ms a frame at 24 px/m.
  - Two densities from one constant: 16 px/m at 640 × 360 and 24 px/m at 960 × 540 (`?ppm=16|24`, default 24 until you choose).
  - **Figures** in two proportions for you to choose from: A, tradecraft's chibi (2.4 heads), and B, a taller soldier (3.9 heads); seven outfits (utilities in the Valles pattern, NCO, PT kit, the light vac suit sealed or open, the plate carrier, a Europa miner); idle, walk, run, the low-g bound, aim, fire, kneel, prone, the magazine out and in, sit, the crash couch, asleep, float and tumbles, in four facings (the left view a true left, the rifle in the right hand). Drawn by three instanced calls from one 4096² atlas, painted in budgeted bands.
  - **The kit:** 40 kinds of furniture in 115 makes (racks, lockers, mess lines, showers, stalls, gym racks, centrifuge gondolas, range booths, suit lockers, airlocks, hatches, crash couches), ten painted materials, the valley floor's ground with rover tracks and boot prints, and air weather (haze, dust storms by optical depth, steam).
- **The look round is ready for you to score** (`./play.sh --gallery`, or `play.cmd --gallery`, then the stars and scores; "Copy my picks" gives the text to paste back): five scenes, each a 2 × 2 of 16 or 24 px/m by chibi or soldier figures, differing in nothing else.
  - L1 the squad bay at reveille (13 marines), L2 the head in the evening (a shower behind its curtain), L3 the mess hall at noon (25 people in the line and at the tables), L4 the valley floor in vac suits by day and in a τ 4 dust storm, L5 a frigate's deck 5 at battle stations, strapped into crash couches.
  - Known to look at: the faint ceiling-lamp pools read as blotches on the floor in L1 and L3, and a body lying head north reads like one standing (L5's couches).
  - `./play.sh` and `play.cmd` take `--gallery` (port 8212, so the gallery's stars never mix with your saves).
- Tests: 115 in 20 suites, all passing (101 s with the heavy ones). run (the runner), syntax (parsing and the four rules, with a self-check of the code reader), version (VERSION, the Unreleased section, the entries newest first ending at VERSION), browser (heavy: the game boots from the title with no errors and `window.__olympus` answers; a save loaded and run on 400 ticks matches the original byte for byte); core (rng, calendar, loop, state, store, input, and the page in Chrome); render (palette, scale, moods, lamps, the batch); figures (art, doll, and the renderer in Chrome); kit (makers, materials, terrain); samples (the matrices, the casts, every shot crisp and on its palette, the gallery's stars surviving a reload).
